# Macaroonie — Claude Code Context

> ⚠️ DEVELOPER ONLY — contains infra details, env var names, auth config.

**Multitenant restaurant table booking + website CMS for the F&B / QSR franchise sector.**

Each master-franchisee registers as a tenant (Auth0 org). They configure venues, tables,
schedules, booking rules, deposits, and per-venue websites via an admin portal.
Guests book through an embeddable widget (iframe / Ember.js) and browse venue sites
at `{slug}.macaroonie.com`.

Owner: Obscure Kitty. Stack chosen for familiarity with existing plugin work (Node.js, PostgreSQL, React).

---

## ⚠️ Project status — pre-production

**Macaroonie is in active pre-prod development and testing.** No real customers, no real
booking traffic depend on it yet. Treat the codebase accordingly:

- **Backwards compatibility is NOT a concern.** Don't add fallback paths for legacy
  data shapes, deprecated field names, or schema-version-N rendering. Drop columns,
  rename without migrations, replace whole subsystems if cleaner. Simpler code beats
  defensive code at this stage.
- **Don't preserve "legacy fallback" rendering for old layouts** when shipping new
  ones — pick the new path and let it be the only path.
- **No need for feature flags or staged rollouts** for new features. Ship them on,
  iterate on the live deploy.
- **Migrations don't need data-preservation routines** beyond what naturally falls
  out of the SQL. Drop tables, drop columns, drop legacy enums when they stop being
  the source of truth.

This stops applying once we onboard the first real franchise. At that point flip
this note and revisit any places it's been quoted as justification.

## SOS Checklist

When a new session begins:

**1. Orient:**
- Read CLAUDE.md (loaded automatically).
- If the conversation has a compacted summary, verify claims against the actual codebase before referencing past items as "pending".

**2. Verify codebase state:**
- `git log --oneline -5` — what changed since last session?
- `git status` — pick up uncommitted WIP.
- `git branch -a` — confirm you're on the right branch.
- If the user mentions earlier work, grep to confirm it exists before assuming it's still pending.

**3. Establish session context:**
- Ask the user what they want to work on; don't assume.
- For a multi-step task, create a TodoWrite immediately.
- Note today's date for changelog entries.

**Critical rule:** Never reference backlog items, bugs, or features from a session summary as "pending" without verifying. Session summaries go stale. Always grep / check live code.

---

## EOS Protocol

When the user signals end of session ("wrap up", "eos", "that's all", or naturally winds down):

**1. Update all docs** affected by this session:
- CLAUDE.md — new completed items, new gotchas, key-file references.
- In-app Help.jsx — **always** add/update the operator-facing section for any new or changed feature. This is the most user-visible doc and must never lag behind shipped code.
- In-app Docs.jsx — **always** add/update the technical section for any new API routes, DB tables, services, or architectural changes.
- CHANGE.md — prepend a changelog entry for shipped features.

**2. Impact analysis:**
- Files modified / created this session.
- Existing features that may be affected by changes.
- Cross-cutting concerns flagged (e.g. RLS implications, template include paths, CSS variable additions).

**3. Risk + gap assessment:**
- Untested edge cases.
- Incomplete bits left behind (mark them clearly in Outstanding items).
- Recommended next actions for the user.

**4. Hand off for commit.** The **user commits and pushes via GitHub Desktop**. Claude must NOT print `git add` / `git commit` / `git push` command blocks for the user to run — they don't use the CLI for this. The GitHub Actions workflow on the server auto-deploys on push. After making code changes, just say "ready to commit" with a short list of files changed and a suggested commit-message subject line — let the user drive the rest in GitHub Desktop. Same goes for any time you'd otherwise tell them to "push from your laptop" — drop that phrase, the user knows their own workflow.

## Standard Design Rules

App-wide defaults — assume these on every new screen unless documented otherwise.

1. **Touch-first.** Admin portal targets tablet-sized touch screens at host stands. Minimum 48×48 px touch targets. `touch-manipulation` on every interactive element.
2. **No hover-only affordances.** Every interactive element must be discoverable and usable by tap alone.
3. **Drag-drop is the default for any orderable list.** If it has a `sort_order` column, the UI must support drag-reorder. Always-visible grip handles; `@dnd-kit` with both PointerSensor and TouchSensor.
4. **No native browser popups** (`window.confirm` / `prompt` / `alert`). Use inline confirmation patterns (double-confirm, typed-confirmation for destructive ops).
5. **Every save is explicit.** Each section has its own Save button — no auto-save, no global form state. Changes are only persisted when the user clicks Save.
6. **Destructive operations require double confirmation.** Delete, anonymise, and data-clearing actions show an inline "Are you sure?" before executing.
7. **No emojis in committed code or files** unless explicitly requested by the user.
8. **Mobile-responsive from 1015 px wide.** Sidebar + main content must remain usable at that width without horizontal scroll.
9. **All modals scrollable.** Use `max-h-[85vh] overflow-y-auto` on modal content areas so content does not clip on smaller tablet screens.
10. **Date as a styled button with invisible `<input type="date">` overlay.** Tapping opens the OS date picker on mobile. No custom calendar components.
11. **`type="tel"` / `inputMode="tel"` on phone inputs.** Triggers numeric keypad on tablets without custom code.
12. **Grey page, white cards, accent card headers.** Pages sit on `bg-page` (grey). Content goes in white bordered cards; a card's header row uses the `section-head` class (tenant website accent), never `border-b bg-muted/40`. Page title bars are `bg-background`. See `admin/src/index.css`.

## Repo structure

```
/
├── api/              Node.js API (Fastify)
├── admin/            React admin portal (Vite)
├── migrations/       PostgreSQL migration files (run in order 001–021)
├── hub/              POS venue hub (Node, no dependencies) on a mini PC: prints the network printers and USB printers plugged into it, its own page on :9180, updates itself (migrations 191, 195; installed by `hub/install/install.sh` / `install.ps1`); terminal sync and offline still parked
├── pos/              The till app, the kitchen screens and the self-order kiosks (React PWA, own package.json), built to pos/dist and served by the API on pos.<domain>
├── screen/           The menu screens' player (plain files, no build), served by the API on screens.<domain>
├── docs/             Deep dives (POS.md: the POS design)
├── shared/           Plain ESM used by both api/ and admin/ (screenLayout.js — menu screens' board renderer, also run by the player; orderInfo.js — what an order shows on order ready board tiles and kitchen ticket titles (number, tent, table, name), also run by the player; menuLayout.js — menu designer renderer; menuDiff.js — menu release content and change lists; fonts.js; orderPricing.js — web ordering prices; procurement.js — purchasing maths; loyverse.js — Loyverse figures for Cash Recon; gloriafood.js — GloriaFood order figures; deliveroo.js — Deliveroo menu file; tax.js — tax rates per order type, location and revenue stream; combos.js — combos (steps, prices, automatic combos); kiosk.js — self-order kiosks (designs, words, kiosk orders as till events); kioskText.js — the kiosk's own words in nine languages; assistant.js — ASSISTANT_NAME, the staff AI assistant's name (Pepper); qr.js — QR codes for device link codes; storeOpening.js — new locations: the areas, the plan, follow-ups; pos/ — the till's rules, pricing, printing (ticketLayout.js — print designs; tickets.js; ticketHtml.js), reports and sequencer, also used by pos/)
├── setup.sh          One-shot Lightsail server setup script
├── deploy.sh         Subsequent deployment script
└── CLAUDE.md         This file
```

## Tech stack

| Layer | Choice | Why |
|---|---|---|
| API | Fastify 4, Node.js 22, ESM | Performance, native schema validation |
| DB client | postgres.js (raw SQL) | Matches migrations exactly, no ORM magic |
| Auth | Auth0 (JWT + JWKS) | Offloads auth entirely, fits multitenancy via Orgs |
| Payments | Stripe Connect | Each restaurant is a Connect account, platform takes fee |
| Queue | BullMQ + Redis | Email jobs, hold sweep fallback |
| Validation | Zod | Both API and admin portal |
| Admin UI | React 18, Vite, TanStack Query, @dnd-kit | |
| Realtime | Native WebSocket (ws package) | Timeline live updates |
| Deployment | Lightsail Ubuntu 24.04, Nginx, PM2 | Single instance |

---

## Admin portal design principles

The admin portal is **optimised for tablet-sized touch screens** used by front-of-house staff at a host stand or service counter. Design decisions must reflect this:

- **Touch targets minimum 48 × 48 px** — buttons, covers selectors, slot tiles, grip handles
- **`touch-manipulation` CSS on every interactive element** — eliminates 300 ms tap delay on iOS/Android
- **No hover-only affordances** — every interactive element must be discoverable and usable by tap
- **`type="tel"` / `inputMode="tel"`** on phone number inputs — triggers numeric keypad on iOS/Android tablets without custom code
- **Custom numeric keypad** for covers/number inputs on touch devices — `inputMode="none"` suppresses native keyboard; a 3×4 grid overlay is rendered instead. Detection: `navigator.maxTouchPoints > 0` evaluated once at module load as `IS_TOUCH`
- **Date as a styled button with invisible `<input type="date">` overlay** — tapping the label opens the OS date picker on mobile, and a native datepicker on desktop; avoids custom calendar components
- **Minimum 1015 px wide layout assumed** — sidebar + timeline must remain usable at that width without horizontal scroll inside panels
- **All modals scrollable** — use `max-h-[85vh] overflow-y-auto` on modal content areas so content does not clip on smaller tablet screens in landscape

When adding new UI: always verify interactive elements are finger-sized, add `touch-manipulation`, and test at 1015 px width.

## Multitenancy — the most important thing to understand

Every tenant table in the DB has:
- A `tenant_id uuid` column
- Row-level security (RLS) enabled **and forced** (migration 176: the app's role owns the tables, and an owner isn't held to RLS unless it is forced)
- One policy: `USING (tenant_id = NULLIF(current_setting('app.tenant_id', true), '')::uuid OR current_setting('app.bypass_rls', true) = 'on')`

**Every DB query that touches tenant data MUST go through `withTenant()`:**

```js
// src/config/db.js
import { withTenant } from '../config/db.js'

// CORRECT — sets RLS context before every query
const rows = await withTenant(req.tenantId, tx =>
  tx`SELECT * FROM venues WHERE id = ${venueId}`
)

// WRONG — RLS will block all rows (returns empty, not an error)
const rows = await sql`SELECT * FROM venues WHERE id = ${venueId}`
```

`withTenant()` wraps a transaction with `set_config('app.tenant_id', id, true)` (ends with the transaction).
`systemSql` / `withSystem()` (a second, small pool whose connections start with `app.bypass_rls = 'on'`) are **system mode**, only for work that crosses tenants on purpose: finding the tenant from a token, slug, device or id; background jobs and sweeps; the platform pages. `withTx()` is gone. A query on the bare `sql` pool sees no tenant rows. Every query still filters `tenant_id` itself (RLS is the backstop; system mode has none). Superusers skip RLS even when forced: production's `macaroonie_user` isn't one, and tests must not run as one.

**Connection pooling**: postgres.js pools straight to Postgres. `withTenant()` is safe with any pooling; `systemSql` relies on a connection startup parameter, so behind a transaction-mode pooler `withSystem()` would have to `set_config('app.bypass_rls', 'on', true)` per transaction instead.

## Auth flow

**Login is identity-only — tenant selection happens on our side, not via a second Auth0 org login.**
This superseded the original org-scoped-login design; the description below is what's actually
live. See the "Tenant selection" Completed item and Docs.jsx's Authentication section for the
full client-side mechanics (`TenantGate`, `TenantSwitcherModal`, `X-Tenant-Id`).

1. User logs in via Auth0 with no `organization` param (`RequireAuth` in `main.jsx`) — except an
   invitation link, which forwards its `invitation`/`organization` query params as-is.
2. API middleware (`src/middleware/auth.js`) validates the JWT via JWKS and checks `platform_admins`.
3. The active tenant is resolved **per request** from the `X-Tenant-Id` header (sent by every
   admin-portal call via `useApi()`), falling back to the JWT's `org_id` claim only when no header
   is sent. A header naming a tenant the caller isn't a member of is ignored (logged), never 401'd.
4. `req.tenantId` and `req.user.role` are attached to every request.
5. Every route handler passes `req.tenantId` to `withTenant()`.

Roles: `owner` > `admin` > `operator` > `viewer`
Use `requireRole('admin', 'owner')` for destructive/config operations.

**Auth0 Dashboard setting this depends on:** Applications → Macaroonie Admin → Login Experience →
**Types of Users = "Business Users"**. This is what actually makes the app invitation-only — see
the Common-mistakes entry below before ever touching this setting.

## Key files

### API
- `src/config/db.js` — `withTenant()`, `tenantSql()` and system mode (`systemSql`, `withSystem()`, migration 176). Touch this carefully.
- `src/config/env.js` — Zod-validated env. App won't start with missing vars.
- `src/middleware/auth.js` — JWT validation + tenant resolution. Auth0 claim namespace is `https://${AUTH0_DOMAIN}/claims/`.
- `src/middleware/error.js` — Global error handler. PG error codes mapped to HTTP codes here.
- `src/routes/bookings.js` — Hold creation, free booking confirm, list/detail, status updates. `POST /bookings/admin-override` — admin direct booking that bypasses all slot/capacity/window rules; handles single table, multi-table (auto-creates combination), or unallocated row.
- `src/routes/customers.js` — Customer profiles. GET /customers?q= (search), GET /customers/:id (detail + bookings), PATCH /customers/:id, POST /customers/:id/anonymise (GDPR erasure), GET /customers/:id/export (GDPR JSON download). Exports upsertCustomer() helper called by bookings.js on every confirm.
- `src/routes/payments.js` — Payment intent creation + Stripe webhook. `handlePaymentSucceeded` copies `combination_id` and `guest_notes` from hold to booking INSERT.
- `src/routes/schedules.js` — Full schedule CRUD (templates, sittings, slot caps, overrides).
- `src/routes/website.js` — Website builder admin CRUD: config singleton, gallery (with reorder), custom pages, menu PDFs, opening hours, allergens, multipart upload, slug availability, DNS verify.
- `src/routes/menus.js` — Structured menu manager (migration 048; `calories`/section `image_url` added in 094/095; print layout `menus.print_settings` + section `print_break_before`/`print_keep_together` in 120, rendered by `views/menu_print.eta` as one `.page` per designed sheet with a screen-only fit check). One bulk-upsert `PATCH /:id` rewrites the whole `menus` → `menu_sections` → `menu_items` tree per save (re-inserting sections and items under the ids the client sends, migration 121, so they keep their ids) (see Docs.jsx's Menus section for the full list of touch points a new item/section field needs). Also: `POST /:id/duplicate` (copy a menu), `menuSaveBody(full)` (exported: a loaded menu back into the PATCH body with every field and id; used by `/duplicate` and the AI menu tools), `GET /public/:menuId` + `GET /:id/print` (unauthenticated), variant-groups + dietary-tag CRUD.
- Menu screens (migration 166; Docs.jsx → Menu Screens): `shared/screenLayout.js` (repo root) is the one board renderer, used by the player and the admin designer: `normalizeBoard()`, `boardMenuIds()`, `boardContext(layout, { menus, sold_out, tags, allergen_notice, now, tz })`, `renderBlockInner()` / `renderBoardHtml()`, `blockStyle()` / `boardStyle()`, `SCREEN_LAYOUT_CSS`, `boardOnAt()` / `itemsOnAt()` (playlist times, end not included), `dishPriceText()` (via `orderPricing.js`), `clockText()`, `SCREEN_FONT_WEIGHTS` (website fonts + board fonts). API: `routes/screens.js` (`/api/screens`, module `screens`: screens + claim by code, designs, playlists, `menu-data`), `routes/screenApi.js` (`/screen-api`: pairings, `config`, `heartbeat`; `Authorization: Screen id.secret`), `routes/screenSite.js` (serves `screen/` and `/lib/<shared file>` on host kind `screens`, writes `playerVersion()` into the page and `sw.js`), `services/screenSvc.js` (`screenConfig()`, `designerData()`, `slimMenu()`, `playerVersion()`), `services/screenAuth.js` (pairing codes, `verifyScreen()`, used by `config/ws.js`: a screen joins `screen:<id>`, `screens:<venue>`, `screens-t:<tenant>`, `menus:<tenant>`). Player: `screen/player.js` + `sw.js` + `index.html` + manifest. Admin: `pages/Screens.jsx` (`/screens`, tabs Screens / Boards / Playlists), `pages/ScreenDesigner.jsx` (`/screens/boards/:id`), `components/screens/shared.jsx` (`BoardPreview`, `useScreenData()`, `useScreenCss()`, `useBoardFonts()`, `ColourField`, `PxBox`).
- Menu screens part two (migration 167; Docs.jsx → Menu Screens): walls (`screen_walls`, `screens.wall_id/col/row`; `/api/screens/walls`, Walls tab `WallsTab` / `WallEditor` in `Screens.jsx`, `WallPreview` in `components/screens/previews.jsx`), playlist transitions (`screen_playlist_items.transition` / `transition_ms`, `PlaylistPreview`), video + scrolling text blocks and block animations (`wrapMotion()`, `startBoardMotion()`, `ANIM_IN` / `ANIM_LOOP` in `shared/screenLayout.js`; designer Play), synced playback (`GET /screen-api/time`, `playlistPosition()`, `replayStart()`, `wallRegion()`, `transitionFrames()`; player `syncClock()`, `prepareNext()`, `syncVideos()`), video cache in `screen/sw.js` (`screen-video-v1`, ranges sliced from the cached file). Media takes MP4 / WebM up to 150 MB (`routes/media.js`, `kind` filter; `scripts/ensure-nginx-media-upload.sh` run by every deploy).

Used by the board designer, the menu designer, the page builder canvas (`PICTURE_FIELD` in `PageBuilder.jsx`), both website `ImageField`s, the designers' picture panels and the Menus page dish / section pictures.

Used by `PUT /api/orders/availability/...` and the till's `PUT /pos-api/sold-out/:itemId` (role permission `sold_out` or `approved_by`); `tillConfig()` sends `sold_out`. Till UI: hold a dish in `MenuPanel.jsx` → `SoldOutModal` (`CheckActions.jsx`), `setItemSoldOut()` / `soldOutMap()` in `pos/src/lib/store.js`.
- `src/services/menuReleases.js` + `shared/menuDiff.js` (repo root) — menu publishing (migration 165). The menu tables are the draft; `menu_releases` rows are frozen copies (`releaseContent(loadMenuFull())`) with `goes_live_at`; live = newest non-cancelled release already due. `loadMenuVersion(tx, menuId, tenantId, { version: 'live' | 'draft', releaseId })` / `loadMenuVersions(tx, tenantId, { menuIds, venueId, publishedOnly, version })` are the only way guest/device readers load a menu (website block, public JSON, print, online ordering, till config, Deliveroo, cooking picker, allergen lookup, AI find_dishes). Also `publishMenu()`, `cancelRelease()`, `publishStatus()` (changes via `diffMenus()`), `menuStatuses()`, `announceDueReleases()` (minute hold sweep: tills + `menus.live` broadcast), `backfillFirstReleases()` (start-up, `menus.needs_first_release`). Admin: `components/menus/MenuPublish.jsx` (`PublishButton` beside Save in the menu editor, with a badge of changes not live, opening the Publishing window; `PublishBadges` on the list; `useOpenPreview()` for draft / release prints through `menu_preview_links`).

- `shared/promotions.js` (repo root) — online-ordering promotions (migration 131): `applyPromotions()` (the discount engine, called by `priceBasket()`), `isLiveAt()`, `covers()`, `promoSummary()`, `promoTerms()`, `promoLink()`, `promoRunning()`, `noticeText()`, `venueNow()`. Served to guests as `/order-api/promotions.js`. `api/src/services/promoSvc.js` loads rows and counts uses (`listPromotions()`, `venuePromotions()`, `findByCode()`, `lockUses()`); `routes/promotions.js` is the admin CRUD; `admin/src/pages/Promotions.jsx` the page; `blocks/promo_cta.eta` + `PromoCtaEditor.jsx` + `PromoCtaCanvas` the website block.

Used by `rotaCalc.js` (`bank_pay`/`cash_pay`), rota fill-wages (`cash_wage_entries.bank_amount`), `WeekWagesEditor` (Bank refill when a Total is typed), `wageEntryForStaff()` and `Staff.jsx`.
