## Outstanding items

### Build next (larger features)

**1. Booking widget — Stripe deposit flow**
The free-booking widget is shipped (`/widget/:venueId` + `/widget-api/*`).
Deposit-required venues currently throw 422 on `confirm`. Wire the existing
`/payments/intent` Stripe flow into the public widget: Payment Intent created
on hold, public Stripe.js form, webhook → confirm_hold. Same UX, just an
extra step before Confirmed.

**2. GloriaFood sales integration** *(shipped with migration 159: Cash Recon fill + orders board; follow-ups in item 20)*

**3. Header & Footer as page-builder blocks**
The site header and footer are currently template-baked (`templates/{key}/partials/header.eta` + `footer.eta`) and read auto-derived data: tenant_site logo + nav from venues/menus/pages, social_links, address, etc. Operators want them editable AS BLOCKS in the page builder so they can add/remove nav links, swap CTA copy, restructure footer columns, surface custom social handles. Required:
- Two new block types: `header` and `footer`. Each has rich data shape (header: `{ logo_url, brand_text, nav_items: [{label,url}], cta: {text, url} }`; footer: `{ columns: [{title, items: [{label,url}]}], copyright, show_social, show_powered_by }`).
- React canvas components for both, with editor controls (add/remove link, drag-reorder).
- Eta block partials per template (`templates/{key}/blocks/header.eta`, `footer.eta`) so each template renders its own signature chrome.
- Rendering shift: `index.eta` and `location.eta` STOP including `partials/header.eta` / `partials/footer.eta`. Instead, the home_blocks/page_blocks array renders, and we auto-prepend a `header` block + auto-append a `footer` block when missing (so the user always gets working chrome out of the box).
- Page builder UX: header block should be locked at index 0, footer at the last position (no drag). New "+" inserter sits between them.
- Picking a page template seeds blocks with header + footer at the expected positions.

**4. RBAC — what's left after the View / Manage rollout** *(rollout done Oct 2026, see Completed items)*
- The `requireRole()` calls left are deliberate extras on top of module permissions: Team and Access (owner), Cash Recon settings and unsubmit, booking delete, refunds, H&S General settings, Loyverse / GloriaFood setup (owner/admin), email suppression removal (owner).
- No per-page route guards in `main.jsx`: a page opened by URL with `none` renders and shows refusals / empty lists instead of redirecting.
- Widget test has no read-only mode (its booking flow goes through the public widget API anyway).
- The Media library still deletes categories and items behind `window.confirm` (Standard Design Rule 4).

**4. Customer hard delete**
Customers page currently supports anonymise (GDPR erasure by overwrite) but not hard delete.
A double-confirmation hard delete (for internal test/demo data cleanup) is still outstanding.
Must cascade-delete linked bookings or reassign them. Requires `requireRole('owner')` guard.

**5. Website builder — deployment pieces** *(feature shipped code-side, not yet deployed)*
- Nginx: wildcard server block for `*.macaroonie.com` (proxy_pass → Fastify API).
- DNS: wildcard A record `* → <Lightsail-IP>` on Lightsail DNS. Wildcard does NOT
  shadow more-specific records (regular `api`, `app`, etc. take precedence as expected).
- SSL: wildcard cert via Certbot DNS-01. **DNS lives on AWS Lightsail** (NOT Route53,
  NOT Cloudflare), so there's no off-the-shelf Certbot plugin. Use AWS CLI hook
  scripts with `certbot --manual --preferred-challenges dns-01` — see the runbook in
  the "Lightsail wildcard cert setup" gotcha below for the full path including IAM
  policy, hook scripts, and the renewal cron entry.
- Custom domains: switched on by the domain sync (`scripts/custom-domains-sync.sh`, installed
  by the deploy) within a minute of Verify DNS. Still to do: verification only checks that the
  domain points at us, not that the tenant owns it (a TXT token would), so a domain someone
  pointed here and nobody claimed could be claimed by any tenant and get a certificate; the
  sync only adds a twin it finds (www / bare), not other names; not yet run on the real server
  with a real certbot and systemd (tested with certbot stubbed).
- Migrations 025 + 026 + 027 + 035 + 036 + 037 + 038 + 039 + 040 + 041 + 042 are applied automatically by the migrate runner
  (or auto-baselined on first run via `AUTO_BASELINE_UP_TO=024`).
- Optional: set `STORAGE_DRIVER=s3` + `S3_*` env vars to use S3 / DO Spaces / R2
  instead of local disk for uploads.

**6. Email system loose ends**
- `nodemailer` is not in `api/package.json` — the SMTP email provider will crash on first use.
  Add `"nodemailer": "^6.9.0"` if any tenant selects SMTP.
- ~~Guest manage page `POST /modify` lacks slot validation~~ — stale: it checks
  `get_available_slots()` and table allocation (now in `services/guestBookingSvc.js`).
- Stripe webhook confirmation path (`payments.js handlePaymentSucceeded`) does not
  schedule reminder emails; only the free-booking path does. Wire `scheduleReminder()`
  into the webhook path too.
- After deploy, clear stale BullMQ jobs in Redis — the `notificationQueue` job name
  changed to `'booking_email'` (with required `venueId` field), so old-format jobs
  enqueued before the change will fail forever.
- Legacy `src/services/notificationSvc.js` is dead code — safe to delete.

**7. Docs.jsx update for platform/team**
Help.jsx has a Team Management section; Docs.jsx does not yet have a corresponding
technical section for the platform-admin routes, RBAC matrix, or `platform_admins` table.

**8. Test suite**
CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) builds an empty database from every migration, starts the API, runs the shared unit tests and the API tests (`api/test`: the cross-tenant sweep and Pepper's chat loop) on every pull request; it isn't a required check yet (add a branch rule in GitHub). Still to do:
- The checks run while building earlier features (Pepper tools, Purchasing, Loyverse, GloriaFood, POS, kitchen, screens, kiosks) live outside the repo; port the ones worth keeping into `api/test` as features are touched.
- Priority targets: `confirm_hold()` race condition, slot resolver output, booking flow end-to-end, admin-override booking creation, schedule exception resolution.
- The sweep only tries ids the fixtures make: 120 of 182 tenant tables have rows (till checks and events, kitchen orders, online order lines, paired screens, media and the integrations are empty because they come from devices, guests or other services). Its own body for each write route is `{}`, so writes are only tested properly through the replay of `fillTenant()`'s writes.

**9. More `/mobile` modules**
The `/mobile` shell + `MOBILE_MODULES` registry (see Key files) now has five modules:
H&S Dashboard, Expenses, Purchasing, Cash Up, and Wages (plus Allergens, Orders, Assistant and Dashboards since). Remaining candidates, in the
order they'd likely get used on a phone away from the host-stand tablet: Food safety
quick-log (temp/hold/cooking entry only, no equipment management), a booking lookup/manage
view, the H&S Action Log standalone.
When adding one: reuse the desktop page's data hooks/mutations/body components (not its
layout), register it in `src/mobile/registry.js`, and check whether its body components
already reflow at phone width before assuming they do (see the Common-mistakes entry on
canvas/SSR parity for the general shape of this risk — a shared body component drifting
between two renderers).
**10. `/mobile` device testing**
Built and verified via production build (`npm run build`) only — this environment has no
way to run a real browser against a live API/DB to click through the installed-PWA flow
end-to-end (Add to Home Screen on an actual iPhone/Android device, confirm the swapped
icon/name/manifest actually take effect, confirm `100dvh`/safe-area handling on a notched
device). Worth a real-device pass before treating this as fully verified.

**11. `/mobile` PWA install identity on Android — confirmed broken on real device, needs a subdomain** *(code done with migration 138: ops.macaroonie.com. Takes effect once `scripts/nginx-office-ops.sh` has been run and Auth0 allows ops.; still to confirm on a real Android device.)*
Real-device testing (item 10) surfaced that Android Chrome does not treat `/mobile` as a
separately-installable PWA the way iOS Safari does. Chrome ties "already installed?" to
the *origin*, not to which manifest is currently linked — since a WebAPK is already
installed for `macaroonie.com` (the main app, `scope: "/"`), Chrome's install flow offers
to open that existing app instead of creating a distinct "Macaroonie Ops" icon, regardless
of `MobileShell`'s manifest/link-tag swap. The only reliable fix is a genuinely different
origin for `/mobile` (e.g. `ops.macaroonie.com`), since PWA install identity on Android is
origin-scoped. **Pick this up together with the office/ops subdomain work discussed earlier**
— fold the `/mobile` PWA identity fix into that same subdomain rollout rather than doing a
one-off DNS/Nginx change just for this. Until then, "Create shortcut" (Chrome's fallback
option) is the only way to get a distinct icon/name on Android, and it's a browser
shortcut, not a standalone WebAPK.

**12. Desktop Wages — submitted-report lock** *(resolved by migration 137: wages are no longer submitted, and every wages surface uses `WeekWagesEditor`)*

**14. Web ordering — follow-ups**
- **Stage 1: Stripe as a payment gateway** (Connect onboarding, Connect webhook secret + event dedupe, platform fee, Apple/Google Pay domain registration). Plug in as a `paymentGateways/stripe.js` + a `paymentPanel()` branch; see Docs.jsx's Web Ordering section. The existing deposit webhook (`payments.js`) likely never receives connected-account events (single platform secret) — fix in the same stage.
- Cash Recon feed: online sales are only in the Online orders report; nothing is written into the day's takings yet.
- Order emails use built-in templates (not editable on the Emails page yet).
- Delivery (zones, fees), table QR ordering: not started.
- Promotions next steps (with customer logins / loyalty): per-customer limits, single-use unique codes, first-order-only, fulfilment/channel scope. A meal deal part picks the dearest matching dish greedily in part order, so overlapping parts (the same section in two parts) may not find the best split. The Promo block's canvas preview shows the heading placeholder in the builder's faded style when the heading is blank. `orders.channel` / `fulfilment` already allow them.
- Printing is browser print (80mm ticket); direct Epson kitchen printing comes with the POS printer layer.

**15. Pepper (AI assistant) — follow-ups** *(the review of 17 Oct 2026 lists more ideas: memory, streaming, voice, help-button prompts)*
- Tool search and the new tools are tested only against a stand-in Claude API (exact block shapes from the SDK 0.133.0 types). First real use: ask something in each area in a fresh chat and check the search finds the tool; then check an old conversation still replays after a deploy that changes tools (the tool list is part of what replayed thinking is checked against).
- Rota tools change one week per call and don't edit shifts, roles, rota setup or tip pot setup; Cash tools don't edit Cash Recon settings, unsubmit a day, upload receipts or edit / delete an expense; order tools don't change ordering setup or promotions' content; venue tools don't change sittings, slot caps, tables or combinations; website tools don't edit pages, blocks or the theme. Those stay on their pages (`open_page` links to them).
- The MCP tool list is filtered by area (migration 179: `SECTION_MODULES` + `toolAllowed()`), not per tool: a person with View on one of an area's modules sees all its reading tools, some of which may still refuse. Access tokens can't be limited to one venue: many tools act on a booking, order or person by id without naming a venue, so a venue scope couldn't be enforced honestly without route-level work.
- OAuth sign-in for the staff connector (migration 181) is tested against the spec's flow (registration, PKCE, the Connect page, the token on the connector) but not yet with claude.ai or ChatGPT themselves. First real use: add the address as a custom connector in claude.ai and check it lands on the Connect page and connects. There are no refresh tokens: when the chosen 30-365 days are up the app asks to connect again. Registered apps (`ai_oauth_clients`) are never deleted. The login-return fix (`appState.returnTo`) was only tried with the harness's stand-in login, not real Auth0.
- Browser-based MCP clients from other origins: the global `@fastify/cors` handles preflight before the MCP routes' own `OPTIONS` handler, and in production only allows `*.{root}` origins. Server-side clients (claude.ai, ChatGPT) are unaffected.
- AI bookings follow the widget's path, so like widget bookings they get no reminder email (`scheduleReminder()` only runs on `POST /api/bookings`; staff AI bookings use that route and do get one).
- Deposit venues can't be booked through AI (same as the public widget): the tools send the guest to the website or phone.
- H&S tools don't edit setup (fridges, check times, sessions, checklist templates), delete readings, add per-task checklist notes, or touch purchasing; those stay on their pages. A temperature reading is replaced only by logging the same check again.
- `override_limits` doesn't cover the booking window, the cutoff, blocked times or a specific table; those stay on the timeline.
- Menu tools don't change dish photos, meal deal contents, print designs or print options, till fields, the tag list or variant groups themselves, and don't delete menus; those stay on the Menus pages. A menu read from a photo or PDF is only as good as the reading (the card lists every dish to check). Not yet tried against the real Claude API with a real menu photo.
- Purchasing tools don't delete anything (items, prices, suppliers and lists are switched off), don't reorder lists or categories, and don't download PO files. An attached Excel file can't be imported (attachments refuse xlsx): save it as CSV, or the model reads it from a PDF. A price list read from a PDF or photo is only as good as the reading, so the import card's dry run is the check; not yet tried against the real Claude API with a real supplier PDF.
- Attachments: staff chats only (not the website chat; MCP clients send files through their own app). Word / Excel / PowerPoint aren't read (save as PDF / CSV). Files are kept in Postgres (`ai_attachments.data`, bytea) for as long as their chat exists (chats idle past the tenant's retention are deleted hourly, migration 174); unsent uploads go after a day. Move to `storageSvc` if chats with files become common. Not yet tried on a real iPhone (HEIC conversion, camera input) or against the real Claude API with a large scanned PDF.
- Fair use and erasure (migration 174): unit weights are hard-coded in `usageUnits()` (`CACHE_READ_WEIGHT`), so an Anthropic price change needs a code change; the website chat has no per-guest cap (only the per-IP rate limit and the monthly allowance). Erasure finds a guest's details only as written on their customer row, bookings and orders (a first name alone, a phone written another way, a typo aren't found), can't reach an MCP client's own history (the person's AI app keeps its copy) and closes every chat it edits. A process that dies mid-turn leaves its chat busy for up to 90 s.
- Help answers search the Help page's words only (no synonyms beyond simple plurals): a question in other words than the guide's may miss; the index ignores the Docs page on purpose. The page note covers six pages (Timeline, Bookings, Rota, Food safety, Online orders, Cash Recon); other pages send only their address. Neither is tried against the live model yet.
- The 5-minute nginx timeout exists only once a deploy has run `ensure-nginx-ai-timeout.sh` (check `sudo nginx -T | grep -n "api/ai"`); a streamed turn doesn't need it (the 15 s heartbeat keeps any proxy's read timeout from firing), a JSON one still does.
- Streaming and routing are tested against a stand-in Claude that streams the API's event shapes, and in Chromium through the Vite proxy, not yet against the live API or through the real nginx. First real use: watch an answer stream on the live site (nginx buffering off), and check a chat that moved to the main model mid-way still answers with its earlier reasoning (the main model is documented to read the everyday model's thinking blocks; not measured here). The `HEAVY_TOOLS` list and `LONG_CHAT_TOKENS` are first guesses; watch the `AI chat moved to the main model` log lines to tune them. Prices in `PRICES` are copied by hand: a price change needs a code change.

**16. Purchasing — follow-ups** *(Purchasing shipped with migration 153)*
- Approval is by role only (migration 180): no "orders over a set value need approval" rule, and approvers aren't told when a request comes in (no email or push; they see the count on the Order tab and in Pepper's day briefing).
- Splitting moves whole quantities between two orders of the same supplier; it can't send part of an order to another supplier, and a split PO that the supplier already had relies on them reading the Amended copy (nothing is phoned or confirmed).
- Stock counts: pars are a usual quantity; there is no stock on hand, so "order to par" can't subtract what's on the shelf yet.
- Invoices and credit notes: no invoice matching against received quantities, no credit tracking for shortages beyond the line note.
- Costs: nothing feeds Cash Recon or a COGS / recipe costing view yet; `purchase_order_lines` hold the prices paid.
- `emailPo()` runs inside the request after the order is saved (one email per supplier); a slow provider makes Place order slow. Move to the notification queue if that shows up.
- PO files always include prices (they go to the supplier), so staff without price access can still see prices in a downloaded PDF/Excel.
- Not tried on a real tablet/phone (keypad temps, print from iPad).

**17. Loyverse — follow-ups** *(shipped with migration 154)*
- Built from the Loyverse API v1.0 shape and tested only against a stand-in server (the docs and API hosts weren't reachable from the build environment). First real use: compare a fill with the Loyverse Back Office sales summary for the same day and store, especially refunds, taxes added on top of prices (not included), and `surcharge` (assumed field name).
- Filling is on demand only: no nightly automatic fill, no webhook.
- Expenses, wages and the day note aren't filled; Loyverse pay-ins/pay-outs (shifts) aren't read.
- Dining option (eat in / takeaway / delivery) isn't offered as a figure; map delivery platforms by payment type.
- One store per venue; a venue with two Loyverse stores can't sum both.
- The AI assistant has no Loyverse tool.

**18. POS** *(design in `docs/POS.md`; phases 1-5 built cloud-first, see section 8 there)*
- Parked by the user: Trial 1 (printing) on the venue's Epson (`hub/README.md`), Trial 2 (terminal ↔ hub over the LAN with the internet down: venue CA, `wss`, Chrome's Local Network Access prompt), the hub's offline role (SQLite store for `createSequencer()`, serving `/pos-api` on the LAN). The hub itself prints, installs and updates (migrations 191, 195).
- The mini PC hub (migration 195) is tested in a container only: the Linux installer with systemd stubbed (no real systemd, journald or ufw), a stand-in sysfs for USB (no real usblp device: whether an Epson answers DLE EOT through `/dev/usb/lp0` is untried), the Windows installer only parsed and partly run in PowerShell 7 on Linux (never on Windows 10/11 with Windows PowerShell 5.1, the scheduled task, the firewall rule or a real RAW spooler job). The RAW job compiles its C# helper on every ticket (about a second on Windows). No serial printers on Linux and no cash drawer kick on its own. The status page is plain http on the venue network (read only; shows the link code while not linked).
- Until the hub syncs tills: tills can't see each other with the internet down (each trades alone, clashes are refused and listed). The hub prints (migration 191) but only while it reaches the cloud: kitchen tickets are made by the cloud's sequencer, so with the internet down nothing reaches the kitchen printers.
- Kitchen printers (migration 191): not tried on a real Epson or a real USB printer through Chrome's --kiosk-printing; a device printer prints through the browser, which can't open a cash drawer or cut by command (the printer's driver settings decide); the hub has no installer or Windows service of its own (run it with NSSM / Task Scheduler); a kitchen printer's tickets show no prices and can't be reprinted from the kitchen (only from the Printers tab's Tickets); the kitchen test bench doesn't show printed tickets.
- Print designs (migration 190) are text only: no logo, picture or QR code on paper (the hub's ESC/POS encoder has no raster or QR yet). One design per use per venue, not per printer: a bar printer prints the kitchen ticket design. The till's browser print draws its own HTML from the same operations (close to, not exactly, the printer's columns). Not tried on a real Epson printer.
- Not built: card terminal adapters (Dojo, SumUp, Zettle, Stripe, Adyen), KDS, the till layout designer (Phase 7), online orders into the till and kitchen, bookings on the floor plan, drawer floats / shifts, a staff AI tool for the till.
- Not tried on a real Android or Windows tablet yet (install, print dialog, keypad), or with more than a few tills at once.

**19. Deliveroo menu file — follow-ups** *(shipped with migration 157)*
- Deliveroo's allergen and dietary codes come from their help pages and Menu API guide (the docs host couldn't be reached from the build environment); not yet checked by uploading a file to a live Deliveroo Hub. First real use: upload a small change, check the Hub shows the allergens, VAT and prices as expected.
- Only the item-level file. Modifier groups and options (Deliveroo's other bulk files), categories, item photos and availability aren't filled.
- One price per item: a dish sold with options is sent at its cheapest option's price; Deliveroo modifiers for the dearer options are still set in the Hub.
- The file lives in the browser tab only; nothing records which file was last downloaded or uploaded.
- No connection to Deliveroo's API (menu push, orders into the till or Cash Recon).

**20. GloriaFood — follow-ups** *(shipped with migration 159)*
- Built from GloriaFood's Accepted Orders API v2 README and its two sample orders (the docs repo was readable; their website wasn't) and tested only against a stand-in server. First real use: check that the "Push Accepted Order" integration accepts our address (or use the poll key), then compare a day's orders, payments and fulfil times with GloriaFood's own order list.
- No history from the API: earlier days come from importing GloriaFood's order export (migration 162). The import assumes the export's Order ID is the id the API sends (not checked against a live restaurant); if not, the `after_live` cutoff still stops overlaps, except for orders a poll venue's queue holds from before the import. The export has no dishes or discounts (Items before discounts / Discounts are £0 on imported days), and a payment option named in GloriaFood (e.g. "Card (G-Pay / via Browser)") is stored as the export spells it, which may differ from the code the API sends for the same option.
- An order paid two ways counts under its main `payment`; GloriaFood doesn't send the split amounts.
- Polling needs Redis (the BullMQ repeat job); with Redis down only Check for orders now, and the preview's own poll, collect orders. A pop whose orders fail to store is lost except for the API log entry.
- Board moves (and the kitchen screens' ready / collected, migration 178) are ours only; nothing is sent back to GloriaFood (no ready time, no status). GloriaFood orders aren't in the Online orders Report tab (they are in its Transactions tab), aren't linked to Customers, and the AI assistant has no GloriaFood tool.
- No GloriaFood menu sync (the Fetch Menu API exists).

**21. Menu publishing — follow-ups** *(shipped with migration 165)*
- Option groups (Variant groups library), tags, the allergen notice, print designs and the online-ordering sold-out list are tenant-wide and change straight away, not on publish. A dish's own option prices are in the release, but the till's nested option groups (`groups` in `tillConfig()`) come from the live library.
- Promotions point at dish / section ids; a promotion on a dish that is only in the draft does nothing until the menu is published (and one on a dish removed from the draft keeps working while the dish is live).
- Releases are never deleted (one row per publish with the whole menu as jsonb); add a clean-up of old past releases if menus are published very often.
- A scheduled publish freezes the draft as it was when scheduled; later saves aren't included unless published again (the card says so). There is no "publish the draft as it is at that time" option.
- Menu screens listen to `menus.live` on the `menus:<tenantId>` room (migration 166).

**22. Menu screens — follow-ups** *(shipped with migration 166)*
- Walls and synced playback tested with two Chromium windows on one machine only. On real screens: check the bezel gap looks right, and that the boards change together (the clock is set to within a round trip; a screen with a slow connection can be a few tens of ms out).
- Videos: MP4 (H.264) is the safe choice; WebM / VP9 depends on the device. A video block always starts muted; sound only plays where the device allows it (Fully Kiosk: allow autoplay with sound). A video can't be longer than the board's time in a playlist without being cut (use Video length). No transcoding or resizing: a 4K video on a weak TV box may stutter.
- The video cache keeps every video the screen's boards use; there is no size limit, so many large videos can fill a small device. Videos are downloaded one at a time after a change.
- Dropping files: up to 10 at a time; the website canvas only replaces pictures on Image, Hero and Story blocks (other blocks get an Image block after them); dropping onto the Gallery block doesn't add to it. Not tried with a real OS drag on an iPad (the tests dispatch DragEvents with real files).
- Not tried on a real Android TV box, a TV's own Chrome, or Fully Kiosk yet (install, full screen, wake lock, a portrait screen turned by `rotation`, memory over days). First real use: leave a screen running for a day and check the admin's last seen / showing.
- Google Fonts are fetched by each screen (cached by the service worker after the first load); a screen that first starts offline shows fallback fonts until it has been online once.
- A playlist with nothing on now shows black; there is no "fallback board" setting.
- Times can't run overnight (22:00 to 02:00): split them into two times (22:00-23:59 and 00:00-02:00).
- Board images and dish photos come from wherever they are stored (relative `/uploads/...` on the screens host, or S3 URLs); a very large photo is shown as is (no resizing for screens).
- The designer has no undo; Discard is reloading the page without saving.
- Sold out from the till needs the till to be online (it isn't an event in the till's outbox).

**23. Kitchen screens — follow-ups** *(shipped with migrations 168 + 169; `docs/POS.md` 9.3)*
- A dish moves through stations one after another; one dish can't be split into two jobs on two stations at once (meal deal parts can).
- The flow designer's canvas has no zoom; a big kitchen scrolls. Not tried with a real finger drag on an iPad.
- Till to kitchen with the internet down needs the venue hub (parked with the POS hub).
- GloriaFood orders on the kitchen screens (migration 178): a change to an order's dishes after it reached the kitchen isn't copied (only a cancellation); a dish whose GloriaFood name differs from the menu's isn't matched (it follows the order type and default links); orders that arrived while sending was off don't come on later; tested only against GloriaFood's documented payload shape, not a live restaurant.
- The till doesn't show what the kitchen has done (ready / bumped).
- No per-dish cook times or staggered starts across stations; all dishes of an order start together.
- Not tried on a real Android tablet, TV box or bump bar; the beep needs a first tap on the screen.
- Ticket titles (migration 189) apply to kitchen screens; printed tickets get theirs from their print design (migration 190). Tents reach the kitchen only from counter tills and kiosks; online and GloriaFood orders have none (a board tile shows their number or name instead). A name on an order ready board shows on a public screen: nothing hides surnames typed on the till.
- Kitchen flows (migration 177) switch by hand only (a kitchen screen or Till setup): no schedule by time of day or day of the week, no Pepper tool, no flow column in the Kitchen report. A switch needs the cloud (a screen offline says so).
- Shared flows (migration 192) match stations, revenue streams and tills by name only (a station called "Grill 1" doesn't get the Grill box); no per-location exception inside a shared flow (copy it as the location's own), no "put this in use at every location" button, and the Pepper tools don't edit flows. Sent from can't single out online orders (no device). The designer's Add them at makes stations with default settings (screens, timings and pieces are set per location afterwards). Not tried with many locations (the setup page loads every location's stations).
- Sleepers (migration 200): one wait per box (a 10 and a 20 minute wait are two Sleepers); the wait counts from when a dish reaches it, never from the order's promised time; a Splitter after a Sleeper decides when the order is sent, with the kitchen as it is then, not when the dishes wake; order status only changes on events, so a dish whose way ends at a Sleeper doesn't hold the order back; held dishes aren't counted in a station's On order counts until they wake (they are upcoming). Not tried on real kitchen screens.
- Splitters (Balance boxes; migration 183): a shared big ticket (`split_above`) is shared by whole dish lines (a line of 3 x 10 Wings is never split into its quantity) and greedily, biggest first, so the result can be a few pieces off the best split; a held course behind a box shows greyed on every station it might go to until fired; an online or GloriaFood order for later is decided when it reaches the kitchen, not at its time; the load counts fired dishes only (not held ones) and, unless the box is Smart, treats both cooks as equally fast; a decision is never moved if a cook falls behind afterwards (Smart only steers the next tickets). Smart's pace is a throughput estimate from bumps: a cook who bumps late (or early) skews it, a station that does several tickets at once looks faster per ticket, and a station with fewer than two bumps in 30 minutes counts as the others' average. The busy order type rule counts orders, not pieces, and only orders with something still to cook at the box's stations; it has no time-of-day schedule. Not tried on real screens yet.
- The kitchen test bench runs in real time (no speeding the clock up), has no till screens or GloriaFood orders as a source, and can't play back a real service. Its screens are scaled frames: tried in Chromium on a desktop only, not on a tablet.

**24. Tax rates, order types, revenue streams — follow-ups** *(shipped with migration 170; `docs/POS.md` 8.1)*
- A rate change on a date isn't built: change the % on the day, or publish a menu that names another rate. All prices include VAT; there is no tax-exclusive pricing per location.
- Online ordering only uses the collection type (delivery and table orders aren't built).
- Cash Recon fill maps income by revenue stream (as before); there is no mapping by order type yet.
- The AI menu tools set a section's rate only (no exceptions, no dish or menu rates).
- GloriaFood and Loyverse figures don't know our order types.
- Not tried on a real tablet: the name and tent fields use the OS keyboard (no custom keypad for the tent number).

**25. Combos — follow-ups** *(shipped with migration 171; `docs/POS.md` 8.2)*
- VAT on a combo is the combo's own Tax setting; a combo mixing standard and zero-rated items (hot food with a cold takeaway drink) isn't split per part.
- Automatic combos are found greedily (biggest saving first); with overlapping combos a different grouping can occasionally be cheaper.
- No combo inside a combo, and no step price that depends on another step's pick.
- The AI menu tools describe combos and warn when a change removes a choice, but don't edit steps (or a choice's options in the deal; `get_menu` only says they are changed).
- A deal's options change the picked dish's own option groups only: an option's nested group (Steak → Cooking) follows the library inside the deal too, and the deal can't add a group the dish doesn't have except by Another group. A swapped group's own min / max apply unless the deal sets How many.
- The Deliveroo file sends a combo as one item at its own price; its choices are Deliveroo modifiers set in the Hub. GloriaFood orders aren't matched to combos.
- Not tried on a real tablet (till steps, the guest wizard on a phone) or with a real bump bar on a combo ticket.

**26. Self-order kiosks — follow-ups** *(shipped with migration 172; `docs/POS.md` section 10)*
- Card payments: test reader only. A real terminal (Dojo, SumUp, Zettle, Stripe Terminal, Adyen) plugs in at the card step and must hand back a reference for `payment.added`.
- Receipts print through the kiosk's browser; the receipt printer picked in Till setup is used once the hub prints.
- With the internet down a kiosk pauses new orders (the hub would keep it trading); only a card order already paid is kept and sent later.
- No promo codes, vouchers, loyalty or logins on the kiosk (automatic offers only); no age check; no upsize prompts beyond automatic combos and "Anything else?".
- Translations are tenant-wide (one Spanish name for a dish at every venue); AI suggestions need the assistant key and count towards the monthly AI allowance.
- The design editor has no undo beyond Discard; designs aren't versioned.
- Not tried on a real kiosk screen, with a real card terminal, with a screen reader, or in Chrome kiosk mode / Fully Kiosk.
- Offline (19 Oct 2026): start screen videos aren't kept on the kiosk (only pictures); pictures on another origin (S3 storage) aren't kept either (the service worker keeps same-origin pictures only). The heartbeat and the quick tries are the kiosk's only: tills and kitchen screens still notice a lost connection when a request fails. During a deploy's API restart a kiosk may pause ordering for a few seconds.
- Words-only choices (19 Oct 2026) show the description on cards only (no room in a circle, an oval or a hexagon); a long choice name is cut after three lines. Centred applies to grids and honeycombs only: the list fills the width and the slider and wheel are centred anyway.
- Screens of another shape or size (26 Oct 2026): the zoom only works where the browser honours the viewport tag (phones' and tablets' browsers); a computer's browser (Chrome in kiosk mode on Windows) keeps its own size and only the shape is kept, so set the screen's display scaling there. Tested in Chromium's mobile emulation, not on a real Android tablet, an iPad, Fully Kiosk or a TV box (zooming in above 1 especially). Designs saved before have the Full HD screen, so kiosks on 10" tablets (800 x 1280) now show bars at the sides and zoom out to 67%; pick the tablet in the design's Screen card if that's the screen they run on. Visits recorded before this have no box: their playback works the box out from the design as it is now.
- The order done screen and receipts (migration 209): the order done designer is per order type, not per revenue stream (a type that asks for a name in one stream and not another is judged by the preview's stream). Emails for card payments go out while the kiosk waits (a slow provider keeps the guest on the sending sheet up to 25 s); a counter order's email waits for the till (migration 210) and nothing tells the guest if it later fails or is given up (the kiosk has long moved on: staff see `pos_receipts.email_error` in the database only, no admin screen). The receipt page has no logo or VAT invoice layout beyond what the kiosk receipt design prints, and isn't in the guest's language (the print design's own words). Nothing links a receipt to a customer or the newsletter. Not tried with a real phone camera scanning the kiosk screen, or a real email provider.
- Layout options (migration 185): hexagons use `clip-path`, so focus outlines and borders at a hexagon's slanted edges don't show; a hexagon tile shows no description or calories (no room); a two-wide section tile is ignored with round photos and hexagons. The split photo needs every option's picture to be the same dish taken the same way for the parts to line up (nothing checks that); it shows at most six parts, ignores nested option groups (Steak -> Cooking) and takes groups in the dish's group order, then pick order within a group. A combo step's switch needs the menu published; a group's works straight away. Not tried on a real kiosk screen (clip-path and drop-shadow cost on a weak Android box with many hexagons).

**27. Installer app — follow-ups** *(shipped with migration 184)*
- Auth0 must allow `https://installer.<domain>` (Allowed Callback URLs, Logout URLs, Web Origins) before anyone can sign in there; nothing else needs server setup (the wildcard nginx block and certificate cover it).
- The in-app scanner needs `BarcodeDetector` (Chrome on Android); elsewhere (iPhone Safari) the phone's own camera app opens the QR code's link, which lands on the same page. Not tried on a real phone, tablet, TV box or with a real camera yet.
- No in-app way to change a till's revenue stream, receipt printer or floor area, a kitchen screen's view / columns / sound, a kiosk's card reader, or a menu screen's size, fit, venue or wall place: those stay in Till setup and on the Screens page (the installer keeps them when it saves).
- A code's status is polled (every 4 s), not pushed; a device that sat on its code for a long time shows a new one every 15 minutes.
- Venue hubs aren't listed or linked in the installer.

**28. New location with Pepper — follow-ups** *(shipped with migration 186)*
- Tested only against a stand-in Claude that returns the answer schema; not yet against the live model. First real use: describe a real opening and check the scope picks a sensible location to copy, asks few questions, and the brief matches the cards.
- Not copied: menus that belong to the template location only (the new one uses the company's menus; a venue-only menu has to be copied on the Menus page), promotions limited to venues (the new location isn't added to them), kiosk designs and menu screen boards (company-wide already), Loyverse and GloriaFood links (follow-ups), staff from the template (only people named in the plan are added), team members' access, past data of any kind.
- A copy is a snapshot: later changes to the template location don't reach the new one.
- Create is one transaction; a very large template (thousands of tables, caps or pars) has not been timed.
- The opening isn't linked to Pepper's chat history: a plan made in the chat and one made on the page are separate openings unless the chat names the opening.

**29. Kiosk shared previews — follow-ups** *(shipped with migration 187)*
- One link per design; there is no per-person link or a password, and the view count can't tell who opened it.
- The link shows the design as last saved: unsaved changes in the editor aren't shared until Save.
- Not tried on a real phone browser at length (the page scales the kiosk down; a phone shows it small).
- Previews with unpublished menu changes (migration 188): the draft is read when the preview loads; an open preview doesn't follow menu saves by itself (Reload in the editor, reopening a shared link). A scheduled release isn't offered as its own version. `menuChanges()` loads every menu the preview could show twice (published and draft) each time a preview link is made.

**30. Card machines — follow-ups** *(shipped with migration 193)*
- Dojo is built from its documented request shapes and never run against Dojo's sandbox (the docs and API hosts weren't reachable from the build environment); Stripe and SumUp come from their SDKs' types. None has been tried with a real machine or a live account. First real use: a Test mode Stripe reader (simulated WisePOS E), a SumUp Solo in Cloud API mode and a Dojo sandbox terminal, one sale, one decline, one refund each, before taking real money.
- No webhooks: the server reads the provider while a device watches and once a minute after; a payment the provider finishes after 15 minutes is marked failed here and must be checked in the provider's own dashboard.
- One machine per till or kiosk (no "pick a machine per payment"), no pay-at-table, no tips on the machine (tips come from the till), no split of one card payment across checks, no pre-authorisation.
- Stripe uses the tenant's own key (not Stripe Connect, so no platform fee); one account per provider key, readers matched by the provider's id.
- Refunds go to the card used, through the provider's API; a provider that refuses a refund (too old, already refunded in its dashboard) leaves the payment on the bill.
- Adyen and Zettle aren't built.

**31. Venue wizard, Brand & theme page, menus from files — follow-ups** *(shipped with migration 196)*
- Menu reading is tested only against a stand-in Claude; not yet with the live model and a real menu (photos of a printed menu, a long PDF, a handwritten board). First real use: read a two-page menu and compare every price. A menu over about 32,000 output tokens is cut off (the reply asks for a few pages at a time); there is no way to read a long menu in parts and join them.
- Migration 196 replaced every group's menu: Navigation designer changes and launcher tiles made before it are gone (pre-prod, accepted by the user).
- The brand reaches the live website only after Publish; the admin's card headers and the designers' Use the brand use the saved draft at once, so for a while the admin can show a brand the guests don't see yet.
- Use the brand on other designs saves designs one after another; a failure leaves the earlier ones changed (each row says which failed). Kiosk designs only change their brand colours and fonts, not their layout pictures.
- The wizard's own-look kit applies to the venue's web page only (not its kiosk designs or boards, which are company-wide).
- The wizard has no undo for settings saved in earlier steps other than changing them again; deleting a draft is the reset.

**32. Invoices on receiving, receipts, product names — follow-ups** *(shipped with migration 197)*
- Tested only against a stand-in Claude; not yet with the live model and real invoices (a crumpled delivery note, a two-page PDF invoice, a handwritten one) or till receipts. First real use: book in a delivery from its invoice and compare every quantity and price; read a few receipts and check the VAT.
- Quantities are matched in the order's packs: when a supplier invoices in another unit (per kg against a case) the conversion is Pepper's (`qty_in_packs` / `price_per_pack`), not checked here beyond being a number. Pack sizes printed in words ("tray of 30") depend on Pepper reading them.
- One invoice per booking-in: a second invoice for the same delivery replaces the matches (both readings stay stored). Invoice lines matched to the same order line are summed; their prices aren't averaged (the first line's price is used).
- Charges (delivery, deposits, discounts) are shown, not recorded anywhere else; the order's delivery charge isn't compared with the invoice's. The invoice total isn't checked against the lines.
- Nothing yet uses invoice prices beyond the price list prompt (no credit notes, no invoice matching report, no cost reporting from received lines).
- Invoice files are stored like receipts (`/uploads/...`, public URLs on the local driver) and kept with the order; readings never applied are kept too (no clean-up).
- Receipt reading doesn't split one receipt into several expenses or read a receipt from a card statement; the category is picked only from the venue's own categories by exact name.

**33. Kiosk analytics — follow-ups** *(shipped with migration 208)*
- A playback uses the design and the menu as they are now (a note says when the design changed): there is no copy of the design or menu as the guest saw them, so a dish taken off the menu since doesn't show in the playback (the steps still name it) and prices may differ.
- Touches are kept in CSS pixels of the kiosk's layout (a phone kiosk zoomed out by `useKioskViewport()` records in its 800-wide layout); the player draws the frame at each moment's `vp` size, so a screen turned mid-visit plays back turned. Nothing records the device's real pixel size or the zoom.
- Not recorded: what the guest typed (on purpose), sheets and screens that don't use `useJourneyState()` (new ones must), the card machine's own screen, anything outside the kiosk app. Videos and animations play as the kiosk plays them now, not as they were.
- The overview sums at most 20,000 visits per request in the API process (`aggregateJourneys()` over the rows); a busy group over 92 days may hit it (`truncated` says so). No CSV export, no comparison between two designs side by side (filter by design instead), no Pepper tool.
- Not tried on a real kiosk for a full day (storage use, the 60 s sends over a weak connection) or with a screen reader.

**13. Audit remaining pages for the sticky-header-hides-hamburger bug**
*(Touch devices no longer affected: they get a burger rail and never see the floating button. The remaining audit only matters for non-touch screens below `lg`, and the spacer class to add is now `max-lg:notouch:pl-14`, not `pl-14 lg:pl-4`.)*
The `z-40` bump on `AppShell`'s hamburger button (see Common-mistakes) makes it clickable
on every page regardless of that page's own header z-index, so nothing is currently broken.
But pages whose own `position: sticky` top-of-page header sits at `z-10` or higher still
visually paint UNDER the (now-topmost) hamburger without the `pl-14 lg:pl-4` spacer, which
looks scruffy rather than actually blocking navigation. `CashRecon.jsx`'s 5 views got the
`pl-14` treatment as part of this fix; still to check (grep `sticky top-0 z-1\|sticky top-0
z-2` across `admin/src/pages`): `Menus.jsx`, `Website.jsx`,
`Timeline.jsx` — confirm each one's header is actually the page's own top-of-viewport
element (not a nested table `<thead>` or in-panel section header, which aren't at risk)
before adding `pl-14`.

---

## Environment variables

Both `.env.example` files document all required vars.
The API will refuse to start if any required var is missing (Zod validation in `src/config/env.js`).

Key vars to set before running:
- `DATABASE_URL` — pre-filled by setup.sh
- `REDIS_URL` — update password to match Redis config
- `AUTH0_DOMAIN` / `AUTH0_AUDIENCE` — from Auth0 dashboard
- `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` / `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` — from Stripe dashboard
- Admin portal: `VITE_AUTH0_DOMAIN` / `VITE_AUTH0_CLIENT_ID` / `VITE_AUTH0_AUDIENCE`
- `LOYVERSE_API_URL` (optional, default `https://api.loyverse.com/v1.0`; only changed to test against a stand-in server — Loyverse tokens are per tenant, saved in Cash Recon settings)
- `GLORIAFOOD_API_URL` (optional, default `https://pos.globalfoodsoft.com`; only changed to test against a stand-in server — poll keys and push addresses are per venue, in Cash Recon settings)
- `SECRETS_KEY` (seals stored integration keys, `services/vault.js`, migration 179: 32 bytes, base64 or hex; required in production, added to `api/.env` by every deploy when missing, never changed; back it up with the `.env`: without it the stored keys can't be read)
- `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (optional fallback: a key saved on the Platform page, AI usage tab, wins; with neither the AI chats are off and the MCP connectors still work), `AI_MODEL` (the main model, default in `config/env.js`), `AI_MODEL_FAST` (the everyday model chats start on, default in `config/env.js`; empty = every chat on `AI_MODEL`), `AI_EFFORT` (default medium)

## Common mistakes to avoid

> **This section is append-only.** Never delete an entry — only correct one if it turns out to be wrong. This is the most-read section of the doc; every "subtle why didn't that work" bug gets a new entry.

- **Never query tenant tables without `withTenant()`** — RLS silently returns empty rows, not an error.
- **Never trust client for payment confirmation** — booking only becomes permanent via Stripe webhook, not client-side resolve.
- **Never store slots** — they are always computed. Do not add a `slots` table.
- **`slot_duration_mins` ≠ `slot_interval_mins`** — duration is how long a booking lasts, interval is how often a new slot starts. Both live on different tables.
- **`max_covers = 0` on a slot cap ≠ fully booked** — it means intentionally blocked. The `zero_cap_display` venue setting controls whether it shows as hidden or unavailable.
- **Hold TTL is configurable per venue** — `booking_rules.hold_ttl_secs`. Default 300 (5 min). Widget countdown is driven by `expires_at` from the hold response, not a local timer.
- **pg_cron is a fallback** — the primary hold release is the explicit `DELETE /holds/:id` call. Don't rely on the sweep as the main path.
- **`slot_time` from DB is `HH:MM:SS`** — PostgreSQL `TIME` columns serialise to `HH:MM:SS` in JSON, but the API validation regex expects `HH:MM`. Always call `.slice(0, 5)` on slot_time values before using them as keys or sending them back to the API. **CAVEAT:** this only applies to columns of type `time` (e.g. `sitting_slot_caps.slot_time`, `venue_sittings.opens_at`). The composite type returned by `get_available_slots()` had its `slot_time` field changed from `time` to `timestamptz` in migration 020, and postgres.js deserialises that to a JS `Date` object — `.slice()` on it throws `s.slot_time.slice is not a function`. For slot_result rows, convert to the venue-local `HH:MM` via `Intl.DateTimeFormat('en-GB', { timeZone: venue.timezone, hour: '2-digit', minute: '2-digit', hour12: false }).format(date)` (see `widgetApi.js`). The admin `/api/slots` route passes the Date through and the consumers handle it — the widget API materialises HH:MM strings because the public widget JS interpolates them directly into a date string.
- **`combination_id` must be copied from hold to booking** — `POST /bookings` (free-booking path) copies it; `confirm_hold()` PG function does not yet — see Outstanding items. Never assume the booking record has `combination_id` set just because the hold had it (on the Stripe webhook path).
- **`guest_notes` was silently dropped on paid bookings** — now fixed. The Stripe webhook INSERT previously omitted both `combination_id` and `guest_notes`. Both are now copied from the hold record.
- **`/relocate` throws 422 when no combo exists for expanded table set** — step 4 no longer auto-creates combinations. If adjacency expansion finds T8+T9+T10 but no pre-configured combination exists for those tables, the endpoint returns 422 and the drag snaps back. Operators must create the combination in the Tables page first.
- **`v_exc_template` in `get_available_slots()`** — when no schedule exception covers the date, the record variable is never assigned by SELECT INTO. Accessing `.id` on an uninitialized PL/pgSQL RECORD throws PG 55000 'not assigned yet'. Always use a scalar UUID variable (`v_exc_template_id uuid := NULL`) and assign it only when the record IS found.
- **Admin override `starts_at` is server-local time** — `POST /bookings/admin-override` receives `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS` without a timezone offset and passes it to `new Date()`, which interprets it as server-local time. If the server and venue are in different timezones, the stored `starts_at` will be offset. Future work: pass the venue's IANA timezone and convert in the server.
- **Timeline grey strips are per-column, not per-row** — grey = outside any sitting OR slot cap explicitly set to 0 (reason='unavailable'). Fully-booked slots (reason='full') are NOT greyed — they stay white. Secondary combo rows clip grey strips to the booking's own time window only; grey IS shown outside the booking's time.
- **`table_combination_members` has no `tenant_id`** — RLS on that table is enforced via a subquery on `table_combinations`. Never pass `tenant_id` to INSERT INTO `table_combination_members`.
- **`notificationQueue.add()` must never be awaited in request path** — Redis may be unavailable. Always use fire-and-forget: `.catch(e => req.log.warn(...))`. The booking is created and the response sent; the email is best-effort.
- **Customer anonymise never deletes** — GDPR right to erasure is implemented by overwriting PII fields with placeholder values and setting `is_anonymised=true`. The row is kept for audit/reporting. Never DELETE a customer row.
- **`api.download()` in the frontend** — use `api.download(path, filename)` to fetch auth-protected binary responses (JSON exports, etc.). It handles the Auth0 token injection and creates a temporary object URL for the browser Save dialog.
- **deploy.sh requires root but builds as ubuntu** — the script requires `sudo` to talk to PM2, but all `npm install` / `npm run build` steps must run as the `ubuntu` user to keep `dist/` and `node_modules/` owned by ubuntu. If `dist/` becomes root-owned, run `sudo chown -R ubuntu:ubuntu /home/ubuntu/app/admin/dist` then redeploy.
- **`slot_result` type requires DROP + CREATE** — PostgreSQL composite types used in functions cannot be altered. Any migration that adds fields to `slot_result` must `DROP FUNCTION get_available_slots` first, then `DROP TYPE slot_result`, then recreate both. See migration 020.
- **slot warning in booking modal uses local time** — `new Date(slot_time).getHours()` interprets the UTC timestamp in the browser's local timezone. If the server timezone and browser timezone differ, warning thresholds may be slightly off. Fix: use `slotsRes.timezone` to convert properly (outstanding).
- **`checked_out` is NOT the same as `completed`** — `completed` was renamed to `seated` in migration 017. `checked_out` is a new separate status (after seated). Do not confuse the two in queries or UI labels.
- **Secondary combo row canvas at z=4** — secondary combo rows (where the spanning card from the primary row above paints over them) must have `z-index: 4` AND `pointer-events: none` on their canvas div. z=4 ensures their clipped gradient background paints above the primary's z=3 stacking context. `pointer-events: none` lets click and drag events pass through to the primary row's spanning card. Without z=4, the gradient is invisible (the primary's z=3 stacking context covers T3B entirely).
- **`handleCustSearch` in BookingDrawer takes a single field value** — pass only the value of the field that just changed, not all three fields. The previous multi-field priority logic meant editing name while email was already filled always searched by email, so name-only changes never triggered search.
- **`ColourPickerRow` has local `hexInput` state** — the component uses `useState(value)` for the colour wheel and hex text input, which only initialises once. If the context value changes externally (e.g. an external swatch button calls `setTimelineBg(hex)`), the picker will show a stale colour unless synced via `useEffect(() => setHexInput(value), [value])`. Always include this effect when using `ColourPickerRow` alongside external swatch buttons that update the same context value.
- **`hourWidth` must be threaded everywhere in Timeline** — `HOUR_WIDTH = 80` is the module-level constant used as the default param in utility functions. The runtime `hourWidth` (80 or 120 from `wideColumns`) must be passed as a prop to `TableRow`, `BookingCard`, and `TimelineHeader`, and used in all pixel calculations (drag/resize deltas, `nowX`, `unavailableStrips`, gradient offsets, `handleCanvasClick`). Using the module constant directly in any of these bypasses the wide-columns setting.
- **`wideColumns` dep in useMemos** — `unavailableStrips` useMemo depends on `[sittingsForDate, slotsOverlay, hourWidth, totalWidth]`. If `hourWidth` is omitted from the dep array, the grey strips will not reposition when the wide-columns toggle changes.
- **`hourWidth`/`totalWidth`/`rowHeight` must be declared before `nowX` useMemo** — these are `const` declarations inside the component function body. `nowX` references `hourWidth` and `totalWidth` inside its callback. Because `useMemo` runs synchronously on first render, accessing a `const` that appears later in the function body throws a Temporal Dead Zone (TDZ) `ReferenceError`. Always keep `hourWidth`/`totalWidth`/`rowHeight` above any useMemo that references them.
- **`headerBgStrips` removes `bg-background` from `TimelineHeader` outer div** — when `headerBgStrips` is true, `backgroundStyle` is applied as inline style to the header's outer div, which overrides the Tailwind `bg-background` class (inline styles win). The sticky label cell inside still carries its own `bg-background` to mask scrolling content behind it. If you add any new wrapper classes to `TimelineHeader` that set a background, they will be silently overridden when `headerBgStrips` is on.
- **`showStartLine` colour picker only mounts when line is enabled** — in Settings, the `ColourPickerRow` for `startLineColour` is conditionally rendered inside `{showStartLine && ...}`. This means the picker's local state is reset each time the toggle is switched on. This is intentional (avoids a stale picker) but means the hex input always initialises from the context value when re-shown.
- **`sessionStartXs` replaces `firstOpenX` in Timeline** — the opening-hour line is now drawn once per sitting (not just the first sitting of the day). The useMemo returns an array; render site maps over it. Never revert to a single scalar — venues with lunch + dinner sessions need both lines.
- **Drawer table override routes single vs multi** — single table → `PATCH /relocate` (cascade displacement); multi-table → `PATCH /tables` (direct assignment, no displacement); unallocated table → `PATCH /tables` (never /relocate — the relocate endpoint rejects the unallocated table ID). Check `handleTableSave` branching logic before modifying.
- **`tables` query in BookingDrawer is non-lazy** — it was previously `enabled: editMode === 'table'`. It now fetches immediately on drawer open so `unallocatedTable` and `hasCapacityIssue` are available in view mode. Do not re-add the `enabled` gate.
- **Sitting `name` field is nullable text** — `SittingBody` validates it as `z.string().max(100).nullable().optional()`. In the frontend, always fall back to the time-range string: `sitting.name ?? \`${sitting.opens_at.slice(0,5)}–${sitting.closes_at.slice(0,5)}\``.
- **`sidebarExpandedDefault` only controls initial state** — it initialises the `open` state in AppShell's `useState`. Subsequent manual toggles work normally per-session. Mobile always starts collapsed regardless of the setting. Touch devices (see the `IS_TOUCH` entry below) ignore this setting entirely and always start collapsed, since they get the phone-style sidebar treatment regardless of screen width.
- **`AppShell`'s sidebar treats ALL touch devices like phone-width, never the `lg:` desktop icon-rail/push-content mode, regardless of actual screen width** — `IS_TOUCH` (`navigator.maxTouchPoints > 0`, same pattern as `NewBookingModal.jsx`/`MobileViewToggle.jsx`) gates every `lg:` class that previously assumed "wide screen = mouse-driven desktop": `open` starts `false` unconditionally, the collapsed aside is `w-0` (never `lg:w-14`, the icon rail), the aside never gets `lg:relative` (stays a `fixed` overlay that never reserves permanent layout width), and the floating hamburger button's `lg:hidden` is dropped so it stays visible and usable at any width. This exists because landscape tablets (an iPad landscape is ~1024px, right at the `lg` breakpoint) were getting the desktop treatment — a permanently-expanded or icon-rail sidebar eating screen width — even though front-of-house staff hold and tap a tablet like a phone, not a laptop with a mouse. Every future `lg:` class added to `AppShell.jsx` for sidebar open/collapsed behaviour must be paired the same way (`!IS_TOUCH && 'lg:...'`) or a touch tablet will silently regress back to desktop chrome.
- **`sittingStats` useMemo uses local browser time for HH:MM comparison** — booking start times are UTC ISO strings; `new Date(b.starts_at).getHours()` returns local time. If the server timezone differs from the browser, bookings near sitting boundaries may be assigned to the wrong sitting in the stats display (cosmetic only — not used for any business logic).
- **Migration 022 uses `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION`** — unlike migration 020 which had to `DROP TYPE slot_result` before recreation, migration 022 does not change the return type so `CREATE OR REPLACE` is safe. If you ever need to change `slot_result` again, revert to the DROP + CREATE pattern.
- **Overlap detection is frontend-only** — `overlappingIds` is computed from the in-memory `bookingsRes` array. It does not call the API. If a booking is created by another user and the timeline hasn't refreshed, an overlap may not be detected until the next WebSocket push or 60-second refetch.
- **`startHour` must be threaded the same way as `hourWidth`** — `timeToX(iso, hw, sh)` and `sittingTimeToX(t, hw, sh)` both accept `sh` (startHour) as a third optional param defaulting to `START_HOUR`. In the Timeline component, `startHour` is derived from `timelineStart` (context). Pass `startHour` as a prop to `TableRow`, `BookingCard`, and `TimelineHeader` — exactly the same threading pattern as `hourWidth`. All useMemos calling these functions must include `startHour` in their dep arrays. Using the module constant `START_HOUR` directly anywhere in the component body bypasses the timeline hour range setting.
- **`table_locked` is checked in `/relocate` only** — the lock prevents cascade displacement when an operator drags a booking onto tables occupied by a locked booking. It does NOT prevent the operator from directly reassigning via `PATCH /tables` (multi-table path) or `POST /bookings/admin-override`. The lock is a hint to the relocation engine, not a hard DB constraint.
- **Website CMS: subdomain slug is a GLOBAL namespace** — `website_config.subdomain_slug UNIQUE` across all tenants. Separate from `tenants.slug` (Auth0 lookup). `GET /api/website/slug-available?slug=...` must be used before POSTing a new config; the endpoint is authenticated but does NOT use `withTenant()` because it needs to see other tenants' rows.
- **Website CMS: custom_domain is the SSR lookup key, not the SSL provisioning key** — the app resolves the Host header against `website_config.custom_domain`. It does NOT issue certificates. SSL is terminated at Nginx (or Caddy on-demand TLS); the tenant-facing flow is: (a) tenant sets `custom_domain`, (b) tenant points DNS at our IP, (c) tenant clicks "Verify DNS" → `POST /api/website/verify-domain` (checks A + CNAME records), (d) an out-of-band process provisions a cert. Setting the domain clears `custom_domain_verified` so the site stays offline until verified.
- **Website CMS: `theme` JSONB is column-overwrite, not deep-merge** — PATCHing `theme: {...}` replaces the whole column. The admin page's `ThemeSection` holds the FULL merged theme in local state and PATCHes the entire object. Don't try to PATCH a partial theme object — the missing keys become null, not defaults.
- **Website CMS: pluggable storage via `STORAGE_DRIVER`** — `local` writes to `UPLOAD_DIR` and serves through `@fastify/static` at `/uploads/*`. `s3` lazy-imports `@aws-sdk/client-s3` (listed as `optionalDependencies` so local installs don't pay the download cost). Switching drivers mid-flight will ORPHAN existing upload URLs — run a migration script to copy files between backends before switching.
- **Website CMS: templates share CSS variables via `views/site/shared/head.eta`** — the shared head computes every `--c-*`, `--f-*`, `--r-*` CSS variable from `config.theme` (merged over defaults). When adding a new template, consume those variables; don't add a per-template CSS variable schema. When adding a new theme knob, update `ThemeSchema` in `routes/website.js`, the default constant in `admin/src/pages/Website.jsx`, and the CSS-variable emit in `shared/head.eta` together.
- **Website CMS: reserved subdomains** — `api, www, admin, app, mail, static, assets, cdn, ws, stripe, webhook, webhooks` never match as tenant sites, regardless of DB content. Extend `RESERVED_SUBDOMAINS` in `siteRenderer.js` before using any new infrastructure subdomain.
- **Website CMS: single-venue tenants skip the location picker** — `loadTenantBundle` hydrates the sole venue's hours/address/PDF menus/allergens onto the home bundle, `/locations` 302s to `/locations/{slug}`, `/menu` 302s to `/locations/{slug}/menu`, and the header drops a `/locations` nav item. Do NOT special-case this with a `site_mode` flag — venue count is the source of truth. Adding a second venue automatically restores the multi-location hub. Hours are loaded independently of `show_find_us` (they used to blank together).
- **Website hours default to the venue schedule** — `website_config.opening_hours_source` defaults to `venue` (sittings on `venue_schedule_templates`). `manual` is only for operators who want advertised hours that differ from booking hours. Empty `website_opening_hours` is not a source of truth. GET `/website/opening-hours` uses the same `loadOpeningHours` helper as SSR so the canvas preview matches the live site. Single-venue tenants get an **Opening hours** item on the Tenant site nav (and an Edit hours button on the block inspector); switching to Manual seeds the form from the current schedule.
- **Menu variant groups are tenant-wide libraries** — `menu_variant_groups` + `menu_variant_options` hold reusable option sets (Protein, Size). Items attach groups via `menu_item_variant_groups`; optional `menu_item_variant_prices` override a single option's price on that dish. Ad-hoc `menu_item_variants` remain for one-off options. Do not copy group options onto items — keep the live link. Item photos live on `menu_items.image_url`.
- **Inbox sender logos are BIMI, not an email header** — `tenant_site.bimi_svg` is served at `GET /bimi.svg` (and `/.well-known/bimi.svg`) on the tenant site. The operator adds `default._bimi.<from-domain> TXT "v=BIMI1; l=https://<host>/bimi.svg;"`. Gmail/Apple also need a VMC; Yahoo/Fastmail do not. Platform logo is `admin/public/bimi.svg` for `noreply@macaroonie.com`.
- **Every website block has an Anchor ID** — stored as `data.anchor_id`, edited in the shared BlockInspector (not per-type editors). SSR wraps the block in `<div id="…">`. Fallbacks: reservations_widget → `reservations`, opening_hours → `hours`, find_us → `find-us`. Do not hardcode those ids on the partials.
- **Extra pages are `website_pages.kind` `page` | `modal`** — standalone at `/p/{slug}`, modals opened via `#modal/{slug}` (optional `:anchor`). Built with the same PageBuilder (`blocks` jsonb). LinkPicker in the inspector lists this-page anchors, other pages ± anchors, and modals. Do not store structured link objects — href strings only. Modal markup is injected from `views/site/shared/modals.eta`.
- **Website CMS: `loadSiteBundle()` reloads on every request** — no caching. Cheap (~1 round trip for the config + a single parallel query for everything else) but not free. If traffic becomes a concern, add a short TTL cache keyed on `(tenant_id, updated_at)` inside the service, invalidated on any admin mutation. Don't cache by slug alone — would leak between tenants if slugs are ever reused.
- **Website CMS: `GET /api/site/:slug` short-circuits on `is_published = false`** — returns 404 for the public JSON AND the SSR renderer. Admin preview must go through the admin portal's authenticated path (or pass `includeUnpublished: true` in a future preview endpoint). Never expose the `includeUnpublished` flag on the public route.
- **Website CMS: `custom_domain_verified` is cleared on every mutation of `custom_domain`** — the PATCH handler explicitly sets `custom_domain_verified = false` whenever the body contains `custom_domain`. Refetch the config on the client after PATCHing so the UI shows the unverified state and the "Verify DNS" button.
- **Website CMS: Eta includes are path-relative to the `views` root** — the templates use `include('../../shared/head', it)` because they live 3 levels deep (`views/site/templates/{key}/index.eta`). Don't change the directory layout without updating every include path. Tests catch this only by rendering each template.
- **`timelineStart`/`timelineEnd` are integers, not strings** — stored in localStorage as numbers. When reading from `tlSettings`, do arithmetic directly: `const totalHours = endHour - startHour`. The `<select>` dropdowns in Settings use `Number(e.target.value)` on `onChange` to ensure the stored value is always a number, not a string.
- **React hook stability** — always return stable references from custom hooks. Any object/function used in a `useEffect` deps array must be wrapped in `useMemo` / `useCallback` or you get an infinite render loop. This applies to every `useContext`-returned value, every query result object, and every callback passed as a prop.
- **Migration idempotency** — wrap migrations in `BEGIN`/`COMMIT` (our runner does this automatically via `sql.begin()`). All seeds should use `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` / `WHERE NOT EXISTS`. Re-running `scripts/migrate.js` must be safe.
- **Trust but verify (session summaries)** — never reference session-summary items as "still open" without checking the actual code. Session summaries go stale between runs. Always grep / check live code before claiming something is pending.
- **File ownership on server** — if git throws "dubious ownership" on the Lightsail server, fix once with `git config --global --add safe.directory /home/ubuntu/app` rather than chowning files. The deploy workflow uses `git reset --hard` which needs this.
- **`manage_token` is the ONLY auth for the guest manage page** — the UUID in the URL IS the authentication. Do not add rate-limiting or IP-blocking that would break email link clicks. Do not expose manage_token in any admin API list response (it would let any admin-portal user access any guest's manage page).
- **`notificationQueue.add()` job name changed to `'booking_email'`** — the old `'confirmation'` / `'cancellation'` job names are no longer used. The worker now calls `processEmailJob` from `emailWorker.js` instead of the legacy `notificationSvc.sendNotification()`.
- **Reminder jobs use `jobId: 'reminder-{bookingId}'`** — BullMQ deduplicates by jobId, so re-confirming a booking doesn't create duplicate reminders. If the booking is cancelled, the reminder job stays in the queue but `processEmailJob` skips it (checks `reminder_sent_at` and terminal status).
- **Email template `theme` JSONB is column-overwrite** — same as website_config.theme. The admin page's ThemeSection holds the FULL merged theme and PATCHes the entire object. Never PATCH a partial theme.
- **SC `included_in_income` XOR variance** — the two SC flags (`included_in_income` + `included_in_takings`) are independent booleans. The variance formula is `totalIncome + scAdjustment - totalTakings` where scAdjustment is the signed XOR sum. When both flags are on, the adjustment is 0 (not doubled). When only income is on, the SC is subtracted from the expected-till figure. When only takings is on, it's added. **Superseded by migration 096** (kept for history, per the append-only rule) — these two booleans were replaced by `takings_effect`/`income_effect` (`'none' | 'add' | 'subtract'` each), since a fixed always-add-on-takings/always-subtract-on-income direction couldn't express every scenario an operator needed (e.g. deducting from takings). See the newer entry below and Docs.jsx's Cash Reconciliation section.
- **Migration 027 number collision** — both `027_cash_reconciliation.sql` and `027_per_venue_websites.sql` exist. They have different version strings in `schema_migrations` so the runner applies both. Confusing but not broken. Do not renumber — would cause re-application on servers that already ran them.
- **`platform_admins` table has NO RLS** — it's a global table queried during auth before any tenant context is set. Never add RLS to it. Access is gated in the API middleware (`requirePlatformAdmin`), not at the DB level.
- **`requireRole()` lets platform admins through automatically** — platform admins bypass all role checks. If you need to restrict something to ONLY tenant-level owners (not platform admins), you need a custom guard that explicitly checks `req.user.role === 'owner' && !req.isPlatformAdmin`.
- **`VITE_AUTH0_ORG_ID` is now optional** — when omitted, Auth0 prompts the user to select an org on login (for multi-tenant users). When set, it forces that org (existing single-tenant behaviour). Removing this env var from an existing deployment changes login UX.
- **Org switcher triggers a full re-authentication** — `loginWithRedirect({ organization: auth0_org_id })` navigates away from the SPA. All local state is lost. This is by design — Auth0 issues a new JWT with the new org's claims. TanStack Query cache is rebuilt on return. **Superseded** — this described the original org-scoped-login design. Everyday tenant switching no longer does this at all: it's a client-side `X-Tenant-Id` swap (`setSelectedTenant()` + page reload), no second Auth0 login, see the "Tenant selection" Completed item. This mechanism is now used only for Auth0 invitation-link acceptance.
- **Self-protection in team management** — `PATCH /team/:userId` and `DELETE /team/:userId` reject operations where the target `auth0_user_id` matches `req.user.sub`. Cannot demote yourself, cannot deactivate yourself. Enforced API-side (not just UI).
- **Auth0 Mgmt API is OPTIONAL** — when `AUTH0_MGMT_CLIENT_ID`/`AUTH0_MGMT_CLIENT_SECRET`/`AUTH0_INVITE_CLIENT_ID` are unset, `auth0MgmtSvc.isConfigured()` returns false and `/team/invite` falls back to creating a local row only (logged warning, Team UI shows an amber banner). This is intentional for local dev. In production all three MUST be set.
- **When the user pastes a string and errors follow, RECHECK THE STRING first** — don't dive into schema, validation, or framework debugging until the pasted value has been verified end-to-end. Bit us hard with Postmark (2026-05-02): an invisible character from the original clipboard paste produced a 401 "invalid token" response, and I spent multiple turns chasing schema bugs, CHECK constraints, and POST upsert behaviour — while the actual fix was the user re-copying the token to the clipboard. **The fastest sanity check** is to call the external API directly (e.g. `curl -H 'X-Postmark-Server-Token: <pasted-value>' ...`) with the same string and see if the third party rejects it. If THEY reject the raw value, it's the string. If they accept it, it's the app. Don't reflexively add `.trim()` calls to mask possible whitespace — that hides the real issue and tomorrow's bug looks identical.
- **`systemctl reload nginx` doesn't always pick up new `location` modifiers** — adding `^~`, `~*`, or other matching modifiers to existing locations sometimes leaves the running workers using the old matching table even after a clean reload (config tested OK, no error). Symptom: the new behaviour is in `nginx -T` output but requests still hit the old block. `systemctl restart nginx` (full process restart, ~50ms downtime) clears it. For routing-modifier changes, prefer restart over reload.
- **Eta `<% ... %>` JS blocks containing `//` line comments break the parser** — production Eta sometimes fails to detect the closing `%>` when the JS body has line comments (especially comments that contain backticks or em-dashes). Symptom: 500 with "Bad template syntax: Unexpected token '>'", and the compiled JS has literal `<%` / `%>` tokens appearing as raw source where Eta failed to compile them. Bit us on `views/site/shared/head.eta` (May 2026) where I'd added a multi-line `// comment` block above some `const` declarations. **Workaround**: use `/* ... */` block comments inside `<% %>` JS blocks (the rest of the file already does this — match the convention). Single-line `//` comments are usually fine if they don't sit between `<%` and the next `%>` boundary, but block comments are universally safe.
- **Eta `<%= %>` interpolation breaks on certain `.filter(...).join(...)` chains** — production Eta sometimes fails to emit the closing `)` of the wrapping `__eta.e(...)` when the inline expression is a chained array method ending in `.join(' ')` or `.join(' · ')`. Symptom: 500 with "Bad template syntax: Unexpected token '>'", and the compiled JS shown in the error has the source line `<div><%= [a, b].filter(Boolean).join(' ') %></div>` followed directly by `__eta.res+='</div>...'` without the matching `)`. The expression itself is valid JS — Eta just trips on the closing-tag detection in this exact shape. Bit us on `templates/classic/partials/footer.eta` (May 2026). **Workaround**: hoist the join into a `<% const x = [a,b].filter(Boolean).join(' ') %>` statement above the markup, then interpolate the variable: `<%= x %>`. Apply this to every venue card and address line — there are ~15 places across `templates/{classic,modern,onethai}/{fallback_home,fallback_location,locations,menu_hub,partials/footer}.eta`. Do NOT inline-chain `.filter(Boolean).join(...)` inside `<%= %>`.
- **Eta JS blocks must NOT start with `(` after a text/HTML line** — JavaScript's ASI (automatic semicolon insertion) does NOT insert a semicolon before a line starting with `(`, so the previous line's value gets called as a function. Symptom: `"<main>\n\n" is not a function` 500 from Eta. Bit us once when an `index.eta` had `<main>\n\n<% (it.config.home_blocks || []).forEach(...) %>` — the literal `<main>\n\n` ends up being called. Fix: assign the expression to a `const` first, OR use a `for…of` loop, OR put a leading semicolon. Same trap applies to any `<% (...)... %>` after HTML text.
- **Block-based page composition lives in `home_blocks` JSONB** — `website_config.home_blocks` is the only source of truth for the home-page layout. Both SSR templates (classic + modern) iterate `home_blocks` via `views/site/blocks/renderer.eta`. An empty array renders an empty page — there is no legacy flat-layout fallback (removed 2026-05-03 since we're pre-prod). Legacy admin nav entries (Hero, About) were also removed; their content lives inside the page-builder blocks. Legacy columns (`hero_*`, `about_text`, `about_html`) remain in the schema for now but are not read by any template — drop in a future migration when convenient.
- **Block dispatch is a single `<%~ include('./renderer', { ...it, block }) %>`** — the renderer.eta switches on `it.block.type` and includes the right per-block partial.
