Adding a new block type means: (1) entry in `blockRegistry.js` with editor component + default data, (2) new partial under `views/site/blocks/<type>.eta`, (3) line in `renderer.eta` for the dispatch. The frontend block list is the contract — server treats `data` as opaque JSONB.
- **Data blocks (gallery, hours, contact, etc.) don't store content on the block** — they just store display options like `heading`. Content comes from the existing per-section data (website_config fields, website_gallery_images rows, etc.). This is intentional — a single content source feeds both the legacy flat layout and any block instance, so users editing one place see the change everywhere.
- **All website-CMS image uploads mirror into `media_items`** — `/website/upload` does the storage put AND inserts a media_items row when `kind === 'images'` so the asset is visible in the Media library. PDF kinds (`menus`, `docs`) DON'T mirror because they're tracked via `website_menu_documents`. The `media_item_id` is returned in the response for callers that want to link.
- **`about_html` is the new rich-text source of truth** — templates check `it.config.about_html` first (rendered raw via `<%~ %>`) and fall back to `it.config.about_text` (escaped, with `white-space: pre-wrap`). The admin section uses TipTap and writes to `about_html`. The legacy column stays so existing data keeps rendering. Both Eta `about` blocks (classic + modern) need updating in lockstep when the about layout changes.
- **Theme presets are partial themes — deep-merged onto the current theme** — so a preset that only specifies colors leaves the user's typography tweaks intact. To curate a new preset, add to `THEME_PRESETS` in `Website.jsx`. Don't make presets mutable — they're keyed by `key` and rendered as fixed buttons.
- **Gallery layout is template-side, not CSS-side** — the Eta template branches on `it.config.gallery_style`. The `gallery_size` value maps to two numbers (`thumbMin`, `thumbHt`) at the top of the gallery block — keep them in sync if you change the size enum.
- **Opening hours `source = 'venue'` derives from `venue_schedule_templates` + `venue_sittings`** — `siteDataSvc.loadSiteBundle()` runs a separate aggregate query (`MIN(opens_at), MAX(closes_at)` per `day_of_week`). Result rows are normalised to the same shape `website_opening_hours` produces (`HH:MM` slices, `is_closed` boolean), so the templates don't branch on source. When the user switches source from manual → venue, manual rows in `website_opening_hours` are NOT deleted — they're just ignored in render. Switch back to keep them.
- **Apex Nginx `location /uploads/` must use `^~` prefix-priority** — the apex `macaroonie.com` server block has a regex `location ~* \.(js|css|png|jpg|...)$` that serves static admin-portal assets from `/home/ubuntu/app/admin/dist`. In Nginx, regex locations take precedence over plain prefix locations, so a plain `location /uploads/` is shadowed by the extension regex for `.png`/`.jpg`/etc. and the request gets served (with 404) from the wrong root. Fix by writing `location ^~ /uploads/ { proxy_pass ...; }` — the `^~` modifier tells Nginx to stop after a prefix match without checking regex locations. Same trap applies to any new path serving binary uploads on the apex.
- **PATCH `/website/config` and POST `/website/verify-domain` MUST scope by venue_id** — both endpoints used to filter UPDATE/SELECT only by `tenant_id`, which silently overwrote every venue's website_config under the tenant. A hero image saved for "One Thai Cafe in Ware" propagated to every venue site of the same master franchisee. Fixed (2026-05-03) by including `id = ${existing.id}` (looked up via the existing `ensureConfig(tenant_id, venue_id)` helper) on UPDATE, and by requiring `venue_id` query/body param on `verify-domain`. When adding new endpoints that mutate `website_config`, ALWAYS scope by `venue_id`. The `ensureConfig` helper exists for exactly this — use it.
- **`POST /api/email-templates/settings/:venueId` upserts ALL body fields** — used to be a thin "create with email_provider only" handler that silently dropped `provider_api_key`, `from_name`, etc. on first save. The frontend would call POST when no row existed, get a 201 back, and think the credentials had been saved when they hadn't. Symptom: switching to Postmark, saving, then trying to send a test → "Request does not contain a valid Server token" because the token never made it to the DB. Fixed by routing POST through the same upsert helper as PATCH (insert-or-noop, then dynamic UPDATE of supplied fields). Always test first-save flow when adding a settings page, not just edits.
- **`provider_domain` field is overloaded across email providers** — the column stores different things depending on `email_provider`: Mailgun uses it for the sending domain (`mg.macaroonie.com`); Postmark uses it for the message stream name (`outbound`); SES re-uses `provider_region` for the AWS region. The UI gates input fields by provider so users never see the overloading. If you add a new provider that needs ANOTHER field type, prefer adding `provider_extra` JSONB or another typed column rather than further overloading. Don't rename `provider_domain` — too many migrations + worker + test-send paths reference it.
- **Auth0 Mgmt API requires a Login Action that reads `app_metadata.role`** — role sync (`PATCH /team/:userId` calling `updateUserAppMetadata`) writes the role to `app_metadata.role` on the Auth0 user. The Auth0 Login Action then injects it as a JWT claim (`https://{AUTH0_DOMAIN}/claims/role`). Without that Action in place, the JWT keeps the old role until the user re-invites or you manually set it in the Auth0 dashboard. Verify the Action exists in Auth0 → Actions → Library before relying on role sync.
- **`assign_membership_on_login: true` lets anyone into the org — auth middleware must enforce users row** — `provisionTenantOrg()` enables Auth0 connections with `assign_membership_on_login: true`, which means any Google/email account that logs in through the org's Auth0 connection is automatically added as an org member and receives the org's `tenant_id` claim. Without the `userNotFound` guard in `requireAuth`, those users pass auth with the JWT role default (`operator`) and can see the app. The fix (June 2026): after the reconcile try-catch, if `userNotFound === true` (reconcile ran cleanly but found no users row), return 403 "not invited". Do NOT set `userNotFound` on DB exceptions — that would block legitimate users during infra failures.
- **Auth0 invitation roll-back on failure** — `POST /team/invite` inserts the local row first, then calls Auth0. If Auth0 returns non-2xx the local row is DELETEd before responding 502. Re-invites work cleanly because no orphan row remains. Do not change the order — calling Auth0 first leaves no audit trail when the local insert fails on a unique conflict.
- **First-login user reconciliation** — `requireAuth` in `auth.js` reconciles Auth0 sub ↔ local user every request. Three cases: (1) already linked → bump `last_login_at`, use local `users.role` as authoritative; (2) `auth0_user_id` is NULL but email matches an invited row → link sub, bump login; (3) `is_active=false` locally → reject with 403 even if Auth0 still trusts the user. The local DB is the source of truth for `req.user.role`, not the JWT — so role changes take effect without waiting for the next Auth0 token refresh.
- **`AUTH0_INVITE_CLIENT_ID` is the SPA client_id, not the M2M one** — the value is the same as the admin portal's `VITE_AUTH0_CLIENT_ID`. It's where users land after accepting their invitation. Confusing because the password reset endpoint also uses it, but the variable name is shared deliberately so there's only one SPA-client value to configure.
- **No conditional `useQuery` between an early return and another hook** — React error #310 (`Rendered more hooks than during the previous render`) fires when a component returns early before some hooks, then on a later render reaches those hooks. Pattern that broke `/platform`: `const me = useQuery(...)` → `if (!me?.is_platform_admin) return <Denied/>` → `const tenants = useQuery(...)`. First render `me` is undefined, only 1 hook runs; once `me` loads with `is_platform_admin: true`, 2 hooks run → React aborts. Fix: declare ALL hooks unconditionally at the top, gate the secondary query with `enabled: condition`, and move the early `return` AFTER every hook call.
- **Auth0 invitation links require `invitation` + `organization` to be forwarded to `/authorize`** — when an invited user clicks the email link they land on `https://<app>/?invitation=X&organization=org_Y`. `RequireAuth` in `main.jsx` MUST detect those query params and pass them as `authorizationParams` on `loginWithRedirect`. Without this, Auth0 runs a normal login → user authenticates with their existing Google/email account → Auth0 rejects with "user X is not part of org_Y" because the invitation acceptance step never ran. The check must run BEFORE the `isAuthenticated` gate too — an already-logged-in user clicking an invitation for a different org still needs the acceptance flow.
- **Auth0 invitation requires `Application Login URI` set on the SPA app** — Auth0 returns 400 `A default login route is required to generate the invitation url` from `POST /api/v2/organizations/{orgId}/invitations` when the SPA application has no Application Login URI. Set it under Auth0 → Applications → Macaroonie Admin → Settings → Application URIs → Application Login URI to the SPA root (e.g. `https://macaroonie.com`). Without this, every invite call returns 400 and the local row is rolled back.
- **Custom error status to dodge Nginx error pages** — Nginx by default replaces upstream JSON 502/504 bodies with its own HTML "Bad Gateway" / "Gateway Timeout" pages, so the admin UI sees no `body.error` and shows the literal HTTP status text. For "I caught the upstream failure cleanly" cases (e.g. Auth0 invitation rejected), throw `httpError(422, ...)` instead of 502 — Nginx leaves 4xx bodies alone, so the actual error message reaches the operator.
- **Auth0 invitation setup is 8 steps and Auth0's own docs are stale** — the actual current dashboard requires: (1) M2M app authorised against Management API with the 6 scopes; (2) `AUTH0_INVITE_CLIENT_ID` = SPA client_id; (3) Application Login URI set on the SPA app (Settings → Application URIs); (4) Allowed Callback/Logout/Web Origins all include the SPA root; (5) Connections enabled at the **Application** level (Application → Connections tab); (6) Connections added at the **Organization** level (Organization → Connections tab); (7) **per-connection Auto-Membership = Enable** inside Org → Connection → Authentication section (THIS IS THE STEP THAT'S LEAST OBVIOUS — without it Google login loops back to the picker with no error); (8) Login Action that propagates `app_metadata.role` to a JWT claim. Help.jsx + Docs.jsx have the operator-facing version. If invitations fail, the order to debug is: pm2 logs (real Auth0 error) → Auth0 → Monitoring → Logs (per-attempt cause) → walk this checklist.
- **`provisionTenantOrg` skips steps 6 + 7 of the manual checklist** — when auto-provision is on, the API creates the org and enables Username-Password + Google connections with `assign_membership_on_login: true` in one call. Steps 1–5 + 8 (M2M scopes, env vars, SPA URIs, app-level connection toggles, role-claim Login Action) are still one-time tenant-wide setup that the platform admin does before the first auto-provisioned tenant. Auto-provision is enabled by default in the Platform → New Tenant card; uncheck if you want to manually paste an existing `org_…`.
- **Auth middleware reconcile uses a 3s Promise.race timeout** — the user-row lookup in `requireAuth` races against `setTimeout(reject, 3000)`. If the DB hangs, the timeout rejects, the catch logs a warning, and the request continues with the JWT role — never produces ERR_EMPTY_RESPONSE for the upstream client. The `last_login_at` UPDATE is fire-and-forget (`.catch(...)` only), so the request never blocks on a write that nobody reads in this request.
- **Fastify preHandlers in arrays must be async or use `done` callback** — a sync hook without a `done` parameter (e.g. `function requirePlatformAdmin(req, reply) { ... }`) chained inside a `preHandler: [requireAuth, requirePlatformAdmin]` array silently breaks the chain — the route handler never runs and the request hangs forever with no error. Always declare guards as `async function` (Fastify awaits the returned Promise). Symptom that took hours to find: logs show every preHandler running, the last one's "pass" log fires, then nothing — no route handler log, no `request completed`. Diagnostic was adding a log line at the very top of the route handler and seeing it never fire.
- **Module registry is single source of truth** — `api/src/config/modules.js` lists every module the platform knows about. To add a new one: (1) add it to `MODULES` with a `group` (or `core: true` for always-on) and per-built-in `default` permission map, (2) if it's part of an existing group, append its key to `MODULE_GROUPS[i].moduleKeys`; if it's its own product, add a new entry to `MODULE_GROUPS`, (3) write a migration to `INSERT INTO tenant_modules (tenant_id, module_key, is_enabled) SELECT t.id, '<new_key>', true FROM tenants t ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, (4) wire `requirePermission('<new_key>', 'manage')` on the relevant routes, (5) add a `module: '<new_key>'` field to the relevant `NAV` entries in `AppShell.jsx`. Don't hardcode module keys outside this file.
- **Site model is one-per-TENANT** — `tenant_site` (singleton per tenant, renamed from `tenant_brand_defaults` in migration 043) owns subdomain, custom domain, brand identity, home_blocks, SEO, banner, locations-index settings. `website_config` (one per venue) owns location-page content only: hero, gallery, hours, address, contact, menus, allergens, plus a `page_blocks` jsonb for the per-venue page layout. Don't put subdomain/SEO/home_blocks on `website_config` — they're tenant-level now. Conversely don't put address/hours on `tenant_site` — those are per-location. The renderer merges them at request time via `mergeLocationConfig()` in `siteDataSvc.js`.
- **Routes**: `/` → tenant home (renders `tenant_site.home_blocks`, falls back to `fallback_home.eta`). `/locations` → list of all venues (404 if `hide_locations_index`). `/locations/:venueSlug` → location page (`website_config.page_blocks`, falls back to `fallback_location.eta`). `/locations/:venueSlug/menu[/{id}]` → location menu(s). `/menu` → tenant menu hub (lists venues with menus). `/p/:slug` → tenant-level custom page. `/locations/:venueSlug/p/:slug` → venue-level custom page. `/widget/:venueId` → venue-direct widget (deep link). `/widget/tenant/:tenantId[?venue=ID]` → tenant widget with location picker.
- **`website_pages` venue_id is nullable** — `venue_id IS NULL` means a tenant-level page (visible in tenant-home nav); non-NULL means a location-specific page (visible only in that location's nav). Slug uniqueness is enforced by two partial unique indexes (one for tenant-level, one per venue). The admin `/website/pages?venue_id=tenant` flag selects tenant-level; a UUID selects venue-level.
- **`brand-defaults` admin endpoints alias `tenant-site`** — kept temporarily so the `BrandIdentitySection`/`BrandThemeSection`/`BrandAnalyticsSection`/`BrandBannerSection` admin components keep working without rewrite. Future cleanup: rename the JSX components and switch to `/tenant-site` directly. Both routes write to the same row.
- **`/website/config` POST + PATCH no longer accept subdomain/custom_domain/is_published/meta_title/meta_description** — they were dropped from `website_config` in migration 043. The Zod `VenueConfigBody` rejects these keys; submitting them returns a 400. Move them onto the `/website/tenant-site` PATCH instead.
- **`PageBuilder` accepts `blocksField`/`saveEndpoint`/`invalidateKey` props** — defaults to tenant home (`home_blocks`, `/website/tenant-site`, `['tenant-site']`) but the `<VenueActiveSection>` dispatcher passes `page_blocks`, `/website/config?venue_id=X`, `['website-config', venueId]` for location pages. Don't hardcode `home_blocks` in any new builder code — always read the field from props.
- **`booking_widget` block has 4 fallback levels** — block-data `venue_id` → location's `it.venue.id` → `tenant_site.default_widget_venue_id` → `/widget/tenant/:tenantId` (location picker). On a tenant home page with multiple venues and no default venue set, it renders the picker. Setting `default_widget_venue_id` on the tenant skips the picker for the home embed.
- **Module groups are the unit of tenant-level toggling, NOT modules** — bookings/venues/tables/schedule/rules/customers/widget_test all belong to the `bookings` group and toggle together (they're operationally one product). The `/access` UI shows one switch per group; `PATCH /access/module-groups/:key` upserts every member module's `tenant_modules` row in one transaction. Don't add per-individual-module toggles to the Modules tab — that's a deliberate design choice. The per-module endpoint (`PATCH /access/modules/:key`) is kept for internal use only.
- **Lightsail wildcard cert setup runbook** — Lightsail DNS has no Certbot plugin, so wildcard issuance uses `certbot --manual --preferred-challenges dns-01` with AWS CLI hook scripts. The full sequence is documented in the chat history (this session, May 2026). Key pieces: (1) IAM user with policy `lightsail:GetDomain`, `lightsail:CreateDomainEntry`, `lightsail:DeleteDomainEntry` on the domain, (2) AWS CLI v2 + credentials on the server, (3) `/etc/letsencrypt/lightsail-auth.sh` and `/etc/letsencrypt/lightsail-cleanup.sh` hook scripts, (4) `certbot certonly --manual --manual-auth-hook ... --manual-cleanup-hook ...`, (5) renewal cron at `/etc/cron.d/certbot` running every Monday + Nginx reload hook. **Don't switch to Route53 or Cloudflare** — the user's preference is Lightsail-everything; the hook-script path keeps that. **Don't switch to Caddy on-demand TLS** for the wildcard either — saved for a future round if custom-domain provisioning becomes high-traffic. Re-running `certbot renew` is idempotent; the hook scripts handle the dance with Lightsail DNS each time.
- **Built-in roles are never deleted, but their permissions can be edited** — `tenant_roles.is_builtin = true` for the four seeded roles. The DELETE endpoint refuses to delete them (422). The PATCH endpoint allows full permission edits (so a tenant can take, say, `email_templates: manage` away from their admin role). The role's `key` is immutable in code (used to map legacy `users.role` enum) — the editor doesn't show a key field for built-ins.
- **Permission gate ordering: tenant module switch wins, then role permission** — `requirePermission()` first checks `tenant_modules.is_enabled` for the tenant; if disabled, even an owner is rejected with 403. Only if the module IS enabled does it check the user's role permission level. Same logic in `/api/me` when computing the `permissions` map. So an owner CAN turn off their own access by disabling a module — that's intentional, gives a single switch to neutralise a feature platform-wide for the tenant.
- **Custom roles bypass the legacy `users.role` enum** — when `users.custom_role_id` is set, the auth path resolves permissions from `tenant_roles.permissions`. When it's NULL (default), it maps `users.role` enum → `tenant_roles.key` (matching one of the 4 built-ins) and uses those. The legacy enum stays for backwards compat — don't remove the column.
- **`/api/me` is the SPA's nav-gating contract** — frontend reads `me.permissions[module]` to decide whether to render a nav entry, route guard, or button. After ANY mutation that affects modules or roles (PATCH module toggle, role permission edit), invalidate `['me']` so the UI refreshes. The TanStack Query stale time is 60s by default — without manual invalidation, changes only show up after a minute.
- **Nginx `client_max_body_size` defaults to 1 MB and silently breaks PNG uploads** — the API allows 30 MB via `@fastify/multipart`, but Nginx in front of it caps the request body at 1 MB by default. JPEGs are usually < 1 MB so they slip through; PNGs are typically 2–10 MB and get rejected with 413 before they reach Fastify. Symptom from the operator's perspective: "I can't upload PNG files" (sometimes works for tiny PNGs, never for normal photos). Fix: every server block that proxies to the API must include `client_max_body_size 30M;` (or higher if the API ceiling is raised). `setup.sh` now sets it on the apex block; the wildcard `*.macaroonie.com` server block must have it too. Same trap applies to PDF menu uploads (25 MB API limit) and receipt uploads in cash-recon.
- **Google Fonts v2 returns HTTP 400 for the WHOLE stylesheet if any one requested weight is missing for any one font** — `head.eta` and `FontPicker.jsx` both maintain a `FONT_WEIGHTS` map of which weights each Google Font ships with, and the URL builders request only those weights per-font. Earlier code requested `wght@300;400;500;600;700;800` for every font, which broke whenever a picky font (Caveat, DM Serif Display, Libre Baskerville, etc.) ended up in the list — the entire CSS file failed and ALL fonts (not just the picky one) silently fell back to system defaults. When adding a new font to the picker: add it to BOTH `FONT_WEIGHTS` maps with its actual supported weight set (look up on fonts.google.com under "Styles"). Don't request a weight a font doesn't have.
- **Page builder block partials should use a unique scoped class for any layout that needs media queries** — inline styles can't carry `@media` rules, so a fixed `display:grid; grid-template-columns:repeat(3,1fr)` inline style means the block stays 3-col forever and ends up wider than the viewport on phones, producing a horizontal scroll strip on the right. Pattern used by `menu_inline.eta`, `dish_list.eta`, `reviews_band.eta`, `story_with_stamp.eta`: derive a `_blockId` from `it.block.id`, emit a `<style>` tag with the desktop layout AND `@media` collapse rules keyed on that class, then put the class on the wrapper. Always pair this with `overflow-x: hidden; max-width: 100vw` on `html, body` (in `head.eta`) as the defensive top-level guard so any future block that overflows doesn't leak the strip.
- **Header block has separate desktop + mobile visibility flags** — `mobile_show_logo`, `mobile_show_brand`, `mobile_show_subtitle`, `mobile_show_links`, `mobile_show_cta`, `mobile_breakpoint`. Defaults: logo + brand + CTA on, subtitle + links off, breakpoint 768px. Each emits a `display:none` rule inside `@media (max-width: ${mobile_breakpoint}px)`. Don't conflate `show_logo` (desktop master) with `mobile_show_logo` — both must pass for the logo to render on mobile.
- **Eta JS blocks must NOT contain regex literals with quote chars OR template literals with `${...}`** — Eta's parser tracks quote state naively to find the closing `%>`. A regex like `/^["']|["']$/g` opens a `"` and a `'` that the tracker counts as live string state, never closes them, never finds the matching `%>`, and consumes the rest of the file as JS. Symptom: a 500 with "unclosed string at line N col M" pointing at a JS line FAR below the broken block — usually inside a `<script>` tag that has nothing to do with the cause. Same trap with template literals like `` `family=${x}&display=swap` `` — Eta misreads the `${` as Eta interpolation territory in some configs, or simply gets confused by the quote-balance asymmetry. Fix: use plain `String(x).replace(/^#/, '')` for non-quote regexes, define helper functions outside the block if you need quote-bearing regexes, and use string concatenation (`'a=' + x + '&b=' + y`) instead of template literals inside `<% %>` blocks. Bit us in `widget.eta` (May 2026) — the new top-of-file `<%` block had `replace(/^["']|["']$/g, '')` which broke widget rendering with an error that pointed 170 lines below into the public JS. Reserve template literals for code OUTSIDE Eta blocks (i.e. inside script tags meant for the browser).
- **Eta's `autoEscape: true` HTML-escapes `"` to `&quot;` in `<%= %>` output — silently breaks CSS font-family strings** — if you build a font-family value in JS that contains the quotes (e.g. `const x = '"Poppins", system-ui, sans-serif'`) and emit it via `<%= x %>`, the rendered CSS becomes `font-family: &quot;Poppins&quot;, system-ui, ...` which is invalid. The browser silently falls through to the next font in the stack (usually `system-ui` which looks plausibly correct on most OSes), so every font picker on the Reservations widget LOOKED like it did nothing for weeks. **Fix: use `<%~ %>` (raw, no escape) when the JS value already contains literal CSS-safe quotes.** Affected files were `reservations_widget.eta` at `--r-font-cal`, `--r-font-slot`, and the body `font-family`. The safer alternative pattern (matches what `head.eta` already does) is to keep quotes LITERAL in the template — `font-family: "<%= bareFontName %>", system-ui, ...;` — so escaping only touches the bare font name. Any time you're tempted to build a quoted CSS value in JS and `<%=` it, stop and use `<%~` instead.
- **Eta auto-declares `include`, `includeAsync`, `__eta` AND `layout` at the top of every compiled template** — any `const/let/var layout = ...` (or the same with the other names) inside a block's `<% %>` produces `SyntaxError: Identifier 'layout' has already been declared` and the whole page 500s. Bit us in `gallery.eta` (May 2026) where `const layout = d.layout || 'grid'` collided with Eta's own `function layout(path, data)`. Fix: rename your variable (`layoutMode` is fine), but more generally avoid `layout` / `include` / `includeAsync` / `__eta` as identifiers inside Eta partials. The compiled output is visible in the 500 response body — look for `function layout(path, data)` near the top and your conflicting `const` below.
- **`setAttribute('disabled', false)` STILL DISABLES the button** — browsers treat the presence of the `disabled` attribute as disabled regardless of the value (`'false'` as a string is truthy from HTML's POV). Bit us hard in the reservations widget's `el()` helper (May 2026): an `else if (v != null) setAttribute(k, v)` catch-all ran for `disabled: false`, silently disabling every calendar cell and slot button for hours. The fix: handle `disabled` (and other boolean HTML attributes like `checked`, `readonly`, `required`) explicitly — set the attribute ONLY when the value is truthy. Same idea: in any DOM-builder helper, the catch-all branch must skip falsy values (`v != null && v !== false`) or the boolean-presence trap will catch you again.
- **`/relocate` step 3b does NOT apply the non-adjacent rule to pre-configured combos** — the adjacency check only governs step 3c (automatic expansion). Operator-configured combinations are always eligible as relocation targets regardless of sort-order gaps. If you add a new step that filters combinations before step 3b's break, do not re-introduce the `isAdjacentSet` check on that path.
- **Widget `tryDisplaceCombo` must check free combos as well as free individual tables** — the SQL slot-availability subquery and the JS displacement logic must stay in sync. If you add a new displacement destination type, update both in `widgetApi.js` or slots will show as available but holds will return 409.
- **Do not diagnose 422 errors on `/relocate` as "missing combination" without checking the error message text first** — there are multiple distinct 422 paths (no allocation found, no pre-configured combo, locked booking). The red banner in the Timeline shows the specific message. Jumping to "create a combination" as the fix is only correct for the "No table combination is configured…" message.
- **`overlapPeerMap` (and any other value from a late-declared useMemo) must be bridged via `useRef` if used in a `useCallback` earlier in the component** — TDZ applies to `const` declarations inside React component functions just like anywhere else. Pattern: `const peerMapRef = useRef(new Map())` at the top, `peerMapRef.current = overlapPeerMap` immediately after the useMemo, read `.current` inside the callback. Never add the useMemo's return value to a `useCallback` dep array when the memo is declared later.
- **`manual_mode: true` on `/relocate` skips conflict resolution but still runs allocation (steps 1-4)** — the booking is placed on the best available single table or pre-configured combo for the target. It does NOT skip the "no combination configured" 422 (step 4). To place onto an arbitrary set of tables ignoring all rules, use `PATCH /bookings/:id/tables` directly.
- **The Edit tool can introduce curly/typographic quote characters (U+2018 `'`, U+2019 `'`) as string delimiters** — these are NOT valid JavaScript string delimiters. They cause two distinct failure modes: (a) in JSX/JS files, esbuild throws `Unexpected "'"` at build time; (b) in Eta `<% %>` server-side blocks, V8 throws `Invalid or unexpected token` at render time producing a 500 on every request. In the `<script>` HTML section of Eta templates they don't break server-side compilation but DO break browser-side JS execution. Diagnosis: `python3 -c "data=open('f','rb').read(); [(print(i,l)) for i,l in enumerate(data.split(b'\n'),1) if b'\xe2\x80\x98' in l or b'\xe2\x80\x99' in l]"` — U+2018 is `\xe2\x80\x98`, U+2019 is `\xe2\x80\x99`. Fix pattern for JSX: use double-quoted strings. Fix pattern for Eta templates: Python byte-level global replacement — `data = data.replace(b'\xe2\x80\x98', b"'").replace(b'\xe2\x80\x99', b"'")`. Do NOT use `content.replace(b'\xe2\x80\x98\xe2\x80\x99', ...)` (replaces adjacent pairs only). Replace each byte sequence independently. Bit us in `ReservationsWidget.jsx` (build-time, May 2026) and `reservations_widget.eta` (runtime 500, June 2026) — 153 curly quotes introduced during confirmation-page feature work.
- **`backlog_items` has NO RLS — it is a global platform table, not tenant-scoped** — all backlog operations use raw `sql` (no `withTenant()`), and the route is gated by `requirePlatformAdmin`. Same pattern as `platform_admins`. Never add RLS to it. In contrast, `issue_log`, `feature_requests`, `feature_request_upvotes`, and `changelog_entries` are all tenant-scoped with RLS and must go through `withTenant()` on routes that read/write them.
- **Apify scrape jobs are fire-and-forget BullMQ** — `POST /api/reviews/scrape` enqueues via `reviewScrapeWorker.js` and returns immediately. The worker polls Apify asynchronously. If `APIFY_API_TOKEN` is missing the job fails silently (no crash). Check `GET /api/reviews/scrape-jobs` to see job statuses.
- **JSX attribute strings follow HTML rules, not JavaScript string rules — `\'` is NOT a valid escape** — in `hint='You\'re done'`, the `\` is a literal backslash, `'` terminates the attribute, and the parser sees unexpected tokens. esbuild error: `Expected ">" but found "\"`. Fix: use double-quoted attributes (`hint="You're done"`) or JSX expressions (`hint={'You\'re done'}`). Double-quoted attributes handle apostrophes with no escaping needed. Bit us in `ReservationsWidget.jsx` and `SiteBlockEditors.jsx` (May 2026) on `hint='Override "You\'re booked"...'`.
- **Auth0 access tokens (RS256, custom API audience) do NOT contain `email`** — only the ID token does. `payload.email` in `auth.js` is always `undefined` unless the Login Action explicitly adds it via `api.accessToken.setCustomClaim(ns + 'email', event.user.email)`. Without it, the email-link UPDATE that links an invited user's `auth0_user_id` on first login never fires → `userNotFound = true` → 403 forever. The fix is in the Login Action (add the claim) + auth.js (read `payload[CLAIM_NS + 'email'] ?? payload.email`). Bit us badly (June 2026) — took many sessions to find.
- **`auth.js` user reconciliation MUST use `withTenant()`, not bare `sql`** — the `users` table has RLS enabled. The middleware runs outside any request-scoped tenant context, so a bare `sql` query against `users` returns 0 rows (RLS silently blocks all reads). The email-link UPDATE (`SET auth0_user_id = ... WHERE lower(email) = lower(...)`) also requires `withTenant` or it matches 0 rows even when the invited user row exists. Symptom: every first-login attempt gets "Your account has not been set up for this organisation" (userNotFound = true) despite the row being present in the DB. Bit us (June 2026). Fix: wrap all SELECT and UPDATE against `users` inside `requireAuth` in `withTenant(tenantId, tx => tx\`...\`)`.
- **`req.auth0OrgId` is the CALLING USER's JWT org — not the target tenant's org** — when a platform admin acts on a tenant, their JWT carries the platform org ID (e.g. `org_FgM9cXJbMwp9jAwE` = macaroonie), NOT the tenant's org. Using `req.auth0OrgId` in `team.js` for `inviteUserToOrg` / `removeUserFromOrg` / `sendPasswordResetEmail` sends those Auth0 operations to the platform org, not the tenant. Invited users then get a JWT with the wrong `org_id`, which maps to no DB tenant → "Tenant not found" 403. Fix: always fetch the tenant's Auth0 org from the DB — `getTenantAuth0OrgId(tenantId)` queries `SELECT auth0_org_id FROM tenants WHERE id = $1`. Same applies to any service that acts on behalf of a tenant via Auth0 Management API. Bit us hard (June 2026).
- **`tenants.nav_style = 'launcher'` hides the ENTIRE sidebar tenant-wide, and `nav_items.show_in_launcher` defaults to `false` for every item** — switching a tenant into Launcher mode before an admin has flagged at least one nav item as a quick-access tile leaves every user (including the admin) with no sidebar. The Nav Designer's Sidebar/Launcher toggle is the way back. **Correction (same day):** the escape hatch was first added only to `/launcher`'s empty state, which meant a tenant with even ONE tile configured had no way back — reproduced live in production. Fixed by moving "Back to full menu" into the page header, always visible whenever `nav_style === 'launcher'` and the viewer has `nav_designer:manage`, regardless of tile count. Don't gate a nav escape hatch on any content-dependent condition — it needs to be unconditionally reachable or it isn't an escape hatch.
- **Dashboard's `SHORTCUT_OPTIONS` (the per-user Quick access widget on `/`) is a SEPARATE feature from the Navigation designer's Launcher** — they're both called "quick access" in normal conversation, which is confusing. `SHORTCUT_OPTIONS` is a hardcoded catalog in `Dashboard.jsx` that every user picks their own subset from, always available regardless of `nav_style`. The Launcher (`/launcher`) only exists when `nav_style = 'launcher'`, only shows links an admin flagged in the Nav Designer, and replaces the sidebar rather than living inside the Dashboard page. When a new top-level module ships, remember to add it to `Dashboard.jsx`'s `SHORTCUT_OPTIONS` — this was missed for Food safety, Checklists, and H&S Dashboard for a long stretch.
- **Eta `<% %>` JS string literals need the same apostrophe-escaping as any other single-quoted JS string, but this also applies inside `DataTable`/table-row arrays in Help.jsx and Docs.jsx** — a straight apostrophe inside a single-quoted array-literal string (e.g. `'this week's list'`) terminates the string early and breaks the esbuild parse with a confusing "Expected ']' but found ..." error pointing at the apostrophe, not the actual mismatched quote. Fix: escape it (`'this week\'s list'`) — plain JSX text content (inside `<P>`/`<H3>` etc., not a JS string literal) does NOT need escaping, only literal JS string values do.
- **"Who completed this" fields are plain `text` storing an email, not a `uuid` FK to `users(id)`** — `checklist_instances.completed_by` set the precedent (`req.user?.email`), and `hs_action_log.completed_by`/`created_by` follow it. This was nearly gotten wrong building the Action Log (drafted as a `users(id)` FK before checking the existing convention) — a FK would also be wrong in practice since not every JWT-bearing caller (e.g. a platform admin with no local `users` row) can be resolved to a local user id, while `req.user.email` always exists. Check `checklist_instances` before inventing a new "who did this" column shape.
- **`MediaLibraryModal`'s `onPick` in picker mode passes `(url, item)` — the full media_items row is the second argument, not just the URL** — most existing callers (hero images, gallery pickers) only destructure the first arg because they store a plain URL string on their own record. Any new feature that needs a real FK to `media_items(id)` (rather than a denormalised URL) must take the second argument — see `hs_action_log.attachment_media_id` in `hsActionLog/shared.jsx` for the pattern.
- **postgres.js deserialises `date` columns to JS `Date` objects, not strings — this breaks map-key lookups built from date fields, silently** — `dashboardTiles.js`'s hs-status aggregation builds a lookup key `` `${template_id}|${period_start}` `` from a `checklist_instances` query; without `period_start::text` in the SELECT, the key becomes `` `${template_id}|Mon Sep 21 2026 00:00:00 GMT+0000 (Coordinated Universal Time)` `` instead of `` `${template_id}|2026-09-21` ``, so the lookup against the JS-string period computed by `periodStartFor()` never matches and a genuinely-completed weekly/monthly checklist always shows as incomplete. No error, no crash — just wrong data. This is the same family of gotcha as the existing `slot_time` entry above (composite-type `timestamptz` → JS `Date`), but for plain `date` columns feeding a string-keyed `Map`. Always cast date/timestamp columns to `::text` in the SQL when the JS value is going to be used as a map key or compared to a plain date string — don't rely on the driver's default deserialisation matching what you expect. Caught by writing a direct test against a disposable Postgres before shipping (see the food-safety migration-testing pattern below) — worth doing for any new cross-table date aggregation.
- **`cash_wage_entries.total` is the full wage cost; `cash_wage_entries.cash_amount` is only the cash-paid portion** — the two are independent and legitimately differ (part or all of a wage paid by bank transfer instead of till cash). Any net-cash-position calculation in cash recon must subtract `cash_amount`/`wages_cash_total`, never `total`/`wages_total` — using the full total silently overstates the cash shortfall whenever staff are paid other than in cash. Both wage-total SQL queries in `cashRecon.js` (`week-detail` and `week` routes) return both sums; don't add a third query that only selects one.
- **`cash_wage_defaults` only stores entries with a `staff_id`** — ad-hoc wage entries (typed name, no `cash_staff` row) have no stable identity across weeks, so "Set as default" silently drops them from the saved list. This is intentional, not a bug — surfaced via a `title` tooltip on the button rather than a popup per Standard Design Rule #4. If you ever need ad-hoc entries to persist as defaults too, you'd have to give them a durable identity first (e.g. auto-promote to a `cash_staff` row), not just store the free-text name.
- **`/mobile` is a second route tree, not a responsive mode of the desktop pages** — `AppShell.jsx` (sidebar, ≥1015px tablet chrome) and `MobileShell.jsx` (header-only, phone-portrait) are two separate layouts mounted as sibling top-level `<Route>` trees in `main.jsx`, both inside the same `RequireAuth`/`TenantGate`. Don't try to make an existing desktop page "responsive down" into the mobile experience — build a dedicated component under `src/pages/mobile/` (reusing the desktop page's data hooks/mutations/body components, not its layout) and register it in `src/mobile/registry.js`. `MobileHSDashboard.jsx` is the reference example.
- **The `/mobile` PWA identity swap is a runtime `<link>`/`<meta>` rewrite, not a second build** — there is one `index.html` and one `vite-plugin-pwa` instance (main manifest, scope `/`). `admin/public/mobile.webmanifest` is a hand-written static file (scope `/mobile/`) that ships in every build regardless of which routes a given visitor uses; `MobileShell`'s mount/unmount effect is what actually makes `/mobile` install under a different name/icon than `/`. If you add a new manifest field that needs to differ per surface (e.g. `orientation`), edit the static file directly — there's no shared generator between the two manifests.
- **iOS "Add to Home Screen" reads `<link rel="apple-touch-icon">` + `<meta name="apple-mobile-web-app-title">` from the current page, not the manifest** — pre-17.4 Safari (and many users are still on it) ignores the Web App Manifest for icon/name purposes. `MobileShell`'s manifest-swap effect must also rewrite those two tags, not just `<link rel="manifest">`, or the installed mobile icon/name on iPhone silently stays "Macaroonie" instead of "Macaroonie Ops".
- **A resolved `null` from `useQuery` is not the same as `undefined` — don't collapse them in a guard clause** — `data` is `undefined` while a query hasn't resolved yet, but becomes `null` once it resolves successfully if the API genuinely returns `null` (e.g. `GET .../wages/:week_start` returns `null`, not 404, when no report exists yet for that week). A guard like `if (!wagesData || !config) return` treats "confirmed empty" the same as "still loading" — so logic meant to run on confirmed-empty (auto-populate defaults, show an empty state) never fires. Check `wagesData === undefined` specifically when the success value can legitimately be `null`. Bit us in the Wages page's auto-populate effect.
- **Canvas components under `admin/src/components/website-builder/canvas/` are a second, independent implementation of their matching SSR `.eta` partial — there's no shared renderer** — any conditional-display flag added to a block (a menu-level toggle, a per-block option, a venue setting) must be ported into the canvas version too, or the page-builder's live preview silently drifts from what actually publishes. Caught with `MenuInlineCanvas` (`dataBlocks.jsx`) ignoring `hide_zero_priced_variants`/`hide_unpriced_variants` for a while after `menu_inline.eta` already had them. When adding a new toggle to any menu/website block, grep both the block's `.eta` partial AND its canvas counterpart before considering the work done.
- **Some touch devices never surface their on-screen keyboard for a text/number input — this is a platform limitation, not something `inputMode` can fix** — confirmed on at least one Linux/Chrome tablet tapping a `type="number" inputMode="decimal"` field. Desktop Linux has no standardised "show virtual keyboard on touch focus" contract the way iOS/Android/ChromeOS do, and Chromium's own virtual-keyboard support on Linux is a long-standing known gap. Don't chase OS-specific workarounds — sidestep it with the same custom on-screen keypad pattern already used for covers entry (`NewBookingModal.jsx`'s `IS_TOUCH` + `CoversInput`/`NumericKeypad`) and now temperature entry (`foodSafety/shared.jsx`'s `TempKeypadModal`): render a button instead of a native input on touch devices, open a full-app keypad UI on tap.
- **`boxedLayout.js`'s current tenant step values are a module-level variable, not React Context** — `currentBoxedSteps`, set once per render by `themeResolver.js`'s `setBoxedSteps()`. Deliberate: there is only ever one `<ThemeFrame>` mounted at a time in the admin (the page-builder canvas), so there's no risk of two different tenants' step values colliding. If a second concurrent canvas is ever added, this must become a Context instead.
- **`Menus.jsx`'s save-payload builder whitelists fields explicitly — it does not spread `...item`/`...section`** — the read-side draft hydration DOES spread (`{...s}`, `{...it}`), so a newly-added field flows into local editor state and displays/edits fine, giving the false impression that wiring is complete. But the PATCH-time payload builder (`(draft.sections || []).map(...)` in the save mutation) lists fields one by one; a field left out of that list is silently dropped on every save even though the UI showed it correctly right up until the page reload. Whenever you add a field to `menu_items`/`menu_sections` (or any similarly-shaped whole-tree-upsert page), grep for the explicit save-payload mapping specifically — don't assume the read-side spread means the field is already wired end-to-end. Bit us building `calories`/section `image_url` (caught before shipping, not in production).
- **A structured-data feature can ship years of iteration with zero Help.jsx/Docs.jsx coverage if nobody added a section for it at the time** — the entire Menus feature (migration 048 onward: sections, dishes, variant groups, dietary tags, print, the `menu_inline` website block) had no entry in either file until the `calories`/section-image change added the first one. Don't assume "no doc gap flagged" means "no doc gap exists" — if you're about to document a small change to a feature and can't find its existing section, that's a sign to add a baseline section, not skip documenting because "there's nowhere to put it."
- **A component's own margin and a parent's `space-y-*` are two competing spacing mechanisms — don't use both for the same gap** — `CashRecon.jsx`'s daily-declaration page wrapped its `SectionCard`s in `space-y-0` while `SectionCard` itself carried `mb-4`, apparently on the assumption the two wouldn't conflict. In production the visible gap between stacked cards was zero (colour-matched header/footer accent bars touching directly, no seam) — whatever the exact cause, the practical lesson holds regardless of the precise CSS mechanics: don't split responsibility for one visual gap across a child's own margin AND a parent's spacing utility. Pick one (usually the parent's `space-y-*`, since it's visible at the call site and doesn't require reading the child's internals) and remove the other.
- **`hideHeader` mode on a shared panel can silently drop functionality the full-page mode already has** — `ChecklistRunPanel`'s `hideHeader` mode (used by the H&S Dashboard's checklist widget) re-derives its own compact header affordance from `onStateChange`, which only exposed `markComplete`, not `reopen` — even though the full-page mode's own footer already had a complete, working double-confirm reopen flow. When adding a `hideHeader`/compact-mode branch to a shared component, diff its exposed capability against the full mode's — a host-rendered compact header is easy to under-build relative to what the component itself can already do.
- **`POST` endpoints that require an id the frontend doesn't have in scope will 400 forever, silently, if the catch block is empty** — `POST /:venueId/cash-recon/expenses` required `report_id`, but its only caller (`ExpensesSection.handleAdd()`, which only has `venueId`/`date` as props) never had one to send and sent `date` instead. Zod validation failed on every call; the `catch {}` around it meant no operator ever saw an error, so "Add expense" just silently did nothing from the button's perspective while the row visibly appeared to have been added optimistically nowhere (it wasn't — `setExpenses` inside the `try` block never ran). Caught only when a second, independent caller (the mobile Expenses page) was built against the same endpoint and needed to understand its actual contract. Never wrap a mutating API call in a bare `catch {}` in new code — log the error at minimum, ideally surface it, so a broken contract like this doesn't go unnoticed indefinitely.
- **A reconciliation variance's sign convention should match the standard accounting one (Actual − Expected), and it's easy to accidentally write the reverse** — Cash Recon's variance was computed as Income (expected) − Takings (actual) in every one of its three independent implementations (`SpreadsheetView`'s `variance(date)`, the day-view's `const variance`, and `WeekView`'s per-day-card recompute). That reads backwards from what an operator expects: taking in MORE cash than declared showed as a NEGATIVE number, and less showed positive. When adding any "did the actual figure match the expected one" calculation, write it as `actual − expected` from the start and sanity-check the sign against a simple story ("I took in more than I declared — should this be positive or negative?") rather than just whichever order felt natural to type.
- **A value duplicated across 3 independent call sites can each drift differently — `WeekView`'s day-card variance referenced a field, `total_sc_in_takings`, that was never actually in its API response** — `parseNum(undefined)` silently returns `0`, so the missing field just meant "no SC adjustment ever applied here," not a crash or a visible bug — it took a deliberate read of the `/week/:week_start` route (which already computed its own, correctly-signed `variance` field) to notice the client was ignoring it and recomputing its own, wrongly-signed version from partly-nonexistent inputs. When a figure is computed in more than one place from what should be the same inputs, check whether the backend already computed it correctly before re-deriving it client-side — the duplication itself is usually the bug, independent of which copy turns out to have the wrong sign.
- **`cash_payment_channels.type` (`'cash'|'card'|'voucher'|'online'|'other'`) was never actually used to decide what counts toward Net Cash — it only drove the tooltip/icon selection** — every active channel fed `Net Cash`/`Cash to bank` regardless of type, so a venue with a "Card machine" channel had card takings silently inflating its cash total. Fixed with an explicit `counts_as_cash` boolean (migration 097) rather than inferring it from `type` at read time, because an operator may want a channel's cash-contribution to diverge from its display type (e.g. a "Cash app" channel that's technically `type: 'online'` but should still count as cash). Don't assume an existing categorical field already gates a calculation just because the categories would make sense for it — grep the actual formula.
- **Two functions in the same file can share a name with radically different implementations if one is dead code from an old refactor** — `Website.jsx` has both a `ThemeSection({ config })` (~line 1628, unreferenced — no `<ThemeSection` anywhere in the file) and a `BrandThemeSection()` (~line 3413, rendered under the `tenant-brand` nav case) that both edit `theme.colors.*`. Adding a new theme field to the wrong one (the plausible-looking, alphabetically-earlier `ThemeSection`) compiles fine and looks correct in a diff, but has zero effect on the live app. Before editing a form/section component, grep for where it's actually rendered (`<ComponentName`) — don't assume a component that looks like "the" theme editor is the one wired into the nav switch.
- **The 5 shared `--boxed-step-N` CSS variables in `head.eta` are the ONE place the mobile-portrait breakpoint (`max-width: 600px` and `orientation: portrait`) is defined for boxed-inset spacing** — both the theme-level default (`--boxed-pad`) and every per-block `boxed_step` override resolve through these vars, so adding per-step `mobile_value`/`mobile_unit` support once here made every existing per-block override mobile-responsive automatically, with no changes needed in any of the ~19 block partials that reference `boxed_step`. When a per-step-number setting needs viewport-dependent behaviour, prefer resolving through a small set of shared CSS custom properties over recomputing a literal value at each call site — the literal-value approach (what this codebase used before) means every consumer needs its own media query.
- **A form field can display and save correctly for months while being completely unread at render time — always trace the actual render path, not just the save path, before trusting a field is "working"** — the venue-level Identity page's `site_name`/`tagline` fields round-tripped through the API fine (real columns, accepted by Zod, returned on GET) and displayed whatever was typed, giving every appearance of working. But `mergeLocationConfig()` never included them in what it inherited per-venue — `head.eta`'s `it.config.site_name` on a location page always read the TENANT's value, never the venue's own. Caught only by explicitly grepping every reader of `config.site_name`/`config.tagline` after a user asked "why do we need this page" — a save button that works and a field that renders correctly in the admin are necessary but not sufficient evidence that a value actually reaches the public site. When in doubt about whether a per-entity override field does anything, grep its readers, not just its writers.
- **A "defensive" hack that forces one side of a merge to always win can be the more serious bug, not the fix** — `brandTheme.js`'s `mergeThemes()` deep-merged a venue's theme under the tenant's, then explicitly overwrote `colors`/`typography` back to the tenant's values regardless, to stop a stale venue-level theme from silently overriding a template's fonts/colours on the live site (a real bug it was patching). But this meant a venue's theme override was ALWAYS partially ignored (colours/typography) and partially applied (spacing/radii/etc) with no way for an operator to tell which was which from the admin UI — a confusing, half-working state that outlived the original bug it patched. Migration 098's `use_brand_override` boolean replaced both the original bug and its patch with one explicit binary switch (tenant's values in full, or the venue's own in full) — when a per-field "inheritance" model needs a special-case override to stop a specific field from behaving badly, that's usually a sign the per-field model itself is wrong, not that it needs one more exception.
- **A `bg-*/NN` (opacity-suffixed) Tailwind background on a `position: sticky` element lets whatever scrolls underneath it bleed through, visibly** — `TempChecksTable`/`HoldChecksTable`'s sticky Equipment/Station header cell used `bg-muted/50` (50% opacity). On a phone-width viewport, with the table's remaining columns wide enough to require horizontal scroll, the next header's text scrolled to sit partially underneath the sticky cell and showed through as garbled overlapping text ("EquipmentRange"). A sticky element meant to mask content scrolling behind it needs a fully opaque background — reserve opacity-suffixed backgrounds for elements nothing will ever scroll underneath. The sticky *body* cells in the same tables already used solid `bg-background` and never had this problem, which is why the bug only showed in the header row.
- **Auth0's "Types of Users" application setting (Login Experience tab) has three options, and picking the wrong one either reopens public signup or reintroduces the org-picker screen the admin portal's client-side `TenantGate` was built to replace** — `RequireAuth` (`main.jsx`) deliberately omits `organization` from `loginWithRedirect()` so tenant selection happens in our own `TenantGate`/`TenantSwitcherModal`, not via a second Auth0 screen. But whether Auth0 honours that depends entirely on this dashboard setting, which is easy to get wrong because two of the three options look similar: **"Individuals"** disables organization support for the app entirely (`organization_usage: deny`) — this breaks Auth0 organization invitations outright (confirmed live: sending an invite to an email that had signed up under this setting returned a bare "Unprocessable Entity" from Auth0's invitation API). **"Both"** ("users may sign up with a personal account and be affiliated with one or more organizations", `allow`) does support invitations, but also means literally anyone with a Google/email account can complete a normal login with zero organization — they land harmlessly on `TenantGate`'s "No restaurant yet" screen, but a real, standalone Auth0 identity now exists for that email regardless of whether anyone ever invited them. Confirmed live: a fresh, never-invited Gmail account completed a full login and reached the app under "Both". **"Business Users"** (`require`) is the correct setting for this app's invite-only model — an email with zero organization memberships cannot complete a login at all, Auth0 rejects it before the app is ever reached, and normal invitation acceptance (which always supplies an explicit `organization` param from the invite link) is unaffected. The one accepted tradeoff: an account that's a genuine member of 2+ organizations and logs in with no stored tenant preference will see Auth0's own "choose an organization / continue with personal account" hosted screen — Auth0 has no way to resolve the ambiguity itself when we deliberately don't pass `organization`, and there's no dashboard setting to suppress that specific screen while keeping "Require". In practice this only affects platform-admin/multi-tenant test accounts (single-tenant restaurant staff — the overwhelming majority of real users — never hit it, since Auth0 resolves an unambiguous single membership silently). Closing that gap fully would require the app itself to remember the last-used tenant's `auth0_org_id` and pass it as a login hint — deliberately not built, since a stale/revoked hint risks Auth0 hard-rejecting a login that should otherwise succeed, which is a worse failure mode than an occasional picker screen for a handful of internal accounts. Revisit only if a real (non-test) customer account ever legitimately needs 2+ tenant memberships.
- **A page under `AppShell` must reserve left-side space for the fixed collapsed-sidebar hamburger button, or its own title overlaps it below `lg`** — `AppShell.jsx` renders `<button className="fixed top-3.5 left-3.5 z-10 ... lg:hidden">` whenever the sidebar is collapsed (the normal state on any viewport under `lg`, and always on mobile). A page's own header needs `pl-14 lg:pl-<normal>` (see `OrderSheets.jsx`'s `pl-14 lg:pl-4`) so its title doesn't render directly underneath that icon. `Dashboard.jsx`'s Overview header (`px-6 h-14 border-b`, no left-padding override) had this bug for a long stretch — invisible on tablet/desktop where the sidebar is normally expanded, only visible once someone actually collapsed the sidebar or opened the page on a phone. When adding a new top-level page header under `AppShell`, check it against a collapsed sidebar at phone width before considering it done — this is the same class of bug as the sticky-header-opacity entry above (correct on the common path, silently wrong on a less-common one).
- **A tile/card grid built with a fixed `style={{ gridTemplateColumns: 'repeat(N, minmax(Xpx, 1fr))' }}` doesn't degrade at phone width — it either forces `N * X`px minimum (horizontal scroll) or squeezes below the `minmax` floor** — `Dashboard.jsx`'s Overview tile grid had this shape (4 columns, 240px minimum each = 960px+ before anything actually reflows). Fixed by switching to Tailwind breakpoint classes (`grid-cols-1 sm:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-4`) with each tile's configured "span" (1-4 quarters) mapped through a `SPAN_CLASSES` lookup so a 4-wide tile is `col-span-1` on a 1-column phone grid, `col-span-2` (full width) on a 2-column tablet grid, and `col-span-4` only once the grid actually has 4 columns at `lg`. Any admin page with a resizable/spannable tile or card grid should use this same breakpoint-keyed span-class pattern rather than a fixed pixel-based `grid-template-columns`.
- **`MobileSuggestModal` (one-time nudge) and `MobileViewToggle` (persistent floating button) solve different problems — don't conflate them** — the suggestion modal fires once per browser on first phone-portrait detection and is then gone forever (`maca_mobile_prompt_dismissed`); it was never meant to be a recurring way back into `/mobile`, and before `MobileViewToggle` shipped there was no way at all to get from `/mobile/*` back to the full desktop admin short of manually editing the URL (the `MobileShell` header's back-arrow only goes to the mobile hub, not out of `/mobile` entirely). `MobileViewToggle` is deliberately simpler in its detection (`IS_TOUCH && matchMedia('(max-width: 700px)')`, no portrait requirement, no localStorage dismissal) since it needs to be reliably present on every visit, not just once.
- **`DayView` in `CashRecon.jsx` was already mobile-ready — no rework needed to reuse it in `MobileCashUp.jsx`** — its inputs are `flex-1 min-w-0` labels next to fixed `w-28` amount fields (never a wide multi-column grid), it already has its own sticky header with a back button (`onBack` prop), and `ExpensesSection` inside it is the same component `MobileExpenses.jsx` already reuses independently. This is the same "check whether the desktop body already reflows before rebuilding it" step the Order Sheets mobile module needed — DayView is the second confirmed case (Order Sheets was the first). Contrast with `WagesView` in the same file, which does NOT reflow: its 8-column staff grid (Staff/Type/Hours/Rate/Total/Cash paid/Notes/Remove) only survives narrow viewports via `overflow-x-auto` + `min-w-[760px]`, i.e. horizontal scrolling, not real responsiveness — so `MobileWages.jsx` is a genuine rebuild (card-per-entry, Name/Total/Cash-paid only) rather than a reuse, unlike `DayView`. Check both possibilities before assuming either.
- **A narrowed mobile form must still round-trip every field the desktop form has, even ones it never shows** — `MobileWages.jsx` only lets an operator edit `total` and `cash_amount` per staff entry, but its save payload still includes `entry_type`/`hours`/`rate`/`notes` read straight from the loaded entry, unchanged. Omitting a field entirely from the PUT body (rather than passing its current value through) would have silently zeroed it out server-side the next time a mobile save fired — e.g. an hourly entry's `hours`/`rate` would revert to blank the moment someone glanced at Wages on their phone, even though they never touched those fields. Any "focused" mobile rebuild of a richer desktop form needs to pass every field the API expects, not just the ones the mobile UI exposes for editing.
- **A `position: sticky` page header with `z-10`+ can fully hide (and swallow taps on) `AppShell`'s fixed collapsed-sidebar hamburger button, not just visually overlap it** — the earlier "page title sits under the hamburger" bug (Dashboard.jsx, fixed by adding `pl-14`) was cosmetic: both elements were visible, just crowded together, because Dashboard's header had no competing z-index. `CashRecon.jsx`'s page headers are `position: sticky` with their own `z-10`/`z-20`, which (a) creates a new stacking context and (b) ties or beats the hamburger's `z-10`, so the header paints fully OVER the button — it disappears completely and the tap lands on the page's own header instead. This is a strictly worse failure mode than the cosmetic overlap: the user has no way to open the sidebar at all, not even an ugly one. Fixed by raising the hamburger to `z-40` (safely above every in-page header currently in the app) rather than relying on every page to keep its own header below `z-10` forever — a single high z-index on the one thing that must always be reachable is more robust than auditing every page's stacking choices. When adding a new `position: sticky` page header, keep it below `z-40` or the hamburger fix stops covering that page.
- **A pre-existing native `<select>`-styled venue/venue picker on a content page is easy to mistake for a sidebar redesign, especially when the sidebar itself is unreachable** — `CashRecon.jsx`'s `VenueSelector` (an actual `<select>` element, styled to look like a button, switching between VENUES within the current tenant for reconciliation purposes) is completely unrelated to `AppShell`'s tenant-name-in-header switcher (added the same day, switching TENANTS). A user who can't open the sidebar (see the entry above) and then spots "Restaurant Name ⌄" at the top of Cash Recon has no way to tell the two apart from a screenshot alone — worth explicitly asking "which page is this dropdown on?" before assuming a bug report about "the sidebar dropdown" is describing the actual sidebar.
- **A mobile page's venue selector should match its own desktop page's show/hide rule, not a different mobile page's** — `MobileCashUp.jsx`/`MobileWages.jsx`/`MobileExpenses.jsx` all originally gated their venue `<select>` on `venues.length > 1`, copied from the pattern in `MobileHSDashboard.jsx` (which correctly mirrors `HSDashboard.jsx`'s own `venues.length > 1` gate). But `CashRecon.jsx`'s desktop `VenueSelector` is unconditional — it always renders, even for a single-venue tenant. Copying a gating rule from one mobile module to another without checking THAT module's own desktop counterpart produced a real inconsistency: a single-venue tenant saw a venue selector on desktop Cash Recon but not on the matching mobile pages. Fixed by changing the three Cash Recon-family mobile pages to `venues.length > 0` (always show once venues have loaded). `MobileHSDashboard.jsx` was correctly left alone — it already matches its own desktop page. When building a new mobile module, check that specific page's desktop show/hide rules, not another mobile module's.
- **Reusing a desktop component that renders its own header inside a `/mobile` full-page-detail view produces a double header — `MobileShell` renders its own, then the reused component renders its own underneath** — `MobileCashUp.jsx` reused `CashRecon.jsx`'s `DayView` verbatim for the day-detail drill-in, which looked fine on desktop (no competing header there) but on mobile stacked `MobileShell`'s "← Cash Up [logout]" bar directly above `DayView`'s own "← Monday 21 September 2026 … Submit Declaration" bar — two back arrows, two bars, wasted vertical space. Fixed with a small, deliberately minimal mechanism in `MobileShell.jsx`: `useHideMobileHeader()` (a hook the detail view calls to suppress the shell header for as long as it's mounted) plus an exported `MobileLogoutButton` (so the replacement header can fold sign-out back in) — and a matching `hideHeader`/`onStateChange` prop pair added to `DayView` itself (same shape as `ChecklistRunPanel`'s existing `hideHeader`/`onStateChange` pattern), so the reused component's internal status/save/submit state flows up into the host's own single header instead of rendering its own. **`MobileOrderSheets.jsx`'s `OrderDetail` drill-in has the same shape (a reused desktop component with its own header row, no back button of its own, shown under `MobileShell`'s header) and was not audited or fixed as part of this change** — worth checking whether it reads as a double header too before assuming it's fine because it wasn't reported.
- **When passing render-time state from a reused child component up to a host header via a callback prop, pass the state setter directly rather than wrapping it inline** — `DayView`'s `onStateChange` prop is called from a `useEffect` whose dependency array includes `onStateChange` itself; `MobileCashUp.jsx` passes its raw `setDayState` (a `useState` setter, referentially stable across renders) rather than an inline arrow function, so the effect's dependency array doesn't see a "new" callback every render and doesn't refire needlessly. Passing `state => setDayState(state)` instead would have created a new function on every render of the host, causing DayView's effect to refire every render too — not an infinite loop here (the object it computes doesn't feed back into its own inputs), but pure waste. The same applies to `useHideMobileHeader()`'s internal effect: it depends on `setHidden` pulled from a `useMemo`-stabilised context value in `MobileShell.jsx`, not a fresh object literal each render, for the same reason.
- **Wages inputs are never disabled once a report is `submitted`, so any edit there autosaves straight into a 422 and shows "Save failed" with no explanation** — the whole-tree `PUT /:venueId/cash-recon/wages/:week_start` in `cashRecon.js` rejects any save once `report.status === 'submitted'` ("unsubmit first"), but neither `CashRecon.jsx`'s desktop `WagesView` nor (until the mobile "Paid" checkbox work) `MobileWages.jsx` ever disabled their inputs to reflect that — an operator could type into "Cash paid" on an already-submitted week, watch the debounced autosave silently fail, and lose the edit with only a small "Save failed" text as a clue. Fixed on the mobile page: `RowAmountField`/add/remove are disabled and hidden respectively while `isSubmitted`, with a one-line explanation ("Amounts and staff are locked while submitted — unsubmit to change them"). **Desktop `WagesView` still has the original bug** — its `AmountInput`/`TextInput` fields for hours/rate/total/cash_amount/notes and the add/remove controls are not gated on `isSubmitted` at all; see Outstanding items. If you see "Save failed" anywhere in Cash Recon with no other explanation, check whether the report/day is already submitted before assuming it's a network or validation bug.
- **Marking a wage entry "Paid" is a different lifecycle step from editing its amount, and locking both the same way breaks the normal workflow** — submitting a wage report is meant to finalise the computed totals (hours × rate, fixed amounts), but an operator typically ticks staff off as actually paid in cash AFTER that point, sometimes days later. Locking `cash_amount` behind the same submitted-report guard as `total`/`hours`/`rate` (the original behaviour, still true of the desktop `PUT`) meant there was no way to record "paid" once submitted without unsubmitting first — which also re-exposes the locked amount fields to accidental edits. Fixed via a narrow, purpose-built escape hatch (`PATCH .../entries/:entryId/paid` in `cashRecon.js`) that updates only `cash_amount` and explicitly does not check submitted status, rather than loosening the whole-tree `PUT`'s guard. When a "mark as done" action is conceptually decoupled from the data it's attached to, prefer a small dedicated endpoint for it over relaxing a broader lock — the broader lock is usually protecting something else (here, the computed amounts) that should stay protected.
- **An enum of allowed values can live in three places — DB `CHECK` constraint, API Zod schema, and the UI's own list — and adding a value to only two of them looks finished but isn't** — migration 092 added `action_log` to `hs_dashboard_widgets`' `CHECK` constraint and `HSDashboard.jsx`'s `WIDGET_TYPES` offered it in the Add widget picker, but `hsDashboards.js`'s `WidgetBody` Zod `widget_type` enum was never updated, so every attempt to add an Action log widget was rejected with a 400 before it reached the database. When adding a new value to any enum-like column, grep the value's siblings (e.g. `'cooking_checks'`) across `migrations/`, `api/src/`, and `admin/src/` and update every hit.
- **Delivery checks feed the Overview H&S status tiles' "unresolved" count only, never expected/completed** — deliveries arrive ad hoc with no schedule, so there's no expected number to measure against (unlike fridge/hold/cooking checks, which have configured capture times/sessions). `computeHsStatus()` in `dashboardTiles.js` counts a delivery as unresolved when it failed any check (`NOT (packaging_ok AND damage_ok AND quality_ok AND temp_ok AND accepted)`) and has no `corrective_action`, and adds a `delivery` category (with a `summary` string, "3 logged · 1 issue") only on days with at least one delivery. Don't add deliveries to expected/completed — any count you'd put there would be invented, and it would dilute the real completion ratio of the scheduled checks.
- **Card-paid expenses (`cash_expenses.paid_by_card`) are excluded from `total_expenses`, so any new expense total must filter them too** — the week-detail route sums only `!paid_by_card` rows into `total_expenses` (card sum returned separately as `total_card_expenses`), and the day view, `SpreadsheetView` and `MobileExpenses.jsx` all filter the same way. A new report, export or tile that sums `cash_expenses.amount` directly without `WHERE NOT paid_by_card` will silently overstate cash spent from the till and understate Cash to bank. VAT is unaffected either way (it is informational only).
- **A page spacer for the floating hamburger must be `max-lg:notouch:pl-14`, not `pl-14 lg:pl-X`** — this corrects the earlier "reserve left-side space" entry above. `pl-14 lg:pl-X` assumed the button only exists below `lg`. Once touch devices got the button at every width, iPad-landscape titles sat under it. Separately, a responsive padding utility on the same element (`sm:px-6` on the Overview header) comes later in Tailwind's CSS than `pl-14` and silently overrides it from `sm` up. Touch devices now get an in-flow burger rail, so the floating button exists only on non-touch screens below `lg`. `max-lg:notouch:pl-14` matches exactly that case, and its `:root:not([data-touch])` selector outranks `sm:px-*`. The `data-touch` attribute is set in `main.jsx` from the same `maxTouchPoints` test as `AppShell`'s `IS_TOUCH`; keep the two in sync.
- **iPad home-screen (standalone) web apps have no Fullscreen API, and iPad Safari only has the webkit-prefixed one** — `el.requestFullscreen?.()` silently did nothing on an iPad, so the H&S Dashboard's Full screen button looked broken. Try `requestFullscreen ?? webkitRequestFullscreen`, listen to both `fullscreenchange` and `webkitfullscreenchange`, and fall back to a CSS pseudo full screen (`fixed inset-0 z-50`) when the API is absent or its promise rejects.
- **A user-configured grid layout must shrink to fit, not reflow to fewer columns** — the H&S Dashboard's `column_count` is a layout the operator deliberately built (e.g. two half-width checklist widgets side by side). An attempt to stop the 4-column grid overflowing on a portrait iPad by computing fewer "visible" columns from the container width made those widgets stack, which the user reported as broken. The fix for overflow is `minmax(0, 1fr)` tracks, which always fit, plus letting wide content scroll inside its own card. Automatic responsive reflow is right for a fixed, app-defined layout (the Overview grid's breakpoint classes). It is wrong for a layout the operator arranged by hand.
- **Cash reconciliation figures have one implementation: `reconCalc()` in `CashRecon.jsx`** — the week grid (`SpreadsheetView`) and every Cash Dashboard balance widget call it, the grid with its override-aware cell reader and the widgets through `useReconWeek()` with saved values. Don't recompute variance, Net Cash or Cash to bank inside a widget or a new report. Add or change the formula in `reconCalc()`, or the dashboard and the Cash Recon page will quietly disagree (the same failure as the three drifting variance implementations fixed earlier).
- **Dashboard grids pack densely, so on-screen order can differ from `sort_order`** — `lib/dashboardGrid.js` uses `grid-auto-flow: row dense` so short widgets fill the space beside a tall one (row spanning). The move up/down controls still change `sort_order`, but a later, shorter widget may render before an earlier, taller one if it fits a gap. This is intended. Don't "fix" it by removing `dense` unless you're also dropping row spanning.
- **A new week's wage entries come from `defaultWageEntries(config)` / `wageEntryForStaff()` in `CashRecon.jsx`, not from per-page code** — `WagesView`, `MobileWages.jsx` and the Cash Dashboard's `cash_week_staff` widget each used to carry their own copy of the "default list, else active roster" fill logic, and all three hard-coded `entry_type: 'fixed'` for the roster fallback. With `cash_staff.pay_type` that would have meant three places to update. Change how a week is filled in the helpers only. Also: any editor that saves through the whole-tree `PUT /wages/:week_start` must carry each entry's saved `cash_amount` over (by entry id), or saving a rate change silently un-ticks everyone the Wages paid widget marked Paid.
- **Dashboard widget `settings` jsonb must be written with `tx.json()`, including inside the `tx(values, ...fields)` PATCH helper** — the widget PATCH maps `settings` through `tx.json()` before building the update object (other fields go in raw). Passing the plain object works by accident in some postgres.js paths and stores a JSON *string* in others, after which `widget.settings?.compact` is always `undefined` and the toggle looks like it never saves. Verified against a throwaway Postgres that `jsonb_typeof(settings)` comes back `object`.
- **A prop gated on the same condition twice in opposite directions renders nothing, silently** — `Dashboard.jsx` passed `headerExtra={editing && <VisibleToButton/>}` while `TileCard` rendered `{!editing && headerExtra}`, so the per-tile role-visibility button could never appear and nobody noticed because nothing errored. When a child conditionally renders a prop, let the caller own the condition (render `{headerExtra}` as given) rather than gating it on both sides.
- **`DashboardPage` configs carry a `useNav` hook, called unconditionally by the engine** — each config (`HS_DASHBOARD_CONFIG`, `CASH_DASHBOARD_CONFIG`) is a module-level constant, so the hook call order is stable. Never build a config inline inside a component or swap configs on the same mounted `DashboardPage`, since switching `useNav` between renders breaks the rules of hooks. Mount a different page instead.

- **Rota maths has one implementation: `computeRotaWeek()` in `api/src/services/rotaCalc.js`** — the Rota page's pay/tips tables, the dashboard widgets and `fill-wages` all read its output via the API. Don't recompute pay, points or tip shares in the frontend or in a new report; extend `rotaCalc.js`. The frontend only sums minutes for the grid's hours column, which is display-only.
- **`PUT /rota/.../weeks/:week/entries` replaces the whole week, both modes** — `RotaGrid` sends only the current mode's entries, so saving a week after switching `rota_settings.mode` deletes the other mode's entries for that week (the grid shows a notice before that happens). Any new caller of this endpoint must send the complete week it wants to keep, not a delta.
- **Staff CRUD lives in `routes/rota.js`, not `cashRecon.js`** — the old `/cash-recon/config/staff*` endpoints and Cash Recon's Staff settings tab were removed in migration 106's change. `GET /cash-recon/config` still returns `staff` for the wages pages; after a staff edit, invalidate both `['rota-staff', venueId]` and `['cash-recon-config', venueId]`.
- **"Is the venue open on this date" has one implementation: `resolveOpenDaysForWeek()` in `api/src/services/openDays.js`** — Cash Recon and the Rota both call it. Don't write another closed-day check from `venue_schedule_templates` alone; it would ignore date overrides and exceptions and the two pages would disagree. It returns every template-open day, so the Rota treats a venue with no templates at all as `open_dates: null` (hide nothing) rather than "closed every day".
- **Cash Recon variance adds back cash paid out of the till: day = Takings + cash expenses − (Income + SC adjustment); week = sum of days + `wages_cash_total`** — staff pay expenses and cash wages before counting, so counted cash is what is banked. There is no Net Cash / Cash to bank figure any more. The rule lives in `reconCalc()` and is mirrored in two places that must match: the day view's `const variance` and the `GET .../week/:week_start` route's per-day `variance` (WeekView cards, MobileCashUp, which adds `wages.total_cash_wages` for the week). Cash wages have no day, so a day whose till paid the wages shows short by them and only the week balances. Also: never sum two child tables in one LEFT JOIN + GROUP BY (the old `/week` query did, multiplying income by the number of takings rows); use one subquery per total.
