- **Every whole-list `PUT /cash-recon/wages/:week_start` caller must send `paid_date` for each entry, or saving wipes it** — the PUT deletes and re-inserts every entry. `WagesView`/`MobileWages` send it from their state; the Cash Dashboard's week staff widget carries the saved one over by entry id, same as `cash_amount`. Rota fill-wages updates rows in place and never touches it. `GET .../week` counts wages paid on a day with no daily report as unassigned (week only), because that route has no variance for unreported days; `reconCalc()` instead adds them to that day, so an empty day with wages paid shows a surplus until its takings are entered.
- **Tip moves are rows in `rota_tip_moves`/`rota_tip_move_lines`, not per-person net columns** — `computeRotaWeek({ moves })` sums them; the pay payload keeps `points_adjustment`/`tip_adjustment` on each row for display. Deleting an earlier move can leave someone who passed on what they received with fewer points (floored at 0) or a smaller tip share (floored at 0); the API only checks the giver's balance at the time a move is made.
- **Which tips go to staff is decided by tip pots, not by anything on the Cash Recon SC source** — `cash_sc_sources.distribution` was dropped in migration 108; `cash_sc_sources.tip_pot_id` (set only from Rota setup, `PUT /api/rota/pots/:id/sources`) is the one link. The Cash Recon SC source editor no longer has a distribution/pot field, and its PATCH/POST Zod schemas silently strip unknown keys, so sending `tip_pot_id` through the Cash Recon routes does nothing. A pot's weekly total is the venue's own sources only, even though pots are tenant-wide.
- **The unallocated tip pot is derived, not stored** — `totals.tips_unallocated` is minus the sum of every `rota_week_staff.tip_unallocated` for the venue week, so nothing else may write that column except `tip-nudge` / `reset-unallocated`. Both checks (person has 0.50, pot has 0.50) run inside a `pg_advisory_xact_lock` on the venue week; a new route that changes a person's tip share should take the same lock if it checks a balance.
- **The printed menu's preview is only accurate at full page width** — `menu_print.eta` uses the same page size and padding on screen and in print so its fit check can measure each sheet, but below 800px the columns collapse to one (screen only) and the check is skipped. Never add screen-only padding or sizing to `.page`, or the "fits / too long" result stops matching what prints.
- **Every rota route that returns the pay payload must go through `weekFor()` / `redactWeek()` in `rota.js`, never return `computeWeek()` directly** — the payload mixes wages (`rota_pay`) and tips (`rota_tips`), and a role may have one without the other. `computeWeek()` is only for internal checks (the tip-move balance check, fill-wages). When adding a pay or tip field to `computeRotaWeek()`, add it to `PAY_ROW_FIELDS` / `TIP_ROW_FIELDS` / `TIP_TOTAL_FIELDS` too, or it leaks to people who shouldn't see it.
- **Menu section and dish ids must survive a save — the print designer points at them** — `upsertMenuTree()` deletes and re-inserts the tree, and until migration 121 every save issued new ids, so anything storing a `menu_sections.id` / `menu_items.id` silently lost its link. It now inserts under the ids the client sends, and `Menus.jsx`'s save payload sends `id` for every section and item. A new caller of `upsertMenuTree()` must send ids for existing rows (or accept that links to them break), and must give fresh ids when copying (as `/duplicate` does, remapping `print_layout`). Note the delete still fires `ON DELETE SET NULL` on `fs_cooking_checks.menu_item_id` on every save — keeping ids did not fix that link.
- **The menu designer and the designed print share one renderer — change block output only in `shared/menuLayout.js`** — the designer canvas must never grow its own copy of a block's HTML or CSS, or the design stops matching what prints (the canvas/SSR drift trap from the website builder). Both sides also have to agree on grid maths (`layoutGeometry()`), so the API's `LayoutBody` bounds check and the designer's clamping both call it. The designer loads Tailwind's preflight while the print page does not: any shared CSS rule on an element Tailwind resets (`img`, headings, borders) must set every property it relies on explicitly, and use at least `.ml-page .x` specificity to beat `.ml-page img`-style rules.
- **Nothing outside `api/` or `admin/` existed before `shared/`** — the admin reaches it through the `@shared` alias and `server.fs.allow` in `vite.config.js` (without `fs.allow` the dev server refuses the import, the production build does not), and Node needs `shared/package.json`'s `"type": "module"` or it parses the file as CommonJS. Keep `shared/` dependency-free: neither app installs packages there.
- **A `date` column sent straight to the frontend arrives as `"2026-09-23T00:00:00.000Z"`, and code that appends `'T12:00:00'` to it crashes the whole page** — postgres.js turns `date` into a JS `Date`, which serialises as a full timestamp. The Food safety Deliveries week list did `format(new Date(d.delivery_date + 'T12:00:00'), ...)`, got an Invalid Date, and date-fns threw, blanking the page as soon as a week with a delivery was shown (a week with none never reached that line). Fixed by returning `delivery_date::text` from `GET/POST/PATCH /food-safety/deliveries` and reading it through `deliveryDay()` in `foodSafety/shared.jsx`. Any route returning a `date` column the UI treats as `YYYY-MM-DD` needs the `::text` cast.
- **In the menu designer, grid position to mm is not `x * colMm` once a sheet is folded** — with `print_layout.fold` set, grid lines past the middle are shifted by the fold gap, so block positions must come from `blockStyle()` (which uses `colStart`/`colEnd`/`rowStart`/`rowEnd`) and pointer maths from `colAtMm()`/`rowAtMm()` in `shared/menuLayout.js`. Multiplying by `geo.colMm` / `geo.rowMm` directly puts anything in the right or bottom half off by the gap.
- **A printed page box as tall as the paper gives a blank sheet after every page on a real printer** — `@page { margin: 0 }` only removes the browser's margins; many printer drivers keep a few mm of their own, so the page area is shorter than the paper and a paper-height `.page` / `.ml-page` spills its empty bottom strip onto a new sheet (PDF export never shows it, which is why it slipped through). Size print pages with `100vh` / `100vw`, which Chromium resolves to the printable page area, not with the paper's mm height. The designed print also translates `.ml-content` by half the difference so the design stays where it was placed on the paper. Reproduce by adding `@page { margin: 5mm }` and counting PDF pages.
- **`app.setErrorHandler()` must come before the route plugins** — `await app.register(plugin)` loads the plugin immediately and it keeps the error handler set at that moment, so the handler that used to be set at the end of `buildApp()` never ran for any route: every thrown `httpError` went out as Fastify's default body (`{ statusCode, error: 'Unprocessable Entity', message }`), and the admin (`body.error`) and reservations widget (`data.error`) showed the status text instead of the message; Zod errors came back as 500. Moved above the routes in `app.js`. The handler now also passes through a string `err.code` and an `err.errors` array (web ordering uses both).
- **Web ordering prices have one implementation: `shared/orderPricing.js`** — the server prices every order with it against the live menu and never trusts the browser's totals; the guest page imports the same file only to display them. Items from the ordering API carry prepared `choices` (the raw `variants`/`variant_groups` are stripped by `publicItem()`), so `priceLine()` uses `item.choices` when present; a new pricing helper must do the same (`choicesOf()`) or it prices every dish with options as "no price" in the browser.
- **A payment gateway's `confirmFromClient()` must ask the provider, never trust the request body** — only the `demo` gateway trusts the browser, because no money moves. And `applyPaymentResult()` is the only place an order becomes paid: it is idempotent and refunds a second successful payment for an already-paid order (a retry plus a late earlier attempt) instead of counting it twice.
- **Web ordering tables point at dishes without foreign keys** — `order_items.menu_item_id` and `ordering_item_availability.item_id` are plain uuids because `upsertMenuTree()` deletes and re-inserts the menu tree on every save; a FK would null them (SET NULL) or block the save. Ad-hoc variant option ids in baskets are `v:<label>` for the same reason.
- **Menu layout fonts are CSS variables, never literal family names in `MENU_LAYOUT_CSS`** — every rule uses `var(--ml-body)` / `var(--ml-heading)` / `var(--ml-script)`, set on the page by `pageStyle()` and on a block by `blockStyle()` when it has `opts.font`. A new rule with a hardcoded `'Inter'` ignores the operator's font choice, and a font name that isn't in `FONT_WEIGHTS` is never loaded (the URL builder drops it), so it silently falls back. `.ml-block` sets `font-family: var(--ml-body)` itself because a child inherits the page's already-resolved family, not the variable: without it a block's font override would only reach elements that set their own font-family.
- **The Website builder caches a venue's config as `['website-config', venueId]` — write saved results to that exact key** — nine save handlers in `Website.jsx` did `qc.setQueryData(['website-config'], cfg)`, a key nothing reads, so the server saved but the section kept its stale `initial`, stayed dirty and reverted on revisit: "Save just blinks". They now use `['website-config', cfg.venue_id]`. `invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['website-config'] })` is fine (prefix match); `setQueryData` is exact.
- **TanStack Query v5 callbacks take the query, not the data** — `refetchInterval: (query) => …` reads `query.state.data`; the v4 `(data) => data.some(…)` shape crashed the Reviews page (blank screen, `.some is not a function`). Same family as the removed `onSuccess` on `useQuery` (see the `EmailMonitoring.jsx` item).
- **A nav-seeding migration must not assume the default nav shape** — 122 only added Online orders under a top-level section labelled "Service" and silently skipped tenants who had renamed it; 123 anchors on a route (`/customers`, then `/bookings`, then the end of the top level) instead. Anchor new links on a known route, never on a section label an operator can edit.
- **"Is this H&S check needed today" has one answer: `hsClosedDates()` in `services/openDays.js`** — `/checklists/due` and `computeHsStatus()` both call it, and the admin only shows what `/api/hs-settings` returns. A new H&S report or tile that counts expected checks must go through it too, or it will flag closed days red while the Overview shows them Closed. Keep an unscheduled venue (no `venue_schedule_templates` rows) as open every day, as `openDatesOrNull()` does; `resolveOpenDaysForWeek()` alone would call it closed every day and silently switch off every check.
- **Every page under `AppShell` must bring its own scroll container** — `AppShell`'s `<main>` is `overflow-hidden` (so pages like the Timeline and Bookings can manage their own scroll regions), which means a page whose root is just `<div className="p-6 max-w-4xl mx-auto">` is clipped at the bottom of the screen with no scrollbar. Ten pages had this (Food safety, Checklists, H&S Action Log, Orders, Ordering setup, Reviews, Issue log, Feature requests, Changelog, Backlog). Wrap the page in `<div className="h-full overflow-y-auto">` around the centred content (as `Menus.jsx` does), or use `flex flex-col h-full overflow-hidden` with a `flex-1 overflow-y-auto` body. Don't put `overflow-y-auto` on the `max-w-* mx-auto` element itself, or the scrollbar sits in the middle of a wide screen and the wheel only works over the content column. `/mobile` pages are fine without one: `MobileShell`'s `<main>` scrolls.
- **Menu variant display settings (`menus.hide_unpriced_variants`, `hide_zero_priced_variants`) have four readers, each its own code** — `menu_inline.eta`, the page-builder canvas (`MenuInlineCanvas` in `dataBlocks.jsx`), `shared/menuLayout.js` (designed print) with `menu_print.eta`, and web ordering (`variantRules()` → `itemChoices(item, rules)` in `shared/orderPricing.js`). Online ordering ignored both for a while after launch. A new menu-level display setting needs checking against all four. In ordering, "hidden" must mean removed from `choices`, not just not drawn, so the server's re-pricing rejects the option too.
- **Newsletter consent is only ever given by the guest, and not ticking the box is not an unsubscribe** — `createOrder()` sets `customers.marketing_opt_in` only when `marketing_opt_in` is true and never clears it; only `POST /customers/:id/unsubscribe` and anonymise clear it. Don't add a staff "subscribe" action or a CSV-import opt-in: a newsletter needs the person's own recorded agreement (`orders.consent.marketing`), which staff can't supply. Any new place that collects guest data (the booking widget does not have these boxes yet) should reuse `loadPrivacy()`'s wording and record it the same way, and anonymise must be extended to any new table holding guest details.
- **A single-column grid on a phone must be `minmax(0, 1fr)`, not `1fr`** — `1fr` means `minmax(auto, 1fr)`, so the column grows to its widest child's min-content width. The ordering page's category tab strip (`overflow-x: auto`, many tabs) made the column ~1000px wide on a 390px phone, so the tabs never scrolled, `auto-fill` grids laid out several cards per row, and the site's `html, body { overflow-x: hidden }` clipped everything past the right edge (no margin, no scrollbar). Use `minmax(0, 1fr)` and `min-width: 0` on grid children whenever a child has its own horizontal scroll, and `minmax(min(280px, 100%), 1fr)` for card grids. Check a page at 390px in a real browser (`document.documentElement.scrollWidth` and each scroller's `scrollWidth`/`clientWidth`), since the body's `overflow-x: hidden` hides the symptom from a quick look.
- **The ordering page is a website page, so `/order` renders `page.eta`, not `order.eta`** — the built-in page (`system_key = 'order'`) comes from `bundle.pages`, which on a published site is the snapshot: a site published before migration 126 has no such page in its snapshot and uses `DEFAULT_ORDER_PAGE` until republished. Only `/order/status/:token` still uses each template's `order.eta`. Anything that builds a URL for a page must map `system_key === 'order'` to `/order` (LinkPicker, sitemap, llms.txt), and the `online_ordering` block works only once per page (the app's script finds `#mo-app` by id).
- **`${…}` or `{…}` in plain JSX text is code, not text** — in Docs.jsx/Help.jsx a code sample typed straight into JSX text (`<Mono> `${template_id}|${period_start}`</Mono>`) renders `$` and then evaluates `{template_id}` as a JavaScript expression; the build passes, and the whole page throws `template_id is not defined` and goes blank. The /docs page was blank from 24 Sep until this was found. Put code samples that contain braces in a string expression, `<Mono>{'`${a}|${b}`'}</Mono>`, and check a docs page renders (not just builds) after editing it.
- **A new key inside a theme JSONB object must be added to `ThemeSchema` in `routes/website.js` too, or saving silently drops it** — Zod `z.object()` strips unknown keys, and the whole theme is written back from the parsed result, so the admin shows the value until the refetch after Save, then it reverts. `boxed_steps[].mobile_value`/`mobile_unit` shipped with the editor, the renderer and the canvas all reading them, but never saved. When adding a theme field, check that it survives a save (save, reload the section), not just that it renders.
- **A controlled number input must not clamp `Number(e.target.value)` straight into its value** — `Number('')` is 0, so emptying the field puts 0 back at once and the caret jumps: the operator can't clear it to type a new number. Keep the typed text in local state while focused and pass valid numbers up (see `StepNumberInput` in `BrandLayoutFields.jsx`, `PriceInput` in `components/menus/shared.jsx`).
- **Printing drops CSS background colours unless the element sets `print-color-adjust: exact`** — Chrome's print (and "Save as PDF") leaves backgrounds out when "Background graphics" is off, which is the default, while the on-screen preview shows them. Dietary tag chips, highlighted sections and house favourites printed white until both menu print layouts (`menu_print.eta`, `shared/menuLayout.js`) set the rule on them. Any new coloured element that must print needs it too (both the plain and `-webkit-` forms). Test with `page.pdf({ printBackground: false })`, not a screenshot.
- **A dish's ordering VAT has three levels: dish, section, venue** — `vatRateFor()` in `orderSvc.js` is the only place that decides; pass the section. Don't read `item.vat_rate_*` directly for a price or a report, or section rates are silently ignored.
- **A rota entry's `status` decides pay and points, so a new rota figure must check it** — only `status = 'on'` is worked time (hours, days worked, points, the grid's Week column and On shift row, the who's-on list); `pto` is paid but not worked; `sick`/`uto`/`abs` are neither. Summing every `rota_entries` row (as all rota code did before migration 129) counts time off as work. Use `statusOf()` in `rotaExport.js` (treats a missing status as `on`) in the admin and `computeRotaWeek()` on the server.
- **Chrome's print dialog Margins setting (Minimum / None / Custom) replaces the page's `@page` margin** — only "Default" honours it. A sheet that relies on `@page { margin }` alone prints edge to edge when someone picks Minimum. Give the body its own padding as well (see `rotaHtml()` in `rotaExport.js`).
- **Whether a dish can be ordered at a time is decided by its menus' schedules, via `itemOffAt()` / `menuOnAt()`, and the collection time counts, not the time of ordering** — a dish in several menus is orderable when any of them is on. Any new ordering path (table QR, delivery, POS) must check the basket at its promised time the same way, or it will sell lunch dishes at dinner. Date ranges only clash from today on in `scheduleProblem()`: pass `today` (venue-local), or an old menu that ended last month blocks saving.
- **`overflow-x: hidden` on both `html` and `body` silently kills every `position: sticky` on the page** — body becomes a scroll container that never scrolls (the window does), so sticky elements scroll away like static ones. Tenant sites had this in `head.eta` from the start, so the header block's "sticky" header and the ordering page's basket never stuck. Use `overflow-x: clip` (keep `hidden` before it as a fallback for old Safari). Test a sticky element by scrolling in a real browser and reading its `getBoundingClientRect().top`, not by reading the CSS.
- **The guest ordering page only knows what `publicItem()` sends** — it prices the basket for display with the same `shared/orderPricing.js`, so a field stripped there reads as 0/undefined in the browser with no error: stripping `vat_rate` meant "Includes VAT" never showed. When the guest page needs a new dish field, add it to the item in `loadOrderingMenu()` and check `publicItem()` doesn't remove it.
- **Every discount comes from `applyPromotions()` in `shared/promotions.js`, via `priceBasket()`** — the server (`createOrder()`, quote) and the guest page must call `priceBasket()` with the same `promos` + `promoCtx` (`at` = venue-local time from `venueNow()`/`localParts()`, `venueId`, `chosen`, `unlocked` ids), or the checkout total jumps. Never compute a discount in the page, a report or an email; read `orders.discount_pence` / `order_promotions`. Keep `subtotal_pence` the full price: the minimum order is checked on it, while VAT, tip and the platform fee use the discounted amount.
- **A code promotion's code must not reach the browser until the guest types it** — `GET /order-api/venues/:id` sends only non-code promotions (`publicPromotion()` without `withCode`); `/promo-code` returns the one matching promotion with its code. The Promo block is the deliberate exception (the operator publishes the code by placing it). Don't add codes to the venue payload, the sitemap, llms.txt or any public JSON.
- **Promotion uses are counted from orders, not stored** — a use is an `order_promotions` row whose order isn't rejected / cancelled / expired, so cancelling gives a use back. Any new path that creates orders with promotions (POS, table QR) must go through `createOrder()` or call `lockUses()` inside its transaction, or two guests can take the last use.
- **Promotion discounts that come out fractional per line must go through `settle()` in `shared/promotions.js`** — rounding each line's share on its own took 1p too many off a £15 meal deal (guest paid £14.99). `settle()` rounds the total once and splits it with `allocate()`. Any new kind that spreads a discount over units or lines must use it.
- **A sticky element on a tenant site must sit below the site's own sticky header, and that header's height varies by template and screen** — the ordering page's category bar stuck at `top: 0` and slid under the header block, hiding the search box. Don't hardcode a header height or make operators type one: measure it in the page (`findTopBars()` in `shared/ordering.eta`: sticky/fixed elements at the top, outside the component, wide and short) and re-measure on resize.
- **RLS is not a tenant filter you can rely on: every query must also say `tenant_id = ${req.tenantId}`** — the Promotions editor listed other tenants' venues and promotions in production (and could switch them off or delete them by id) because `routes/promotions.js` and `promoSvc.js` relied on `withTenant()` alone, while every older route filters explicitly (`venues.js`: `WHERE v.tenant_id = ${req.tenantId}`). The app's database role evidently bypasses RLS (table owner / superuser), so `withTenant()` sets the context but doesn't enforce it. A local test as the `postgres` superuser has the same blind spot, which is how it shipped: test with a second tenant's rows in the DB and check they don't appear. Tables without `tenant_id` (e.g. `table_combination_members`) must be joined to one that has it; a lookup by a venue id from the request must check the venue's tenant (see `venueOf()` in `orders.js`). The Customers routes had the same gap (the Customers list showed every tenant's customers) until they were fixed: an older route is not safe just because it's old, so check the filters on any route you touch.
- **Theme role `background` is `--c-bg`, not `--c-background`** — the site and the builder canvas define `--c-bg`, so building `'var(--c-' + role + ')'` from a role name gives an undefined variable for Background and the colour silently falls back. Map roles through a table (`ROLE_VAR` in `shared/headingBlock.js`). `scrolling_text` (`.eta` and canvas) had this bug for its text colour; fixed alongside the Heading block.
- **Anything in the ordering page's basket column has to leave the basket sticky** — the column (`.mo-basket-col`) is what sticks, sized to its content, so stacking the promo box above the basket made the column taller than the screen and cut off Checkout. With promos the column switches to `align-self: stretch` / `position: static` and the basket sticks inside it (`.has-promos`). Anything else added to that column needs the same treatment, and the promo box shows one card at a time so it never grows with the number of promotions.
- **Headings inside a block don't inherit the block's text colour on tenant sites** — `head.eta` sets `section.block h2 { color: var(--c-primary) }` (and templates add their own heading colours), which beats inheritance. The ordering page's "Your basket" title stayed primary on a primary-coloured basket until it got `color:inherit` inline whenever the block sets a basket text colour. Any heading placed on a coloured panel needs an explicit colour.
- **The page builder canvas must take the template and theme from the site, not from the page being edited** — `tenant_site` holds `template_key`/`theme`; a `website_pages` row has neither and a venue's `website_config` has stale copies the live site ignores unless `use_brand_override` is on. Passing the edited record straight to `ThemeFrame` previewed every page but the home page as Classic. Use `brandConfig` in `PageBuilder.jsx`; if `BRAND_OVERRIDE_FIELDS` changes in `siteDataSvc.js`, change the copy in `PageBuilder.jsx` too.
- **Anything that points at a menu row by id needs that row to keep its id across `upsertMenuTree()`** — sections and dishes did since migration 121, footer notes (`menu_callouts`) only from the footer-notes pick: before that every save gave them new ids, which would have silently emptied any designer block that picked notes. A new pointer (designer option, website block) at another menu child table must first make that table insert the client's id, send the id from `Menus.jsx`, and remap it in `/duplicate`.
- **A dish's attached variant group comes in two shapes: `{ options: [{ option_id, overridden, price_pence }] }` from the server (`loadMenuFull()`) and `{ overrides: [{ option_id, price_pence }] }` once edited in `Menus.jsx`** — read its price overrides only through `overridesOf()`. Code that read `g.overrides || []` treated a freshly loaded group as having none, so changing one option's price wiped the dish's other custom prices.

- **A menu section's `visibility` has four website readers and two print readers, each its own code** — website: `loadInlineMenus()` SQL in `siteDataSvc.js`, `MenuInlineCanvas` in `dataBlocks.jsx`, `GET /api/menus/public/:menuId`, `loadOrderingMenu()` in `orderSvc.js` (all show only `visibility = 'show'`); print: the print route's filter before `menu_print.eta` and `buildContext()`/`placementSummary()` in `shared/menuLayout.js` (drop only `hidden`). A new place that lists a menu's sections (llms.txt, JSON-LD, a new block, POS) must apply the same rule, or a "Hide on website" section leaks onto the site. Don't filter hidden sections in `loadMenuFull()` or `loadPrintMenu()`: the Menus page and the designer need them to show the setting and explain a placed block.
- **A menu's print design is `menus.print_design_id`, not the only design** — since migration 135 there can be several `menu_print_designs` rows per menu. Anything that prints or previews "the menu's design" must go through the print route (`?design=` or the menu's chosen one), and code copying a menu must copy every design and remap it (`remapLayout()` in `menus.js`).
- **Food safety setup has one home: H&S settings (`components/hs/HsSetup.jsx`)** — the check panels in `foodSafety/shared.jsx` (used by Food safety, the H&S Dashboard and its mobile version) only record readings. Don't add a gear, "Manage" button or add/edit list back into a panel or the Food safety tabs; add a section to `SETUP_SECTIONS` instead. Fridge check times (`fs_capture_times`) and hold check times (`fs_hold_capture_times`) are separate tables and separate sections on purpose. Setup changes must invalidate the `fs-*` query keys (`FS_KEYS` in `HsSetup.jsx`) so a check page open underneath refreshes.
- **Wages have one editor (`WeekWagesEditor`) and one write route (the whole-list PUT)** — don't bring back a per-entry paid PATCH or a page-specific wages grid. A new wages surface renders `WeekWagesEditor`; a new wage field goes into its `toRow()`/`toPayload()` (and `WageEntrySchema`), or the whole-list PUT drops it on every save from every surface.
- **Rota pay has two parts: `base_pay` (computed or overridden) and adjustments; `pay` is the total** — anything that writes or reports rota pay (fill-wages, a report, a widget) must use `pay`, and anything that shows "what the rota worked out" must use `base_pay`. `carried_owed` is derived from last week's part-paid wage rows at read time, so changing last week's Paid changes this week's pay; after that, fill-wages must be run again for the new total to reach this week's wage row. A new pay figure in `computeRotaWeek()` must be added to `PAY_ROW_FIELDS` too, or it leaks past `redactWeek()`.
- **The platform's own subdomains (office, ops) must stay in `RESERVED_SUBDOMAINS`, and the apex is not a tenant site** — `platformHostKind()` in `services/platformHost.js` is the one check for them in the API, `lib/hosts.js` in the admin. A new platform host (e.g. `status.`) needs adding to both and to `RESERVED_SUBDOMAINS`, or a tenant can claim it as a site address. The apex hook in `routes/platformSite.js` redirects every GET it doesn't know to office., so any new public path that must work on macaroonie.com (a guest link, a webhook) has to be added to `PASS_PREFIXES` there and proxied by nginx.
- **Browser storage and service workers are per origin, so moving the admin to office. resets them once** — the tenant pick, `maca_settings`, `maca_timeline_prefs`, shortcuts and launcher order start from defaults, and Auth0 needs every origin in Allowed Callback URLs / Logout URLs / Web Origins (the SDK uses `window.location.origin`). The old apex install is removed by the self-unregistering `/sw.js` the apex serves; without it a cached admin keeps showing on macaroonie.com instead of the platform page.
- **nginx picks a server block by listen address first, then by name, so a host with no `listen 443` falls to the `*.macaroonie.com` tenant block** — office.macaroonie.com first came up with only `listen 80`, so https went to the wildcard block, which proxies everything to the API: the admin showed the API's JSON 404 (`Route GET:/ not found`). Then `certbot --nginx --redirect` added `if ($host = office…) { return 301 https://… }` inside that block, and once the block also listened on 443 it redirected https to itself. `nginx-office-ops.sh` now writes the whole file itself (separate port 80 redirect block and 443 block, certificate found on disk or from `certbot certonly --webroot`). For any new platform host, don't let `certbot --nginx` edit a block you also add 443 to by hand; check with `sudo nginx -T | grep -n "server_name\|listen"`.
- **`certbot --nginx` installs a new name's certificate into whichever server block matches it, including the `*.macaroonie.com` tenant wildcard block** — running it for office.macaroonie.com before office had its own 443 block put `ssl_certificate .../live/office.macaroonie.com/` into `macaroonie-wildcard.conf`, so every tenant site then served a certificate without its name (browsers and NordVPN flagged them). Check `grep -n ssl_certificate /etc/nginx/sites-available/*` after any certbot run; the wildcard block must point at `live/macaroonie-wildcard/`. Don't use `certbot --nginx` on this server; `scripts/nginx-office-ops.sh` uses `certonly --webroot` and writes the nginx file itself.
- **A website block that needs data loaded before render must be hydrated from every block list the bundle can render** — home `home_blocks`, a location's `page_blocks`, and each `website_pages.blocks` (extra pages and modals, tenant and venue). Only the first two were scanned for `menu_inline` / `gallery` / `reviews_band`, so those blocks on extra pages looked unconfigured on the live site while the builder canvas (which loads data itself) looked fine. A new pre-resolved block type must be added to the loaders' walk and fed all of these. And an SSR partial must never print builder instructions: render nothing when it has no data.
- **Generated test rows are marked `is_generated`; anything that bulk-writes demo data must set it and clear by it** — the H&S generator never overwrites a slot that already has a reading, and completing a checklist someone started leaves that row `is_generated = false`, so Clear can't undo it. Don't clear demo data by date range alone or by a notes marker: that would delete real readings.
- **A wage row's cash due is `total - bank_amount`, not `total`** — since migration 140 part of a week's wage can go by bank transfer. Anything that asks "was this paid in full", "what is left to pay" or "what carries to next week" must subtract `bank_amount` first (`cashDueOf()` / `unpaidOf()` in `WagesTable.jsx`, the carried-owed query and fill-wages' fully-paid check in `rota.js`). Cash Recon variance still uses only `cash_amount`. The bank/cash split of rota pay comes only from `splitPay()` in `shared/payMethod.js`.
- **A new container block type must be added to `SLOT_FIELD` in `blockTree.js` and to every server walker** — the three loaders in `siteDataSvc.js` (`loadInlineMenus`, `loadGalleryItemsForBlocks`, `loadReviewsForBlocks`) and `collectBlockFonts` in `head.eta` walk `data.columns` and `data.tabs` only. A container they don't know renders its children with no menu/gallery/review data and no fonts on the live site while the builder canvas looks fine. Containers never nest: keep the drag-end, `addInColumn`/`insertAfter` and `BlockInserter nested` guards in step.
- **An allergen's level has six menu renderers and they must agree: May contain looks like Contains, Can be removed adds the asterisk** — `menu_print.eta`, `menu_print_dietary.eta`, `shared/menuLayout.js`, `blocks/menu_inline.eta`, `MenuInlineCanvas` (`dataBlocks.jsx`) and `shared/ordering.eta`. Each reads `item.allergen_levels[code]` itself. A new place that draws dish badges must do the same, and must get `allergen_levels` from its loader (it is a separate field from `dietary`, so a loader that only selects codes silently drops every level, and a writer that only sends `dietary` resets every allergen to Contains). Only staff tools (`LevelChip`) draw May contain differently.
- **A tag with `show_on_menu` off must be filtered where guest data is loaded, not in `loadMenuFull()`** — the menu editor and the matrix need every tag. The guest loaders (`loadPrintMenu()`, `GET /menus/public/:id`, `loadInlineMenus()`, `loadOrderingMenu()`) drop hidden tags, and every renderer skips a dish code with no matching tag, so a new guest-facing menu surface must load through one of these or call `menuTags()` itself (as `MenuInlineCanvas` does, since it reads the admin route). The "Can be removed" note must use `hasRemovable(item, shownTags)`, not raw `allergen_levels`, or it shows for a hidden allergen.
- **A dish's dietary tags have two writers: the menu editor's whole-tree PATCH and the Allergen matrix's `PUT /menus/:id/allergens`** — the PATCH rewrites `menu_item_dietary` from whatever the editor loaded, so a menu open in `Menus.jsx` from before a matrix save puts the old tags back when it is saved. The matrix PUT invalidates `['menu', id]`, which only helps a tab in the same browser. Any new place that edits dish tags must write only the dishes it changed (never a whole-menu replace) and invalidate both `['menu', id]` and `['allergen-matrix', id]`.
- **A checklist's completion is derived from its ticks in `PUT /checklists/instance`, never set on its own** — there is no complete or reopen request any more. Anything that writes `checklist_instances` (the H&S test data generator, the legacy import) must set `status = 'completed'` only when every active task is ticked, or the dashboards and Checklists page disagree with what a later save computes. A template gaining a new task does not reopen existing completed instances until their next save.
- **The cooking-check dish list has one source: `loadCookingPicker()` in `services/cookingItems.js`** — menus, the venue's own `fs_cooking_items`, or both, per `fs_cooking_settings.item_source` (no row = menus). Don't query `menu_items` for cooking checks anywhere else (the generator used to), or a venue set to own items gets menu dishes. A reading points at `menu_item_id` or `cooking_item_id`, never both; `dish_name` is the durable record.
- **A tip pot paid to specific people changes everyone's cash, not only the payees'** — `computeRotaWeek()` settles each person's `tip_share` minus what the pots paid them in cash (bank when no shared pot is cash), so tip moves, nudges and any people pot all land in `tip_cash`. Anything that reports or pays tips per person must read `tip_cash` / `tip_bank`, never re-split `pot_shares` by pot method, or a people pot gets paid twice. A negative figure means the person hands money on; don't clamp it to 0 or the totals stop matching the money in the pots. Payees carry forward by week (`rota_week_pot_payees`, latest row with `week_start <=`), so writing a pick for an old week changes that week and every later week without its own row.
- **What the key under a menu lists, and the asterisk note's words, come only from `allergenKey()` with the tenant's allergen notice** — an `allergens` footer note no longer prints its own `title`/`body` (the column is still NOT NULL, so the admin keeps sending the stored title). A new surface that shows menu badges and a key must load the notice (`loadAllergenNotice()`, or `allergen_notice` from `loadMenuFull()`) and pass it to `allergenKey()`; filtering tags by "used on this menu" by hand ignores the operator's ticks and `used_only`, and hardcoding "Can be removed on request" ignores their wording. `in_key` is written only by `PUT /menus/allergen-notice`, which sets it for every tag of the tenant from `key_tag_ids`, so a caller that sends a partial list switches the rest off.
- **Website routes that scope to a location read `venue_id` from the query, and a missing one fails quietly** — `ensureVenueConfig()` returns null without a venue, so `GET /website/menus` returned `[]` and `POST` 404'd for as long as the PDF menus page called them bare; the page looked empty, not broken. Any admin call to a per-location website route (`/menus`, `/gallery`, `/allergens`, `/opening-hours`, `/config`) must pass `venue_id`, and its query key should include the venue so switching locations refetches.
- **Which blocks the page builder offers can depend on the page: `BlockAvailabilityContext` (`canvas/BlockInserter.jsx`)** — `PageBuilder.jsx` provides `hidden` (block keys left out of every `BlockInserter`) and `pdfMenus` (the venue and switch state the PDF menus preview needs). A block that only makes sense when a setting is on goes through this context, not through a filter in each of the inserter's call sites; a block already on a page stays and its SSR partial must render nothing while the setting is off.
- **On the grey page, a bordered element's background comes from the `.border:where(...)` rule in `index.css`, so a bordered element meant to show its coloured parent through must set `bg-transparent`** — the rule makes any element with the plain `border` class and no `bg-*` class white (one-class specificity, components layer: `bg-*` utilities and `hover:`/`disabled:` variants still win). ImageEditor's save-mode toggle on the dark editor needed `bg-transparent`. A responsive-only background (`sm:bg-x`) does not count as "has a background", so it loses below `sm`. A Tailwind colour class that isn't defined in `tailwind.config.js` (as `bg-card` was) generates no CSS but still opts the element out of the rule, leaving it see-through on grey. And `section-head` sets a 2px bottom border: don't add `border-b` to the same element, it resets the width to 1px.
- **An AI tool that changes a booking must call the admin (or widget) route through `callApi()`, be `confirm: true` with a `describe()`, and `audit()` its outcome** — writing bookings with the tool's own SQL skips the route's role checks, emails and timeline push, and a write tool without `confirm` runs the moment the model calls it in a chat, with no Confirm card. Read tools filter `tenant_id` explicitly. Guest tools must never return `manage_token` (the AI would hold the guest's master key); changes go through the emailed code in `ai/codes.js`.
- **Never edit stored `ai_messages` or a conversation's `system_prompt`, and don't put anything that changes (today's date, the venue list) into a system prompt** — the chat replays the stored history to the model unchanged each turn; editing an earlier message or re-rendering the system prompt invalidates the earlier thinking blocks (a 400 on accounts where that is enforced) and breaks the prompt cache. Tools give the date (`list_venues`, `get_restaurant`). New information goes in as a new appended message, as the Confirm notes do.
- **Personal access tokens (`mcp_...`) are accepted only by routes with `config: { allowAccessToken: true }` (the staff MCP) and by in-process calls carrying `INTERNAL_CALL_SECRET`** — don't add `allowAccessToken` to an admin route: a leaked token would then reach the admin API directly instead of only the booking tools. A token stands for its `users` row (role, custom role), so revoking the user (is_active false) also stops their tokens.
- **The Anthropic key comes from `activeKey()` in `src/ai/apiKey.js`, never from `env.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` directly** — a key saved on the Platform page (`platform_secrets`) wins over the env var, so code that reads the env var ignores it, and `chatConfigured()` is async (an unawaited call is a Promise, always truthy, so a "no key" check silently passes). Each PM2 process caches the key for 15 seconds, so a save reaches the other processes within that time; a new place that calls Anthropic must take its key from `activeKey()` per call (or rebuild its client when the key changes, as `chat.js` does), and must never log or return the key.
- **`rescheduleBooking()`'s `overrideLimits` is honoured only with `staff: true`, and the staff AI's move card is a `dryRun` of the same call** — never pass `overrideLimits` from a guest path (manage page, guest tools, widget). If you change what a move checks, change it in `rescheduleBooking()` only: the card and the run both come from it, so a check added elsewhere makes the card promise what the run then refuses. The same goes for a new booking: `newBookingPlan()` in `tools/staff.js` feeds both `describe()` and `run()`.
- **The food safety and action log POST routes default their date to the server's UTC date** (`new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10)`) — around midnight that is the wrong day for a UK venue. Any caller that isn't the admin page (the AI tools, imports) must send `log_date` / `check_date` / `delivery_date` / `logged_date` itself, in the venue's time zone (`localParts()` in `ai/tools/util.js`).
- **`PUT /checklists/instance` replaces every tick, every task note and the instance notes** — a caller that changes a few ticks must send the whole active task list with the current notes (as `tick_checklist` in `ai/tools/hs.js` does), or it unticks everything it left out and wipes the notes.
- **Never store file bytes in `ai_messages`; a stored attachment block carries only `_attachment` and gets its data from `withFileData()` at send time** — the history is replayed on every request, so data in the rows would be re-read (and kept) every turn, and anything that rewrites or re-encodes a stored block breaks the replay and the prompt cache. A new kind of user content block with a marker must be handled in `clean()` (strip / fill it before sending) and in `displayItems()`, or the marker reaches the API (400) or the file never shows in the chat. Files are served and sent only to their uploader (`user_sub`) within the tenant, and an upload is claimed by a message once; keep both checks in any new route that touches `ai_attachments`.
- **A chat turn that Claude rejects because of an attached file must leave nothing behind** — `runTurn()` stores the user message before the first model call, so a 400/413 then would leave a message every later request replays and fails on, locking the chat. `withdrawMessage()` removes it (only while it is still the last row) and frees its files; any new early-failure path after `appendMessage()` (a new validation, a new pre-call check) must withdraw the same way or throw before appending.
- **A person's points have three parts: earned (`base_points`), point moves (`points_adjustment`) and +/- taps (`points_nudge`)** — `computeRotaWeek()` adds all three (floored at 0), and every points pot shares over everyone's points plus the week's spare points (-sum of `points_nudge`). Anything that shows or changes points must use `row.points`, a % of the pot must divide by `totals.points + totals.points_unallocated`, and a display of "what changed" must add `points_adjustment + points_nudge` (the Changed column). Undo all point entries (`reset-moves`) clears only moves; `reset-points-nudges` clears only taps. A new per-person tip field must be added to `TIP_ROW_FIELDS` in `rota.js`, as `points_nudge` is, or it reaches people without `rota_tips` view.
- **Unallocated tips carry forward, so a week's `tips_unallocated` depends on every earlier week** — `carriedIn()` sums all earlier weeks' £ taps and what their spare points were worth; editing an old week (its taps, rota, pots or SC amounts) changes the carry into every later week. Only `computeWeek(..., { carry: false })` may be called from inside `carriedIn()` (else it recurses), and checks that don't need the carry (pay, fill-wages, tip-move balances, point taps) pass `carry: false` to skip the extra work. A new place that pays tips or reports what was paid must use `tips_carry_out` for what is left over, not `tips_unallocated` alone.
- **Every dashboard widget type is listed in three places: the `hs_dashboard_widgets.widget_type` CHECK, `WIDGET_MODULE` in `routes/hsDashboards.js`, and `WIDGET_TYPES` in `components/dashboards/widgetTypes.jsx` (with its `module` and `nav`)** — this corrects the earlier "Adding a widget type means three places" note (`WIDGET_TYPES_BY_KIND`, `CASH_WIDGET_TYPES` + `renderCashWidget`), which predates migration 152. A type left out of `WIDGET_MODULE` is rejected by every mount; one whose admin `module` differs from the API's is offered in Add widget and then refused with a 403. Since any dashboard can hold any widget, a widget body must cope with every navigator mode: read only `ctx.date` (H&S) or `ctx.weekStart` + `ctx.selectedDay` (cash, rota) and never assume which page it is on.
- **`DashboardPage` configs no longer carry `useNav`, `widgetTypes` or `renderWidget`** — this corrects the earlier "configs carry a `useNav` hook" entry. The engine calls `useDashboardNav()` itself and takes widgets from the shared catalog; a config only picks `apiBase`, `keyPrefix`, `primaryGroup`, `defaultNav` and texts. Don't add a per-page widget list back: it would make that page's dashboards reject or hide widgets the other pages can add, which is exactly what migration 152 removed.
- **Showing a dashboard widget is gated twice: the API refuses to add a widget without view on its module, and the admin replaces the body with `NoAccess` for a viewer without it** — the second check matters because someone else can add a cash widget to a dashboard an operator without Cash reconciliation can open. Never rely on the widget's own data routes failing quietly (a 403 inside a widget looks like a broken page). A new widget whose body needs a narrower permission than its `module` (as the rota pay/tips gates do) must check it inside the body too.

- **An inventory item has no price: what it costs is its supplier quotes, picked by `chooseQuote()` in `shared/procurement.js`** — venue choice, then the company star, then the cheapest per base unit at that venue's price. Never add a price column to `inv_items` or pick a quote another way in a new report or page, or it will disagree with what an order is placed on. Anything reporting what was actually paid must read `purchase_order_lines` (prices are copied there at ordering time), not today's quotes.
- **Pars and pack sizes are stored in the item's base unit (g / ml / each), never in packs** — `order_list_pars.par_base`, `inv_quotes.pack_base_qty`. Show and enter them in packs through `packsToPar()` / `packBaseQty()`, so changing a supplier or pack size doesn't change the par. An item's base unit can't change while it has quotes or pars (the API refuses).
- **Purchase orders are emailed after their transaction commits** — `POST /orders/:id/submit` creates every PO first, then calls `emailPo()` per supplier, so a failed email never loses an order (the PO stays `to_send` with `send_error`). A new path that creates POs must do the same: never send an email inside the creating transaction.
- **A purchasing AI tool must show only what the purchasing routes return, and change only through them** — the routes strip prices for people without Purchasing setup view (`hideMoney()`, quote views), so a tool that read `inv_quotes` itself, or worked a total out from quotes, would show prices to ordering staff. The tools' cards use `order.show_prices` / `d.show_prices` from the route response before printing any money. The few direct reads in `tools/purchasing.js` (venues, PO number to id, supplier delivery settings, chat attachments) carry no prices and filter `tenant_id`.
- **`place_order` submits the venue's whole draft when Confirm is pressed** — the draft is shared (the Purchasing page autosaves into the same one), and the card shows the draft as it was when the model called the tool. Lines someone else adds before Confirm go too. `order_form` (and `update_order` / `place_order`) also make an empty draft when none is open, the same as opening the list on the Purchasing page; a viewer without Purchasing manage never makes one.
- **Receiving owns the delivery check it writes** — `POST /pos/:id/receive` upserts `fs_delivery_checks` by `purchase_order_id`. Editing that check on the Food safety page doesn't change the purchase order, and booking the PO in again overwrites the check. Use Correct the delivery on the PO.
- **Loyverse figures come only from `summariseReceipts()` / `sumTerms()` in `shared/loyverse.js`, and a fill re-reads Loyverse on the server** — never compute a Loyverse figure in a page or accept amounts from the browser in `/apply`; the preview and the fill would then disagree with each other or with Loyverse. A new figure kind goes into `TOTAL_KINDS` / `REF_KINDS` and `summariseReceipts()` / `termValue()` together, or `isValidTerm()` silently drops it from saved mappings. Receipts are placed by `receipt_date` in the trading day (`day_starts_at`, venue time zone), not by `created_at`.
- **The day `PUT /cash-recon/daily/:date` changes the note only when the body has a `notes` key** — the week grid saves a day without its note. Any other caller that means to clear the note must send `notes: null` explicitly. The PUT also rewrites every expense from the body, so a caller must send `vat_amount` and `paid_by_card` with each expense (the grid didn't, and reset them on every cell save).
- **A transparent `<input type="date">` laid over a styled button must have `onClick={openPicker}` (`lib/utils.js`)** — on a desktop browser the native date input opens its calendar only from its own icon, which is invisible under `opacity-0`, so clicking the button just focused a hidden field and the date looked impossible to change (reported on Purchasing's Place order). Phones and tablets open the picker on tap either way. Any new date-as-button overlay needs the same handler.
- **Who is on a week's rota is all active staff minus `rota_week_staff.removed`, minus people off the rota all seven days (migration 164: past their `left_on`, or in a `staff_rota_off` period), plus anyone with entries** — a removed person with entries always shows (the PUT refuses that combination, but copy and old data can't be ruled out), and one with money that week (pay adjustment, carried owed, tip move, manual pot amount, nudge, people-pot payee) still has a pay row. A new rota surface that lists staff must take them from `GET .../weeks/:week`'s `staff` or the pay payload's `rows`, never straight from `cash_staff`, or it shows people taken off the week. `removed_staff_ids` on the entries PUT is the whole list (absent = unchanged): a caller that sends a partial list puts everyone it left out back on.
- **Cash Recon income, takings and SC amounts can be negative** (a Loyverse day where refunds beat sales; the Zod schemas have no `min(0)`). Code that sums or displays them must not clamp to 0 or treat a minus as invalid; `fmt()` shows −£x. Expenses and wages are still 0 or more.
- **A check changes only through an event and `applyEvent()`; never write `pos_checks`, `pos_check_lines` or `pos_payments` directly** — they are projections of `pos_events`, rewritten by the sequencer after each accepted event. A new check rule goes in `shared/pos/checks.js` only: the till and the server both run it, and a rule that exists on one side only makes every offline change of that kind come back refused (or lets the till show something the server will never accept).
- **A till's PIN check uses the venue's `pos_settings.pin_salt`; changing it, or hashing a PIN any other way than `hashPin()`, locks every person at the venue out of the till** — PINs are unique per venue because the PIN alone finds the person. Anything that changes what a till needs offline (menus, staff, roles, tables, printers, settings) must call `configChanged(venueId)` in `posSetup.js` (or broadcast `pos.config`; menus and promotions do it through the `notifyTillsOnWrite` onResponse hook from `services/posNotify.js`), or open tills keep the old config for up to 10 minutes (their refresh interval). Staff and table edits outside Till setup don't notify yet.
- **Till discounts from Promotions are worked out on the till and stored as a `promotions.set` event** — reports and the server read the stored amounts; never re-run `applyPromotions()` on a closed check, or a promotion changed since would rewrite past takings.
- **A web page can't print to a network printer or reach the venue LAN freely** — no raw TCP from a browser, an `https` page may only call `https`/`wss`, and Chrome asks permission before a public site talks to a LAN address. That's why the POS prints through the venue hub (`hub/`) and why the hub's address and certificate scheme matters (`docs/POS.md` §2.1). Don't plan POS features that assume the till itself talks to printers or other tills directly.
- **A Deliveroo file must go back byte for byte as it came, apart from the cells we fill** — read it with `parseCsv()` (keeps every value as text, reports the line endings) and write it with `toCsv(columns, rows, { eol, trailing })`. Reading a CSV with the `xlsx` library or any parser that types values turns ids like `1e5` or `0123` into numbers and dates into dates, and Deliveroo then can't find the items (item_id must never change). Only `FILL_FIELDS` columns may change; a new column the tool fills must be added there, which also makes it keepable and typeable.
- **An allergen we know about must never be dropped from the Deliveroo file silently** — a tag with no Deliveroo code is an error in `dishValues()` and blocks the CSV download. Don't turn that into a warning or filter unmapped tags out to get a clean file: the dish would show on Deliveroo without that allergen.
- **A new menu, section, dish or footer-note field must also go into `menuSaveBody()` in `routes/menus.js`** — it is the loaded-menu-to-PATCH-body copy used by `/duplicate` and by every AI menu tool, and the PATCH rewrites the whole tree, so a field left out is wiped from the whole menu the next time the assistant changes anything on it (and copies lose it). This is on top of the existing touch points (Zod bodies, `loadMenuFull()`, `upsertMenuTree()`, the Menus page payload). The round trip `menuSaveBody(loadMenuFull())` → PATCH must leave the menu unchanged; check a new field survives it.
- **AI menu tools never write ad-hoc `variants` and never change a variant group** — dish options go only through `variant_groups` with per-dish `overrides` (the Menus page also sends `variants: []`). A choice a group doesn't have is refused rather than added, because adding it would change every dish using that group. Allergens and dietary tags are matched to existing tags only: never create a tag from a name the model read off a photo, or a misread word becomes an allergen on the website, print, ordering and the till.
- **A custom domain reaches the site only through its own nginx block (`scripts/nginx-custom-domain.sh`)** — nginx picks a server block by name, and nothing else names a tenant's domain, so a request for it falls to the default server for the port; on this server that is certbot's port-80 block for the apex, which ends in `return 404`. The guest sees a bare nginx 404 however the app is set up (domain saved, verified, site live). When a custom domain 404s, check `ls /etc/nginx/sites-enabled/macaroonie-domain-*` (or the Domain section's notice) before looking at DNS or the app. The API never issues certificates or runs anything as root; the domain sync on the server does (corrected: this entry first said nothing was automatic). Only say a domain switches on by itself while `server_auto` is true (the sync's `last-run` is fresh).
- **A form that sends a field on every save must not trigger that field's side effects on every save** — `PATCH /website/tenant-site` cleared `custom_domain_verified` whenever the body had `custom_domain`, and the Domain section always sends it, so switching Site is live (same Save) silently took a verified domain offline. Compare with the stored value first (`current.custom_domain`) and only act on a real change.
- **The domain sync owns every `macaroonie-domain-*` nginx block: the database decides** — each run removes the block (and certificate) of any domain no tenant has saved, so a block made by hand for a domain that isn't saved in Website > Domain disappears within a minute. Save the domain first; to keep a block the app doesn't know about, switch the sync off (`/var/lib/macaroonie-domains/disabled`). Only `custom_domain IS NOT NULL` rows count as saved; verification decides only what gets switched on, so a domain whose re-verify fails keeps its block.
- **A script holding a `flock` must close the lock for children that may outlive it** — `exec 9>lock; flock 9` hands fd 9 to every child, and anything that daemonises (nginx started directly, a stray background process) keeps the lock forever, so the next run waits 10 minutes and gives up. `systemctl` on the server hands the start to systemd so it's safe there, but the scripts still pass `9>&-` / `8>&-` to child commands; keep that on any new call. Test shims that start nginx must detach it like systemd (close fds, stdio to /dev/null), or `$(...)` capturing their output never returns.
- **`getent ahostsv6` returns nothing for a name's AAAA records on a server without IPv6** (getaddrinfo's AI_ADDRCONFIG), which is exactly when a stray AAAA record breaks certificate checks. The sync reads AAAA with `getent hosts` (IPv6 first, no ADDRCONFIG) and keeps only addresses with a colon.
- **The API runs as several processes (PM2 cluster, `instances: max`), so in-memory state is per process** — a WebSocket client is connected to one process; `broadcast()` reaches the others only through the Redis relay in `config/ws.js`. Don't send to sockets any other way (e.g. iterating `rooms` from a new module), and don't keep anything else in a module-level variable that another process must see (caches, rate counters, locks): use Redis or Postgres. Test anything realtime with two processes, not one.
- **A till only knows what its last config download held (PINs, staff, menus, prices)** — it gets a new one on a `pos.config` push, on reopening, or every 10 minutes. Anything that changes what the till checks offline must call `configChanged(venueId)` (or broadcast `pos.config`), and a check that fails because the till may be behind (an unknown PIN) should fetch the config and try again, as `staffForPin()` does.
- **A website field that shapes the published site must be in `publishSvc.js`'s content lists, or it only ever works on staging** — production renders from `published_snapshot`, which holds only `TENANT_SITE_CONTENT_FIELDS` / `WEBSITE_CONFIG_CONTENT_FIELDS` / `PAGE_CONTENT_FIELDS`. `use_brand_override` was left out, so a location's own Brand & theme showed on staging and never live. When adding such a field, add it to the list (and to `TENANT_AREAS` in the same file so the publish status names it, else it shows as "Site settings"); existing snapshots lack it until the next publish, and the status compares missing as null.
- **The staging password only covers routes registered inside `siteRendererRoutes`** — its gate is that plugin's own `onRequest` hook (not `fastify-plugin`), so a new route that renders draft website content on a staging host must live in `siteRenderer.js` (or check `req.siteHost.isStaging` and the gate itself), or it shows the draft to anyone. Don't add the hash to `tenant_site` or return `staging_access.password_hash` from any route: every cookie and preview link is an HMAC keyed by it, so knowing the hash is as good as knowing the password.
- **A deploy step that checks the API right after `pm2 restart` must wait for it** — the cluster's processes take a few seconds to listen, so a single check after a fixed `sleep` fails at random while a rerun of the same commit passes. Poll with a time limit (the health check loops for 30 s) and keep every multi-line construct in `deploy.yml`'s script valid when drone-ssh's `script_stop` inserts an exit-code check after each line: a loop on one line is the safest shape.
- **nginx server blocks on the same address must agree on `http2`, or nginx warns "protocol options redefined"** — certbot writes `listen 443 ssl;` while our scripts write `listen 443 ssl http2;`. On nginx 1.24 http2 is a property of the socket, so the warning is harmless, but it hides real warnings in the deploy log; `scripts/ensure-nginx-http2.sh` aligns them each deploy. Keep writing `ssl http2` in new nginx scripts on this server. `http2 on;` (the newer directive) doesn't exist before nginx 1.25.1, which Ubuntu 24.04 doesn't ship.

- **A GloriaFood order reaches us once: store it before anything else can fail, and never delete `gloriafood_orders` rows to tidy up** — GloriaFood has no history to read back, and its poll is a pop (each order is handed over once). `pollVenue()` pops and stores in one transaction and logs what it popped if storing fails; a new caller of `popOrders()` must do the same. Cleanup only clears guest details (`pollAll()`), never the row: the amounts are the only record Cash Recon has.
- **What counts as a GloriaFood sale is decided only by `summariseOrders()` in `shared/gloriafood.js`** (status `accepted`, source not `admin`). A new report, widget or AI tool must go through it (or `countsInRecon()`), or it will include cancelled orders and GloriaFood's menu-preview test orders. Figures must come from the stored pence columns, which `orderFromPayload()` works out once; don't re-derive them from `payload`.
- **A GloriaFood order on the board is not an `orders` row** — its id is a `gloriafood_orders` id, and only `POST /api/orders/gloriafood/:id/status` may change it (`board_status`). Board code must check `isGloriaFood(o)` before calling any own-order action (status, mark paid, refund, promised time), and a new board action needs the same branch.
- **The fill window and mapping editor are shared by Loyverse and GloriaFood (`reconShared.jsx`)** — a change there changes both. A source-specific figure or summary row goes into that source's own `summary` / `notices` props, never into `ReconFillModal` itself; the preview's per-field value key comes from `source.valueKey` (`loyverse` for Loyverse, `source` for GloriaFood).
- **A dashboard's layout controls must check the page's own dashboard module (`config.moduleKey`), never a widget's module or the user's role** — View on a dashboard means use it, Manage means change its layout, and the API mount checks the same key (`moduleKey` in `app.js`). A new `DashboardPage` config without `moduleKey` hides every layout control from everyone but platform admins. A new layout action (a button that calls a dashboard or widget write route) must sit behind `canEditLayout` / `editing`, or View users see it and get a 403. Permission to change data inside a widget is the widget module's business, not the dashboard's.

- **A new route must use module permissions, never `requireRole()` alone** — reads `requirePermission(module, 'view')`, changes `requirePermission(module, 'manage')`, following the file's `PERM` object. `requireRole()` only checks the fixed `users.role`, so it ignores custom roles and the role matrix (a custom role with View could change things, and a Viewer was refused reads). Add `requireRole('admin', 'owner')` only as an extra on top of manage, for actions that undo finished work or move money. Leave a read open (no gate) only when other modules' pages need it (venues, tables, menus, tenant site); gating it breaks pages the person can otherwise use.
- **A new screen that changes data must check `usePermission(module).canManage`** — hide or disable every change control (buttons, inputs, drag handles, clickable rows that open editors) and show `ReadOnlyNotice`, or View users see buttons that fail with a 403. `ReadOnlyFieldset` disables native inputs, selects and buttons only: links, `div onClick`, pointer-drag handlers and `@dnd-kit` sensors keep working inside it and need their own `readOnly` check. Call the hook above any early return (hooks order). The hook only decides what's shown; the API's gate is the real check.
- **A built-in role with an empty `permissions` map means "module defaults", not "no access"** — `resolvePermission()` falls back to the module's default for that built-in key. Code that reads `tenant_roles.permissions[k]` directly (instead of `resolvePermission()`) shows `none` for every module a built-in role hasn't been edited for.
- **postgres.js caches prepared statements per connection, so after changing a column's type the running API can keep failing until it restarts** — the Feature requests list still 500'd after migration 161 changed `user_id` to text, until the process restarted. Deploys restart PM2 after migrating, so production is fine; when testing a type change locally, restart the server before deciding the fix didn't work.
- **Imported GloriaFood orders (`received_via = 'import'`) are records of past days, never live orders** — corrected: they were first kept off the board entirely; now `boardOrders()` takes them only with `includeImported`, which `GET /api/orders` passes for days before today (venue time), and they are view only (the status route 422s them, the board shows no actions or print). The settings tab's latest orders and the Orders received count still say `received_via <> 'import'`, and a new live list, count or chime must too; a list of past orders may include them but must mark them (`imported`) and must not offer actions. An import may only write rows that are still `import` (its ON CONFLICT has that WHERE); a pushed or polled copy of the same order replaces the imported one. Don't relax the `after_live` cutoff in `importExport()` to import "just a few more days": the export's ids may not match the API's, and the cutoff is what stops a day counting twice.
- **Don't put our own button in front of a third-party widget and click theirs for the visitor** — the Guestplan block's "Start closed" mode did that (load Guestplan on our button's tap, find its launcher by `gstpln`/`guestplan` names, `.click()` it) and on the live site the click didn't open the form, so visitors tapped twice; it was only ever tested against stand-in scripts because Guestplan's script isn't reachable from the build environment. A script we don't control decides its own behaviour (what opens, when, inside which frame); embed it as its owner documents, and if its defaults are wrong ask the vendor for a documented option rather than driving its markup.
- **A Workbox `navigateFallback` must point at a page that is in the precache** — `admin/vite.config.js` leaves `index.html` out of `globPatterns` (a cached shell kept booting old deploys), but kept `navigateFallback: '/index.html'`, so the generated `sw.js` called `createHandlerBoundToURL('/index.html')` and threw `non-precached-url` on every worker start (in every admin console from 22 Aug to 14 Oct 2026; the worker still installed, but the routes registered after it never were). It is `navigateFallback: null` now. If `index.html` ever goes back into the precache, the fallback can come back with it, never one without the other.
- **A page crash is caught by `PageErrorBoundary` only while rendering** — errors in event handlers, effects that call async code, and promises don't reach it, so an action that awaits the API must still catch, log (`console.error`) and show a message itself, and must check the reply's shape before rendering from it (`checkImportReply()` in `GloriaFood.jsx`): `request()` in `lib/api.js` returns `null` for a 200 that isn't JSON, which would otherwise show as nothing at all. A new page shell (another `<Outlet>`) needs its own boundary.
- **A blank admin with the address unchanged and nothing in the console is probably the window scrolled, not a crash** — the shell is `h-screen overflow-hidden` and pages scroll in their own panes, so the window should never scroll; if something makes the document taller (an absolutely positioned element with no positioned parent inside a scrolled pane sits at its unscrolled place) and then gets focus or `scrollIntoView`, the whole app leaves the screen and only the grey page shows. Check `window.scrollY` before hunting for errors. Hidden inputs (`sr-only`) are pinned on screen by `index.css`; still give their label or wrapper `relative`. Playwright's `setInputFiles` / file chooser doesn't return focus to the input the way a real file dialog does, so an upload test passes while the real browser jumps: call `input.focus()` after picking.

- **"Is this person on the rota on this day" has one answer: `loadOffDays()` in `routes/rota.js` (migration 164)** — a day after `cash_staff.left_on` or inside a `staff_rota_off` period is off. Don't filter by `left_on` or periods by hand in a new rota route, report or widget, or it will disagree with the grid (e.g. count a leaver in a week they're blocked from). Outside the rota, lists of "current" staff must skip people past their last day themselves: new wage weeks and Add person use `leftBeforeWeek()`, the till's sign-in list `loadTillStaff({ signIn: true })`. The Cash Recon config route returns staff with `SELECT *`, so `left_on` arrives there as a full timestamp string: compare `String(left_on).slice(0, 10)`, never the raw value. And never treat a leaver as inactive: `is_active = false` hides them from every week, past ones included, which is exactly what the leaving date exists to avoid.
- **Anything that shows a menu to guests or devices must load it with `loadMenuVersion()` / `loadMenuVersions()` (`services/menuReleases.js`), never `loadMenuFull()` or its own SQL on `menus` / `menu_sections` / `menu_items`** — since migration 165 those tables are the draft, so a new reader that queries them shows prices and dishes nobody has published (and a reader that drops `is_published` from the version and reads it off the `menus` row instead gets the draft's setting). Editors and admin pickers stay on the tables. A menu with no release isn't live: a reader must treat "no live version" as "not on the menu", not fall back to the draft (staging is the one deliberate draft reader).
- **A field added to a menu row must also be judged by `shared/menuDiff.js`** — `releaseContent()` decides what a release keeps and `contentKey()` what counts as a change. A column that is re-made on every save (like one-off option ids, or `sort_order` renumbered 0, 1, 2) must be left out of the comparison, or every save shows "changes not published" and Publish is offered for nothing; a tenant-wide setting that should stay live must go in `MENU_DROP` and be attached at read time in `withExtras()`.
- **Release rows are immutable and cached by id in each API process** — never UPDATE a release's `snapshot` (or `goes_live_at` of a live one) to fix a menu: publish again, or cancel a scheduled one. A test that moves `goes_live_at` must keep it after every earlier publish, or the older release stays live.
- **A print link from the admin to the draft goes through `useOpenPreview()` (a preview link), not `/api/menus/:id/print`** — the plain print URL is public and shows the live version; opening it from the editor after a save shows the old menu, which reads as "my change didn't save".
- **A menu screen board draws through `shared/screenLayout.js` only, in the player and in the admin designer** — never add block HTML or CSS to `ScreenDesigner.jsx` or `screen/player.js` (the designer may only add placeholders, the selection box and handles), or boards stop looking the same on the screen as in the designer. A new block type goes into `BLOCK_TYPES`, `BLOCK_DEFAULTS`, `renderBlockInner()` and the designer's `BlockPanel`; a new shared file the renderer imports must be added to `PLAYER_LIBS` in `services/screenSvc.js` (and `SHELL_FILES` in `screen/sw.js`), or the player gets a 404 for it and shows nothing.
- **Screen data must come from `screenConfig()` / `designerData()`, which read the live menu versions** — a board that read the menu tables would show unpublished prices on the restaurant's screens. Sections hidden everywhere are dropped in `slimMenu()`, tags through `menuTags()`; a new field a board shows must be added to `slimItem()` / `slimMenu()` or the player never gets it.
- **Sold out is written only by `setSoldOut()` + `announceSoldOut()` (`services/soldOut.js`)** — a route that writes `ordering_item_availability` itself leaves tills and screens showing the dish (no `pos.config` / `screens.sold_out` push) until their next refresh.
- **Playlist times end before their end time (`boardOnAt()`), unlike online-ordering menu times (`menuOnAt()`, end included)** — so back-to-back times (11:00-15:00, 15:00-22:00) never show both boards. Don't swap one function for the other; 23:59 counts as midnight in `boardOnAt()` only.
- **The screens host is a platform host (`screens` in `RESERVED_SUBDOMAINS`, `platformHostKind()` 'screens')** — `routes/screenSite.js` answers every GET on it except `/screen-api`, `/ws`, `/uploads/` and `/api/`, so a new path a screen needs (e.g. a media proxy) must be added to its `PASS_PREFIXES`. A screen's secret is never stored by the API (only its sha256, first on the pairing row, then on the screen); don't add a "show the secret" or re-send it anywhere: a lost device is re-paired with a new code (New device for this screen).
- **A screen's board timing comes from the shared clock, never from when its timer fired** — `playlistPosition(list, nowMs())`, `replayStart()`, transition `currentTime = nowMs() - pos.startMs` and `syncVideos()` all read `nowMs()` (device clock + offset from `/screen-api/time`). Code that starts an animation, a video or a board "now" (`Date.now()`, `play()` from the start) puts that screen out of step with the rest of its wall. A new timed effect on boards must take its start from the clock the same way.
- **A screen on a wall gets its board from the wall, not from `screens.design_id` / `playlist_id`** — `screenConfig()` reads the wall first (`source`). Admin code showing what a screen shows must use `screenView()`'s `shows` (it already does) rather than the screen's own columns, and a screen moved to another venue must leave its wall (the PATCH does it).
- **Uploads over 30 MB need the media upload nginx location** — the API allows 150 MB videos only on `POST /api/media/items/upload` and `/items/:id/replace`, and only because `scripts/ensure-nginx-media-upload.sh` (every deploy) adds a regex location with `client_max_body_size 160M` before each `location /api/`. A new route that takes big files needs adding to that regex, or nginx answers 413 before the API sees it. In the API, `@fastify/multipart`'s global 30 MB limit applies unless the route passes its own `req.parts({ limits })`, and each part still has to be checked against its own type's limit.
- **A Cache API entry can't be a 206** — the screen service worker stores each video whole (status 200) and slices ranges itself. Don't `cache.put()` a ranged response or answer a video request with the whole file when it asked for a range (some players then refuse to seek).
- **Pictures inside an editor canvas must not be natively draggable** — a mouse press on an `<img>` starts the browser's own drag, which fires `pointercancel` and ends a pointer-event move after a few pixels (the block snaps back); on an iPad a press and hold opens the picture menu or the system drag. A canvas that moves blocks with pointer events needs `onDragStart={noImageDrag}` and `user-select: none; -webkit-touch-callout: none` on its blocks (both designers have it). `@dnd-kit` sensors cancel `dragstart` themselves.
- **Don't hide a control with an inline style that a CSS rule is meant to undo** — inline styles beat every selector without `!important`: the page builder's grip handle had `opacity: 0` inline and a `:hover { opacity: 1 }` rule, so it never showed. Put both states in CSS.
- **Every place that takes dropped files must call `preventDefault()` on dragover** — `installDropGuard()` cancels any drop nobody took (dropEffect none), so a drop area that only handles `drop` never receives it. Use `useFileDrop()` / `FileDropArea`, which also ignore non-file drags (text, links) so dragging inside the page keeps working.

- **What the kitchen shows changes only through `shared/pos/kitchen.js`, inside the sequencer** — never write `kds_orders` or `kds_log` directly, and never put a kitchen rule in the screen or the API alone: the screen applies its own taps with the same `applyKitchenEvent()` and a rule on one side only makes every tap of that kind come back refused. A new till event that changes what was sent (like a move) must be added to `KITCHEN_CHECK_EVENTS` and handled in `kitchenForCheckEvent()`, or the kitchen keeps showing the old state. Online orders come in only as server-made events (`submitServerEvent()`): devices are refused `KITCHEN_SERVER_EVENTS`.
- **Kitchen events have no staff member and don't open a trading day** — the sequencer's kitchen branch skips the staff check and the day opening. Code that reads `pos_events` for staff or day figures must skip `kitchen.*` types (or `staff_id IS NULL`), and the Postgres store must write `staff_id ?? null` (an undefined value is a postgres.js `UNDEFINED_VALUE` 500 on every bump).
- **`pos.seq` carries `kinds`; a till pulls only on `checks`** — anything new that broadcasts `pos.seq` for a venue must say which kinds changed, or every till pulls on every kitchen tap.
- **A kitchen dish's way is decided once, when it is sent (`item.route` from `itemRoute()`), never looked up again from the flow** — anything that shows or changes where a dish is must read `item.route`, `item.done` and `flowTimes()` / `nodeState()`, not `cfg.flow`. The flow (`cfg.flow`) only matters in `kitchenForCheckEvent()` / `itemFromOnline()` when items are made, and in the designer. A station missing from `cfg.stations` is passed straight through, so the sequencer's config must hold only active stations and the screen's config the same set, or the two disagree about whether a dish has arrived.
- **A new link filter kind must be added to `FLOW_TARGET_KINDS` + `RANK` + `linkRank()` in `shared/pos/kitchen.js`, the target the item builders pass to `itemRoute()`, the API's id checks in `PUT /kds-flow`, and the designer's `targetName()` / filter panel** — a kind only some of them know is refused on save or silently never matches.
