- **Times compared with a fractional clock must allow for `Date` dropping the fraction** — the screen player's `nowMs()` includes a fractional offset, but `new Date(nowMs())` truncates, so "elapsed since a change" can come out as -0.7 ms when the draw is in the same millisecond; the Order status animation skipped those. Stamp whole milliseconds (`Math.floor(nowMs())`) and treat a tiny negative gap as zero.
- **A `<label>` around a row of buttons presses the first button when its text is tapped** — the label forwards the click to its first labelable child. `Field` in `components/staff/shared.jsx` takes `group` for rows of buttons (Segmented, chips, colour swatches, editors with remove buttons): it renders a div with `role="group"`. Use `<Field group ...>` for anything that isn't a single input or select.
- **A dish's tax has one rule: `taxFor()` in `shared/tax.js`, fed the dish's levels `[dish, section, menu]` and the sale's context `{ venue_id, order_type_id, revenue_stream_id }`** — this corrects the earlier "A dish's ordering VAT has three levels" entry: `vatRateFor()` and the `vat_rate_takeaway` / `vat_rate_eat_in` columns are gone (migration 170). A new place that prices or reports a sale (table QR, delivery, a GloriaFood import into the till) must build its context and call `taxFor()` (or the till's `posTax()`), never read a rate off the dish, or exceptions for order types, locations and streams are silently ignored. Revenue streams decide tax only through exceptions that name them.
- **Changing a check's order type or revenue stream must carry the re-rated lines** — `check.order_type_set` / `check.stream_set` take a `lines` map `{ lineId: { vat_rate, tax_rate_id } }` built by `rerateLines()` on the till, and `applyEvent()` writes it. An event without it leaves every line at the old type's tax. The till computes the rates (it is the one that showed the price), so the server never re-rates on its own.
- **Order types are tenant rows, not keys** — kitchen orders' `type`, flow targets `{ kind: 'order_type', id }`, `orders.order_type_id` and the kitchen screen's filter tabs all hold `order_types` ids. Don't compare with `'eat_in'` / `'takeaway'` anywhere; find a type by id (or, for a default, by name as `fillOrderTypes()` and the Deliveroo settings do), and keep archived types resolvable (old checks and orders point at them).
- **A migration that changes the shape of menu content must rewrite `menu_releases.snapshot` too and blank `content_hash`** — readers load live versions from the snapshots, so a column moved only on the menu tables leaves the live menu in the old shape, and an unchanged hash makes every untouched menu read as "changes not published". Migration 170 rewrote the snapshots in SQL and set `content_hash = ''`; `rehashReleases()` (run by `backfillFirstReleases()` at API start) re-hashes them with `shared/menuDiff.js`.
- **A `<fieldset>` can't be a scroll container in Chromium** — `overflow-y: auto` on it is ignored, so its content spills past a fixed-height parent and can't be reached (the Menus section and dish panels, wrapped in `ReadOnlyFieldset` by the View / Manage change, never scrolled; found when the Tax box sat below the fold). Put the scrolling on a plain `div` (`flex-1 min-h-0 overflow-y-auto`) and the `ReadOnlyFieldset` inside it.
- **Don't key list rows by their label** — two rows can share a name (a revenue stream kept for its old sales next to a new one of the same name), and React then warns and can mix the rows up. Key by id or position (`Rows` in `PosReports.jsx`).
- **A combo's steps, picks and prices have one set of rules: `shared/combos.js`** — a new surface that shows or sells a combo (kiosk, table QR, a report) must go through `resolveSteps()` / `priceCombo()` (via `priceItem()` or `priceLine()`) and show its price with `comboMenuPrice()`. Pricing parts by hand skips conditions (a sauce step left after the fries were swapped), sold out choices and the rule that a part's base options don't change the deal price. Anything that finds a combo's dishes needs the `{ itemsById, sectionItems }` ctx: online it must include dishes switched off for ordering (`combo_parts`), or a combo offering them can't be completed.
- **A combo step's condition may only point at an earlier step** — `resolveSteps()` walks the steps in order and drops picks on hidden steps as it goes, and `cleanCombo()` drops a `when` naming a later or unknown step on save. An import or editor that writes combos must keep that order, or the step silently never shows.
- **The till keeps one modifier per repeated pick; group only to show them** — `selectionFromModifiers()` (Make it a combo) rebuilds the selection from a line's modifiers, so storing "2 x Cheese" as one modifier would lose a pick. Show modifiers through `groupModifiers()` + `modifierText()` and combo parts through `countParts()`. Online order lines are different: their options are display only (the order is priced from the selection), so `priceChoiceSet()` stores a repeat once as "2 x Cheese" at the summed price; never parse those labels back into picks.
- **Automatic combos are discounts, not changed lines** — on the till a `promotions.set` entry with `kind: 'combo'` (`promotion_id` = the combo's item id), online an `order_promotions` row with `promotion_id` NULL and `combo_item_id` set. A report or query that groups discounts by `promotion_id`, or joins `order_promotions` to `promotions` with an inner join, drops combo savings. And combos run before promotions: a new discount engine must take `line_total - comboOff`, as `priceBasket()` does, or a line gets both discounts in full.
- **A new boolean on an option group must be left out of `releaseContent()` when false (`GROUP_FLAGS` in `shared/menuDiff.js`)** — attached groups ride in every release snapshot, so a new column that defaults to false appears in the draft and not in older releases, and every menu reads as "changes not published" until republished.

- **A kiosk is a device, and its orders name the device as their server** — `server_id` / `staff_id` on a kiosk's checks and events is the `pos_devices` id (`kioskStaff()`), not a `cash_staff` id. Anything that names who took an order (reports, the till's X / Z, a new export) must look in the venue's kiosk devices too, or kiosk sales show as "Unknown". The sequencer's `staffById` must include the venue's kiosks, or every kiosk event is refused as an unknown staff member.
- **What a kiosk may do is decided by `kioskProblem()` in `shared/pos/checks.js`, and the order it sends is built by `kioskOrder()` in `shared/kiosk.js`** — never price or tax a kiosk line in the app itself or add an event type the rules don't allow: the till's sequencer would refuse it after the guest has paid. A new kiosk feature that needs another event type (vouchers, a cash machine) must be added to `KIOSK_EVENTS` with its own check.
- **A kiosk order is sent again with the same events, never rebuilt** — `placeOrder()` keeps the last pay-at-counter attempt and resends its events when the guest taps Try again, and the outbox resends a paid card order's events: the server skips event ids it already has and returns the order number for the duplicate. Building the order again (new check id, new event ids) after a lost reply puts the order on the till twice.
- **A design field the kiosk reads must be in `normaliseDesign()`** — the API stores only its output and the kiosk and editor run it again, so a field it doesn't know is dropped on save (and a value it doesn't check can crash the kiosk). Typed words the guest sees also need a `TK.design()` key in `translationSources()` and to be read through the kiosk's `texts.t()`, or they can't be translated.
- **The kiosk preview frame only takes messages from its parent window, and the editor only from its frame** — the editor posts the design to the frame's own origin (taken from its `kiosk.ready`), not the origin it guessed from the link, and checks `e.source` is the frame's window. Don't relax either check to `*` / any sender: the preview link is a signed key to the tenant's menu.
- **Button colours on the kiosk come through `:where(.k-root) button`** — the reset that makes buttons inherit colour is kept at the lowest weight on purpose, so classes like `.k-chip-on` and Tailwind text colours win. A plain `.k-root button { color: inherit }` beats them and left chip text and the reach button invisible.
- **Kiosk sheets close on a click of the backdrop, not on pointerdown** — closing on pointerdown removed the sheet before the click, so the tap fell through to whatever was underneath (the idle warning's own button never got its click). Keep `onClick` on the backdrop, and while the "Still there?" warning shows, only its button or backdrop answers it.
- **A combo's allergens are the union over every dish it can hold (`comboItemIds()`), never "none listed"** — a combo row has no tags of its own, so reading its own `dietary` showed "No allergens listed" for a meal full of them. Use `itemLevels()` (strongest level per tag over all its dishes, with the "Depends on what you choose" line), and on the order page the allergens of what was actually picked.
- **A fieldset doesn't stop a dropped file** — `ReadOnlyFieldset` disables buttons, but a picture area that takes dropped files (`FileDropArea`, `PicturePick`) needs its own `disabled` for View users, as the kiosk editor's `LockedCtx` and the combo editor's `readOnly` pass.
- **Every staff AI tool needs a `section` from `SECTIONS` in `tools/index.js`, a chat label and an Activity label** — the registry throws at start-up without a known section (older files get theirs from `withSection()` by file), and a new area must be added to `SECTIONS` and to the area list in `staffInstructions()` (`prompts.js`), or tool search won't find it from the words staff use. Read tools need a `TOOL_LABELS` line in `chat.js` (else the step shows the raw tool name); change tools need `TOOL_LABELS` + `changeDetail()` in `AiAssistant.jsx` for Activity. Only add a tool to `CORE_TOOLS` when it answers an everyday question: each one is sent in full in every staff chat request.
- **Never answer, strip or rewrite `server_tool_use` / `tool_search_tool_result` blocks** — they are the API's own tool search, answered by the API in the same response. `clean()` must pass them through, `handleToolUse()` must answer only `tool_use` blocks, and the whole assistant message is stored and replayed as returned. A `tool_result` for a `srvtoolu_` id, or a reply missing the search blocks, is a 400 on the next request. `stop_reason: 'pause_turn'` means call again with the conversation as it is, not "finished".
- **A route plugin under `/api/venues/:id/...` must register `requireOwnVenue(param)` (`middleware/venue.js`) after `requireAuth`** — RLS doesn't stop the app's database role (see the RLS entry above), so a venue id from the URL was used as given by `schedules.js`, `venues.js` and `testData.js` (another tenant could close a venue's weekdays or rewrite its booking rules). The hook answers 404 for a venue that isn't the caller's tenant's. A route that takes a venue id in the body or query must check it the same way (`venueOf()` in `orders.js`, `assertVenueOwnership()` in `cashRecon.js`).
- **Online order lookups by id must filter `tenant_id`** — `loadAdminOrder(tx, id, tenantId)` returns null for another tenant's order, and `changeStatus()`, `markPaid()`, `refundOrder()` and `changePromisedTime()` select and update with `tenant_id`. A new order action must do the same, or an order id is enough to read a guest's details or refund them from another restaurant group.
- **A confirm tool whose input defaults to "now" must return `pin` from `describe()`** — a chat card is run up to 30 minutes later from the stored input, and `run()` re-plans, so today / this week / the check due now / "order 12" (a day's number) would resolve again at Confirm: a cash day submitted at 23:55 and confirmed at 00:05 would submit the wrong day. `pin` holds the resolved values (`date`, `week`, `from` / `to`, an id for an order, check time or session, a refund amount); `handleToolUse()` merges it into the stored input and re-validates it, so every pinned value must be accepted by the tool's own schema (ids go through `pickByName()`, which matches ids first). MCP calls `run()` straight away and needs no pin.
- **Anything that writes to a chat's history or settles its cards must run inside `withHold()` (`ai/chat.js`)** — a turn, a Confirm and a Cancel each hold `ai_conversations.running_until`; a new path (another route, a job that carries a chat on, a retry) that appends to `ai_messages` without it can land between a tool call and its result, and every later request in that chat fails. The hold is a lease in Postgres because the API runs several processes: never replace it with in-memory state.
- **A chat with `closed_at` set has had its history edited and must never go to the model again** — erasure (`eraseFromAi()`) replaces text and drops thinking blocks, so a replay would fail or be wrong. `runTurn()` refuses it; any new way of running or continuing a chat (another channel, a job) must check `closed_at` first. Don't add a "reopen" action.
- **New places that keep guest details from AI chats must be covered by `eraseFromAi()` and `cleanUpAi()` (`ai/erase.js`)** — a memory, summary or transcript table (or a new jsonb column on cards / the audit) that isn't scrubbed there leaves an anonymised guest in Pepper's records, and one that isn't cleared there is kept for ever. Erasure must stay inside the anonymise transaction, so it either all happens or none of it does.
- **Every model call for a tenant is counted with `addUsage()` with its `model` and, for staff, its `person`, and checked first with `chatUnitsLeft()` / `personUnitsLeft()`** — a call without `model` is weighted as if it were the default model, one without `person` doesn't count towards (or respect) the person's daily share, and one without the checks can spend past the allowance. The allowance (`monthly_token_limit`) is in units (`usageUnits()`), never compare it with raw token counts.
- **A tool the model fills from a long file or photo needs a batch cap and a way to continue** — the tool input is written in one reply under `MAX_TOKENS` (16,000 in `chat.js`), so a whole menu or price list in one call is cut off (`CUT_OFF`). Keep a cap in the Zod schema (dishes 50, rows 100), say in the description and the prompt how to send the rest, and make a follow-up call work before the first card is confirmed when it builds on it (as `loadForAdd()` does).
- **nginx ensure-scripts that insert before `location /api/` must find their own block in the text since the previous `/api/` block** — `ensure-nginx-media-upload.sh` and `ensure-nginx-ai-timeout.sh` both insert there, so a check in a fixed window before the line stops seeing a block once the other script's lands in between, and the script adds itself again on every deploy (nginx then refuses the duplicate location). A new script inserting at the same place must use the same region check.
- **Migrations must build on an empty Postgres without pg_cron (CI checks every pull request)** — TRUNCATE tables that others reference in one statement with `CASCADE` (separate statements are refused even when the tables are empty), and use an extension a hosted or CI Postgres may lack only inside a `DO` block that tolerates its absence. The live server never re-runs an applied file, so fixing an old migration to build from empty changes nothing there; never change what an applied migration does to existing data.
- **Expected refusals are 4xx and are logged at warn** — `errorHandler()` logs only 500s as errors with the stack, so don't throw `httpError(500, ...)` for a refusal or log refusals at error level in a route, or real failures get lost. A malformed uuid (Postgres 22P02) is answered 404; validate ids with Zod where a 400 is the right answer.

- **Every Pepper model call goes through `callModel()` in `ai/chat.js`, and nothing on its stream listener may throw** — the listener runs inside the SDK's event emit, so an exception there errors the whole stream and fails the step. Don't switch it to the stream's async iterator either: leaving a `for await` early (an error in the loop body) aborts the model call. Send what the browser should see through `emit` / `streamEvents()`, which swallow their own errors.
- **A chat stream opens with its first event, so anything that refuses a message must run before `turn()` emits `start`** — up to then an error is a normal JSON reply (status code, composer keeps the text); after it, it is an `error` event inside a 200, and the composer only puts the message back when the event says `withdrawn`. A new early check belongs before the user message is stored.
- **A chat only moves up a model, and only `modelOf(conv)` decides which** — `upgradeChat()` sets `model_tier = 'main'` for good; the main model reads the everyday model's thinking blocks, the reverse drops them. Never pick a model per request elsewhere. A new model needs a `PRICES` row in `ai/context.js` (else it counts at default ratios) and, if it has no server-side fallback, a match in `NO_FALLBACK()`.
- **A message Claude refuses must not stay in the chat history** — it would be sent again merged with the next message (back-to-back user rows are merged) and most likely refused again, stalling the chat. `turn()` withdraws it when nothing of the turn's reply was stored; a new path that ends a turn on a refusal must do the same.
- **The chat's parents key `ChatView` by conversation id, so it remounts when a new chat gets its id** — anything from a new chat's first turn that must still show afterwards (a notice, an error) goes through the module-level `carried` map in `ChatView.jsx`; component state alone is lost at that moment.

- **Every id in a request body must be checked to be the caller's tenant's, not only the row being changed** — a route that inserts a body id as given (a table's `section_id`, a staff member's `role_id`, a day's `source_id`, an item's option `group_id`) lets one tenant's rows point at another's, and a later join then shows the other tenant's names or prices (a wage default returned another tenant's staff names and pay). Venue ids are refused app-wide by `refuseOtherTenantsVenues` (`middleware/venue.js`); every other kind needs the route's own check (`assertVenueRows()` in `cashRecon.js`, `assertRole()` in `rota.js`, `assertSection()` in `venues.js`, the own-id sets in `upsertMenuTree()`). A child row under a parent named in the URL needs the parent checked against the URL's venue and the tenant (an override's sittings were added under any tenant's override). A route parameter named like a parent (`:venueId`) passing doesn't make its child (`:sid`, `:oid`) the caller's.
- **The API tests empty the database they run against** — `resetAndSeed()` truncates `tenants` (cascading to everything) and the platform tables, and refuses a `DATABASE_URL` whose database name lacks `test`. Never point `npm test` at a real database, and keep that check.
- **A new table or a route that takes ids is only swept once the fixtures make a row for it** — add a step to `fillTenant()` in `api/test/support/fixtures.mjs` that makes the row through the API (so its write is also replayed as the other tenant). A named route parameter whose ids come from one table goes in `KINDS` in `tenant-sweep.test.mjs` (with the real table name: the test fails on a `KINDS` table that doesn't exist). A deliberate cross-tenant change or reference goes in `ALLOWED_CHANGES` / `ALLOWED_REFERENCES` with its reason, never by loosening a check.
- **Kitchen flow links belong to a flow: read `kds_flow_edges` by `flow_id`, never by venue alone (migration 177)** — a venue can have several flows, so a query by `venue_id` mixes every flow's links into one and routes dishes along links from flows that aren't in use. The flow in use comes only from `activeFlowOf(flows, pos_settings.kds_active_flow_id)` (NULL or a deleted flow = the first). A new place that routes dishes or draws the flow must take its links from `loadKitchenConfig()` / `kdsConfig()` (`flow`), or `loadFlow(tx, tenantId, flowId, stations)` for a given flow.
- **A kitchen flow switch only changes where dishes sent after it go** — never re-route `item.route` on kitchen orders already sent, even "to help": a dish half made at the grill would vanish from the grill's screen. `switchFlow()` must keep taking the venue's sequencer lock (`pos:<venue>`), and the Postgres store's `context()` must keep re-reading `kds_active_flow_id` per event, or a switch made during another device's batch lands in the middle of it. A new way to switch (a schedule, Pepper) must go through `switchFlow()` and broadcast `pos.config` so every kitchen screen reloads.
- **A Help page subsection heading (`<H3>`) must be plain text** — the page gives an H3 its anchor id only when its children are a string, and Pepper's `how_do_i` links answers to `/help#<helpSlug(heading)>`; a heading with markup inside (`<H3>Tips {' '}...</H3>`, a `<Mono>` in it) gets no id, so Pepper's link lands at the top of the page. `api/test/pepper-help-briefing.test.mjs` fails on one. Two H3s with the same text link to their section instead, so keep headings distinct. Help sections need `<section id="..." data-help="">` exactly as the others, or the index skips them.
- **A `day_briefing` part must call an existing reading tool's `run()`, never query tables itself** — the parts run as the person asking and rely on each tool's own permission check (a 403 becomes `not_included`). A part with its own SQL would show a role figures it can't see on their page and skip the tenant checks the tools already have. Pass the venue to those tools by id (`v.id`), not its name: two venues can share a name.
- **The page note is information from the browser: only `pageNote()` turns it into words** — it takes the page's name from `ROUTE_CATALOG` and the venue's from the tenant's own venues, never a label the browser sent; keep it that way for anything else the drawer starts sending (a selected booking, a filter). The `_page` block is stored with the message and replayed; never rewrite it later, and never show it as the person's own words (`displayItems()` skips it).
- **Inside Till setup a View user's page is a disabled fieldset, so a control they must still use can't be a `<button>`** — the kitchen flow tabs (`FlowBar`) are `div role="tab"` with keyboard handling for that reason, like the flow designer's link labels. A Playwright `click()` waits forever on an element with `aria-disabled="true"`: tap a locked tab with `{ force: true }` in tests.

- **A kitchen order's source comes from its id, and every place that handles online orders must take GloriaFood ones too** — `web-<uuid>` is an online order, `gf-<uuid>` a GloriaFood one (`sourceOfId()` in `shared/pos/kitchen.js`); a payload can't choose its source. Code that checks `source === 'online'` to mean "not from the till" (screen labels and filters, `onlineMoves()`, reports) misses GloriaFood orders: test for both, or for `source !== 'till'`. A new server-made order kind needs its own id prefix in `isOrderId()`, a source in the `kds_orders_source_check` constraint, and a branch wherever kitchen ready / collected moves the outside order on (`afterKitchen()` in `posApi.js`).
- **Kitchen copies of outside orders must read the stored row by id, after the storing transaction has committed, never the pushed payload** — `kitchenOnGloriaFood()` takes ids from `ingestWithTx()` and re-reads them under a per-venue advisory lock, so an order and its cancellation arriving together (two pushes, two processes) land in that order. Called inside the ingest transaction, the sequencer's own transaction wouldn't see the row; called with the payload, a late accepted copy could put a cancelled order back on the screens.
- **RLS is forced now (migration 176): this corrects "RLS is not a tenant filter you can rely on" above, but not its advice** — every tenant table forces RLS, so a query outside `withTenant()` and outside system mode sees no rows and changes none, silently. Keep filtering `tenant_id` in every query anyway: system-mode code (`systemSql`, `withSystem()`) has no backstop, and an explicit filter makes another tenant's id a clean 404 instead of an empty result the route may misread. A local test as the `postgres` superuser still hides everything (superusers skip forced RLS): run tests as a role that isn't one, as CI does.
- **A path that finds its tenant itself must use system mode, then filter by what it found** — public pages and APIs reached by a slug, token or id, device and webhook routes, MCP and access tokens, auth's membership look-ups, background jobs and sweeps, the platform pages. On the bare `sql` pool they find nothing (a 404 or an empty list, never an error). Keep system mode to the look-up and switch to `withTenant(found.tenant_id, ...)` for the rest where you can. Add such a path to `api/test/system-paths.test.mjs`.
- **A new table with a `tenant_id` must enable and force RLS in its own migration** — `ALTER TABLE x ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY; ALTER TABLE x FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY; CREATE POLICY tenant_isolation ON x USING (tenant_id = NULLIF(current_setting('app.tenant_id', true), '')::uuid OR current_setting('app.bypass_rls', true) = 'on');` (176 only covered the tables that existed then; 177's `kds_flows` is an example). Without it the table has no backstop at all.
- **A new stored secret (an API key, token, password or signing key in a table) must be sealed** — `seal()` it where it is saved, `unseal()` it where it is used, add the column to `SEALED` in `services/vault.js` (so keys saved before the change get sealed at start), keep a hint column for display, and never return it from a route (not even sealed). A column that holds a secret for one provider and plain settings for another (as `provider_domain` did for SES) can't be sealed: give the secret its own column.
- **Comparing a typed key with a stored one needs `unseal()` first** — sealing uses a fresh iv every time, so two seals of the same key never match, and a sealed value never equals the key as typed (the GloriaFood link route compares `body.poll_key !== unseal(before.poll_key)` to decide whether to check the key again).
- **A new staff tool's area must name every module the tool checks in `SECTION_MODULES` (`ai/tools/index.js`)** — the staff connector lists a tool only when the person has View (Manage for a change tool) on one of its area's modules; a tool that checks a module the map leaves out is hidden from people who could use it. Over-including is safe (the tool still refuses), under-including isn't. Start-up throws when an area has no modules or a module key doesn't exist.
- **Anything outside the API that reads tenant tables (server scripts with psql, cron jobs, one-off node scripts) must use system mode: `PGOPTIONS='-c app.bypass_rls=on'` for psql, `systemSql` / `withSystem()` in node** — forced RLS (migration 176) makes the app's own role see no rows anywhere else, silently. The domain sync (`scripts/custom-domains-sync.sh`) read `tenant_site` plainly, got no rows after 176 was deployed, concluded nobody had a custom domain and removed every domain's nginx block and certificate (guests got `NET::ERR_CERT_COMMON_NAME_INVALID`, the wildcard certificate, with HSTS blocking the bypass). A script that deletes what the database no longer lists must also refuse when the list comes back empty in a way that can't be real (the sync exits when `tenant_site` has no rows at all). When adding a migration that tightens access, grep `scripts/`, `api/scripts/` and the workflows for `psql` and bare `sql` before shipping.
- **The kitchen test bench answers the kitchen screen's `/pos-api` calls itself (`Bench.jsx` → `window.kdsBench`, rules in `shared/pos/kitchenBench.js`)** — a new call the kitchen screen's store makes (another endpoint, another push message type) must be added to the bench's `request()` and, when it changes kitchen orders, to `createBench()`, or the screen breaks only on the test page (a 404 from the bench). Anything the store keeps on the device must go through `keep` / `prefsStore()`, never `idb` or `localStorage` directly: on the bench several screens share one browser (and maybe a paired real screen's storage), so a direct write would mix them up.
- **Pepper's model has no clock: the date reaches it only through the `[Now: ...]` line `nowNote()` puts before every staff message** — never put the date in the system prompt (stored once per chat and replayed with the cache), and don't make a tool's date required when today is the obvious default (a required date made the model guess one, then blame "the app's clock"). A new channel that runs the staff model (another screen, a job that carries a chat on) must add the line too; a new hidden block before a message needs a marker that `clean()` strips and `displayItems()` skips, as `_context` is.
- **Anything drawn inside a scaled-down editor canvas shrinks with it: draw thin guides beside it in screen pixels** — the screen designer's grid was a 1px line inside the board, which is `transform: scale()`d to fit (0.2-0.5 usually), so it rendered at a fraction of a pixel and the grid button looked broken for months. Selection boxes, handles, wall guides and the grid sit beside the board and multiply positions by `scale`; only things that must look like the screen (blocks, background) go inside. A style inside that has to stay a fixed screen size divides by `--sd-scale` (`.sd-empty`).
- **A website block colour from `ThemeColourPicker` is a theme role name or `#rrggbb` (its custom swatch)** — every reader must take both: map a role to `var(--c-*)` (background is `--c-bg`) and pass a code through. A reader that only looks up `ROLE_VAR[v]` silently falls back to its default for a custom colour (the footer did until the custom swatch shipped). Add a new colour field's reader to the list in Docs.jsx → Website CMS → Block colours, or pass `allowCustom={false}`. Anything that decides light / dark text from the background must handle a code too (`_lightHex()` in `promo_cta.eta`, `isLight()` in `ordering.eta`).
- **A device runs the copy of the till app its service worker kept until a reload, so a new device kind or a new screen must never assume the copy is current** — the till app decides what a device is from the kind saved at pairing; a copy that doesn't know a kind ran it as a till (a kiosk on the PIN screen). Routing goes through `openOtherApp()` in `pos/src/lib/store.js`: a new kind gets a branch there and in `App.jsx`, and anything unknown waits for an update rather than falling through to the till. New copies arrive through `pos/src/lib/appUpdate.js`; a new full-screen app or mode must register its own quiet moment with `whenSafeToReload()` (or it inherits the till's rule), and pages that must never reload by themselves (previews, the bench) must not call `startAppUpdates()` (`pos/src/main.jsx`). Don't turn the plugin's `injectRegister` back on: two registrations would race, and the plugin's own autoUpdate reload doesn't wait for a quiet moment.
- **Online sales and their tenders have one implementation: `onlineTransactions()` in `services/onlineSalesSvc.js`** — the Transactions tab and its CSV both read it, and the page only shows what the route returns. A new place that lists or totals online sales (a dashboard widget, Pepper, a Cash Recon line) must call it rather than query `orders` / `gloriafood_orders` itself, or it will disagree on which orders count, which day a GloriaFood order is on (the link's trading day, not the calendar day) and which tender it is. A new payment method needs a tender key there (`ownTenderKey()` / `gfTenderKey()`); an unknown one shows as `other:<raw>`, never silently as cash or card.
- **Tills, kitchen screens and kiosks run one build on three addresses, and the address decides the app** — `HOST_APP` in `pos/src/lib/appHost.js` (pos. till, kitchen. / kds. kitchen screen, kiosk. kiosk; any other address takes any kind). Browser storage is per address, so a device paired on one address knows nothing on another: never move a device by copying its secret into a link; use `POST /pos-api/move` (a one-time code in `#pair=`, removed from the address at once). A new device kind needs its own subdomain in `platformHost.js` (`DEVICE_KIND_OF_HOST`, `RESERVED_SUBDOMAINS` in `siteRenderer.js`), `APP_HOSTS` / `APP_NAMES` in `posSite.js`, `HOST_APP` / `SUBDOMAIN` in `appHost.js` and `DEVICE_APPS` in `posApi.js`, or its codes are refused everywhere but a test address. Links the admin makes for a device app (previews, the kitchen test bench, pairing instructions) must use `deviceOrigin(kind)` / `deviceUrl(setup, kind)`, never the till's address.
- **A device's Disconnect clears its secret but keeps its row** — `POST /pos-api/disconnect` / `/screen-api/disconnect` set `secret_hash = NULL` (Not paired; New code / New device pairs it again with its settings); removing it in Till setup (`revoked_at`) is the permanent one. A device must never disconnect with sales or paid kiosk orders still in its outbox (pairing again clears the outbox): the till and kiosk refuse, the kitchen screen warns with the count.
- **A key press that opens a panel and moves focus into it must be `preventDefault`-ed** — Chrome activates a focused button on the same Enter / Space press, so the menu screen player's fifth OK press opened its settings and pressed Close in the same moment (the panel looked like it never opened). Any keyboard shortcut that focuses a button as it opens something needs the same.
- **Who may change an order is decided by `orderActions()` / `assertEditable()` in `routes/purchasing.js` (migration 180), never in the screen** — a draft by anyone with Purchasing manage, a request waiting for approval only by someone with Purchasing approval. The admin shows only the `can_*` flags the API returns. A new order action needs its flag in `orderActions()` and the same check in its route; a new route that changes what is ordered (placing, PO quantities, split, cancel) must use `canApprove`, or request-only staff get round the approval.
- **A purchase order with `amended_at` changed after the supplier had it**: anything that sends or prints POs must go through `poHtml()` / `poPdf()` / `poXlsx()` / `emailPo()`, which say Amended and Split off (`poChangeNotes()`). Never clear `amended_at` when resending; the copy then says it replaces any earlier one, which stays true.
- **The staff connector's OAuth sign-in gives out ordinary access tokens (`ai_access_tokens`)** — so revoking, expiry, read only and the MCP tool list work the same for a signed-in app as for a token made by hand. Don't add a second kind of token for OAuth apps, and keep `/oauth/` and the `.well-known` metadata paths in platformSite.js's `PASS_PREFIXES`, or the apex hook sends apps to the office site. `GET /oauth/authorize` must never redirect to an address the app didn't register (a page instead), and a code must stay single use.
- **A login started on a page other than the Overview must carry `appState.returnTo`** — `RequireAuth` passes it and `onRedirectCallback` in `main.jsx` goes there (a full page load, since the router has already read the address). A new `loginWithRedirect()` call that leaves it out lands the person on the Overview, which broke the connector's Connect page for anyone not already signed in on office.
- **Which stations a kitchen flow uses is its links plus `kds_flows.off_station_ids` (migration 182), never the venue's station list alone** — a designer view, warning, report or Pepper tool that lists "the flow's stations" from `loadStations()` shows stations the flow leaves out; take the flow's stations from its links (as `kdsConfig()`'s `station_ids` does) or drop `off_station_ids`. A route that writes a flow's links must keep refusing links to an out station, or the designer and routing disagree (the box isn't drawn but dishes go there).
- **A kitchen dish's way never names a Balance box once decided: anything that routes dishes must decide boxes with `balanceTicket()` over all the dishes one event sends or fires together, never dish by dish** (corrected: this first named `makeBalancePicker()`, replaced by `balanceTicket()` when boxes could share big tickets) — deciding per dish splits a ticket's wings between two cooks even when the box keeps tickets together, and a route left with a box in it puts a fired dish on every station the box shares between. A new way dishes reach the kitchen (another server event, the hub) must also get `cfg.balance` from the sequencer (open orders + last picks) or every box picks as if the kitchen were empty, always the same station. Lists of stations for kitchen screens use `BUMP_KINDS`, not `kind !== 'board'`.
- **A code a device shows (link code) must never be live in `pos_pairings` and `screen_pairings` at once** — the installer finds a code's device by its hash alone (`findLinkCode()`), so both pairing routes check `linkCodeTaken()` before inserting. A new kind of device code must use the same alphabet, the same check and `findLinkCode()`, or a typed code can find the wrong device. The device keeps the secret; only its hash is stored, and linking copies that hash onto the device row (never a secret made at link time, which the device couldn't learn).
- **`PATCH /api/pos/devices/:id` replaces the whole `settings`** — a page that edits a few settings (the installer's device page) must send the device's other settings with its changes (`{ ...device.settings, ...changes }`), which is why `GET /api/installer/venues/:id` returns each device's whole settings. Sending only the fields shown silently clears the rest (revenue stream, receipt printer, kitchen screen view).
- **Per-address rate limits count every device at a venue together** — tills, kitchen screens and kiosks share the venue's internet address, so a route a device polls (link codes every 4 s) needs a limit that fits a dozen devices at once (`GET /pos-api/pairings/:id` 240 a minute).
- **A new id-holding field in a kiosk design must be added to `ownDesign()` in `services/kioskSvc.js`** — `normaliseDesign()` only checks an id's shape, so without it another tenant's section, dish or order type id is stored (the tenant sweep only catches it once a fixture writes one). Kiosk designs stored such ids unchecked until migration 185's change.
- **The split photo's pictures come only from `splitImages()` in `shared/kiosk.js`** — the dish page, the combo builder and the order line all call it with the builder's selection shape; picking pictures by hand in a component would drift (another order, an option twice, a group without the switch). A new boolean on a combo step, like `split`, must be stored only when true (`cleanCombo()`, `comboPayload()`), or every menu reads as having changes to publish (the same reason as `GROUP_FLAGS` in `shared/menuDiff.js`). (Corrected by migration 201: `splitImages()` is now `pickImages()`, returning `{ split, corner }` from each group's `kiosk_pick_photo` and each step's `pick_photo`; the same rules apply to it, and a step's `pick_photo` is stored only when set.)
- **The kiosk's hexagon grid is laid out in JS (`HexGrid` measures its width), not CSS alone** — a honeycomb needs every other row moved by half a cell and rows overlapping by a quarter of a cell's height, which depends on the cell width. Anything that changes a hexagon cell's padding, ring or aspect must keep w / h = 0.866 and the -h/4 bottom margin, or rows stop locking together (the browser test measures it). With hexagons, `.k-card` gets hexagon ends by `clip-path`: content within about 0.9em of a card's left or right edge near its top or bottom corner is cut, so cards keep at least that much side padding.
- **A kiosk screen's way back is `BackButton` (`pos/src/kiosk/parts.jsx`), first in the screen's bottom bar, never a link at the top** — on a tall kiosk the top is out of reach (and reach mode moves only the frame down), and guests look at the bottom for what to do next. A new kiosk screen that can be left must put `BackButton` in its bottom bar (a `.k-bar`, or the builder's light bar: `.k-btn-back` picks its colours from the bar), and a top bar may hold only a title or a running price.
- **Kiosk pictures follow two styles: dishes (`.k-tiles-<style>`, `layout.tiles`) and choices (`.k-opts-<style>`, `layout.option_tiles`)** — a new element that shows a dish must use the dish classes (`.k-item`, `.k-item-img`, `.k-thumb`, `.k-hero-img`) and one that shows a choice the choice classes (`.k-opt`, `.k-opt-img`, `.k-opt-thumb`), with its kiosk.css rules keyed on the matching root class, or it takes the other kind's shape (a choice thumb with `.k-thumb` turned round with round dishes). A display gets the choices' style as its `tiles` prop; never pass `layout.tiles` to a display. Honeycomb positions come only from `hexLayout()` in `shared/kiosk.js`: adjacent rows must start on opposite half-columns (they overlap by a quarter of a hexagon), so a row may move only by whole hexagons.
- **A new per-venue setup table must be added to `services/storeSetup.js`, or New location silently leaves it out** — `createStore()` copies only the tables its `AREAS` functions name. A table whose rows other rows point at by id (a station, a section, a printer) must also be in `OWNED_TABLES`: `copyTable()` drops any id the template venue owns that wasn't copied, and a table missing from that list keeps its id, so the new location's copy points back at the template's row (the store-opening test scans for exactly that). Copy children through `copyTable()` with `parentCol` / `parents` after their parent, in `AREA_ORDER`, never with your own INSERT ... SELECT, which skips the id remapping.
- **What a new location gets is decided by `normalisePlan()` in `shared/storeOpening.js`, never by the AI's answer directly** — every step merges the answer with the person's own overrides through it, and `createStore()` reads only its output. Don't read `opening.ai` in the create path or the page, or a field the person changed by hand reverts to Pepper's guess. A new area needs an entry in `OPENING_AREAS` (its modules decide who may set it up), its plan fields in `normalisePlan()`, a function in `AREAS`, a link in `AREA_LINKS` and the answer schema in `storeOpeningAi.js` (all properties required, empty values for unset: structured outputs refuse optional fields).
- **Store-opening routes live under `/api/ai/` for the 5-minute proxy timeout** — a Pepper step can take over a minute; mounted anywhere else, nginx answers 504 at 60 s while the step carries on and its answer is lost. A new long AI route must be under `/api/ai/` too.
- **A kiosk share link is found by its token alone, in system mode** — `GET /pos-api/kiosk-share` must keep checking the token's shape first, refusing a past end date (410) and loading the design's own tenant from the row, and must never return anything a staff preview link doesn't (no tenant ids beyond the config, no who-made-it). New link must replace `share_token` (not add a second), so the old link stops.
- **How a kiosk choice looks comes from `choiceLook(design)` (`pos/src/kiosk/displays.jsx`)** — `tiles`, `pictures` and `center` together. A new choice display must take all three (and a new screen showing choices must spread `choiceLook()` into it): one that reads only `option_tiles` shows photos on a words-only design, and one that renders a picture without checking `pictures` brings empty picture frames back. The split photo (`splitImages()`) deliberately ignores `option_pictures`.
- **The kiosk's online state is decided only by `linkOk()` / `linkTrouble()` in `kiosk/store.js`** — never set `online` directly from a request's result (a single failed request must get the two quick tries before ordering pauses, and `linkOk()` is what reconnects the socket, sends the outbox and saves pictures when the link comes back). A new request path must call `handleError()` on failure (it routes no-reply errors to `linkTrouble()`) and `linkOk()` on success. Playwright's `context.setOffline()` doesn't cut the service worker's own requests: test an outage by stopping the server the kiosk talks to.
- **A picture the kiosk must keep offline has to be requested as a picture** — the service worker's picture rule matches `request.destination === 'image'`, so `photos.js` loads with `new Image()`; a `fetch()` (destination empty) goes past the cache. A new picture field in the kiosk config is found by `configImages()` only when its key is `image_url` or `logo_url` (or a start media `url`): name new picture fields that way.
- **`Btn` in `components/pos/shared.jsx` passes extra props to the button** — it used to drop them, so `data-testid` and `aria-*` on a `Btn` silently never reached the page. Don't destructure only the known props in a shared button again.
- **Only previews may load the draft menu: `kioskConfig()` uses `menuVersion: 'draft'` only together with `preview: true`** — a device route (`/pos-api/config`) must never pass a menu version, and a new preview kind must carry its choice in its signed link or stored row (never a query parameter beside the token, which anyone holding a published preview link could flip). Anything a preview shows from the menu must come through `tillMenus(..., version)` so the editor's switch reaches it; option groups are tenant-wide and live in both versions, which is why a flavour picture shows in the preview before publishing and a dish photo doesn't.
- **A till, kitchen screen or kiosk is added only by linking the code it shows (`POST /api/pos/venues/:id/link`)** — never add a route that creates a `pos_devices` row with a code for someone to type into the device, or Till setup and the installer go back to two opposite flows (the reason they were removed). `POST /pos-api/pair` and `openPairing()` exist only for the move hand-over, whose code the app at the new address types itself; don't show those codes to people. Tests add devices with `linkDevice()` in `api/test/support/devices.mjs`.
- **What an order shows on an order ready board tile or a kitchen ticket title comes only from `orderInfo()` / `orderValue()` in `shared/orderInfo.js`** — the tile, the kitchen screen's `ticketTitle()` and the bench all call it, so a new place that names an order (a printed ticket header, a Pepper answer, a report) must too, or it disagrees about which name counts (online guest first, then the till or kiosk name, never a table's tab) and what happens when the chosen field is missing (fall back to number, tent, table, name). A new field goes into `ORDER_FIELDS` and `orderValue()`, and into `boardNumbers()`'s view in `shared/pos/kitchen.js` if boards should show it.
- **In a migration, bracket a jsonb `->` before `-`, `||` or `?`** — `->` is a generic operator with lower precedence than `-`, so `b->'opts' - 'show_names'` parses as `b -> ('opts' - 'show_names')` and fails with "operator is not unique: unknown - unknown". Write `(b->'opts') - 'show_names'` and `(b->'opts') ? 'show_names'`.
- **A printed ticket is drawn only by `kitchenTicket()` / `receipt()` in `shared/pos/tickets.js` from a layout (`shared/pos/ticketLayout.js`)** — the till's browser print, the hub's ESC/POS, the sequencer's print jobs and the designer's preview all go through them, so a new place that prints (a reprint, a report slip) must pass the venue's layout (`cfg.layouts` / config `print_layouts`) or it ignores the design. A new block type needs `BLOCK_TYPES` (options and defaults), `cleanOpts()`, its drawing in `tickets.js`, `blockSummary()` and `BlockOptions` in `PrintDesigner.jsx`; one with words a venue can change also needs `TEXT_KEYS`. Keep `defaultLayout()` drawing exactly the old tickets (the shared test compares them).
- **A venue's print design is resolved in one place: `printLayouts()` in `services/printDesignSvc.js`** (venue choice, else company; kiosk receipts else till receipts; the venue's variation applied). A new device config or print path must take its layouts from it, never read `print_design_uses` itself, or kiosk receipts and venue words silently stop applying there.
- **Menu variant display settings have a fifth reader: the kiosk** — this corrects the "four readers" entry above. Kiosk dishes carry `variant_rules` from `tillMenuData(..., { guest: true })` (`services/posSvc.js`) and `posChoices()` (`shared/pos/pricing.js`) passes them to `itemChoices()`; tills get no rules on purpose (staff see every option). A new guest-facing surface built on the till config must load with `guest: true` or it shows £0 options the menu hides. Shipped after the user's report (19 Oct 2026) that the kiosk showed zero-priced options.
- **Kitchen tickets come only from printer boxes in the kitchen flow (`kitchenPrintJobs()`), never from `jobsForEvent()`** (migration 191 dropped `pos_print_routes` and `routeFor()`). A new way dishes reach the kitchen (another server event, the hub) gets its tickets for free through the sequencer's kitchen branch; one that writes `kds_orders` itself, or marks `item.printed` itself, prints nothing or prints twice. A dish prints once per printer box: keep `item.printed` / `hold_printed` / `void_printed` on the item.
- **Every print job's printer must exist when the sequencer saves it** (`pos_print_jobs.printer_id` references `pos_printers`): a job for a deleted printer fails the event's whole transaction. Deleting a printer clears it from kitchen printer boxes and devices' receipt printer, and `loadKitchenConfig()` / `printConfig()` drop ids of printers that are gone or switched off. Anything else that stores a printer id must be cleared the same way.
- **A print job is collected by whoever prints its printer, and only the collector reports on it** (`POST /pos-api/print-jobs/claim`: the hub takes `connection = 'hub'` printers' jobs, a till or kitchen screen its own `device` printers'). A new device kind that prints must be added to the claim's kinds and its config must carry its printers (`tillConfig()` and `kdsConfig()` do); never let any device mark another's collected job printed.
- **A shared kitchen flow (migration 192) has no `kds_flow_edges` and no station ids: its `box_links` point at box ids, resolved per venue by `resolveSharedFlow()`** — anything that routes dishes, draws a venue's flow or lists a flow's stations must take a flow's links through `loadFlow(tx, tenantId, flow, stations, { venueId })` (or `kdsConfig()`), never read `box_links` or `kds_flow_edges` by flow id alone, or a shared flow routes nowhere (or to box ids no station has). Lists of a venue's flows must use `loadFlows()` (own then shared): a query `WHERE venue_id = X` misses the shared ones, and the flow in use may be one of them.
- **Anything that adds, reorders or deletes shared flows must call `pinActiveFlows()` first** — a venue with no flow in use written down uses "the first" of its list, and its list includes shared flows, so a new shared flow could silently change what a venue's kitchen does. Changes to a shared flow broadcast to every venue (`everyVenueChanged()`), not just the one whose page made them.
- **A new link filter that names something a location has its own of (like streams and tills) must be added to `NAMED_TARGET_KINDS`**, stored by name in shared flows (`cleanTargets()`), turned back into ids in `resolveSharedFlow()` and `shareFlow()`, ranked in `RANK` / `linkRank()`, and offered by name in `LinkPanel` for shared flows; an id of one venue's in a shared flow would match nothing at the others.
- **Money taken on a card machine goes on a check only through an approved `pos_card_payments` row** — never accept a `card_integrated` payment, refund or void on the device's word: the sequencer checks `cardNeed()` / `cardProblem()` (`shared/pos/cards.js`) against the stored attempt (venue, check, payment id, amount and tip, approved, used once). A new event or path that moves card machine money must add its case there; a store without `loadCardPayment()` (the hub) must keep refusing. Hand-recorded card payments stay `card_external` and can't name an attempt.
- **Call a card provider outside any database transaction, after the attempt row is written** — `cardSvc.js` inserts the attempt (with its id as the provider's idempotency key), commits, then calls the provider and writes the result in a new transaction. Calling inside a transaction holds row locks for seconds, and a rollback after the provider took the money would lose the only record of it. A provider error marked `retry` (network, 5xx) leaves the attempt pending for the next read; never mark it declined.
- **A device's `card_reader` is a `pos_card_readers` id of its own venue, or `'none'`** — `checkDeviceSettings()` refuses another venue's machine, and a new location's copied devices get `'none'` (`storeSetup.js`). Code that writes device settings another way (a migration, an import, the installer) must keep that, and must never write the old `'test'` value (migration 193 turned those into Test card machine rows).
- **The rota's print and image colours (`ENTRY_STATUSES` `solid` / `soft` in `rotaExport.js`) copy the grid's Tailwind classes (`STATUS_CLS` / `STATUS_SOFT` in `rota.jsx`) by hand** — a print can't use Tailwind classes (it renders in its own iframe, and the image on a canvas). Change a status colour in both places, or the printout stops looking like the grid. A new status needs a row in each.
- **The hub's code reaches mini PCs only through `hubRelease()`: a file the hub needs must be imported (statically or with `import('./...')`) from `hub/bin/*.js`** — `importClosure()` follows relative imports to decide what goes in the release; a file read any other way (`fs.readFile` of a sibling, a path built from strings) isn't shipped and the installed hub breaks after the next update while the repository copy works. Any change to `hub/` or the shared files it imports is a new version that every hub installs by itself at its next quiet moment, so run `node hub/bin/hub.js --self-test` and `node --test hub/test/*.test.mjs` before merging; a change that passes the self test but stops at start is rolled back on each hub and held back (Update now retries it).
- **A hub must never exit on a refusal it can recover from** — the launcher counts quick stops and goes back a version after three, so an unlinked hub (401) or an unreachable cloud must keep running (showing a new code, retrying), not `process.exit(1)`.
- **A USB printer on the hub is found by its id at every print, never by its device file** — `/dev/usb/lp0` and `lp1` swap when printers are plugged in another order; `local_id` (usb:vendor:product:serial or port, or win:name) is what Till setup stores. Status questions and tickets to one printer go through the same queue (`queued()` in `usb.js`), or a DLE EOT can land inside a ticket's command.
- **A theme goes into a designer only through its mapper in `shared/themeKit.js`** — the theme maker's previews (`KitPreview`) draw through the same `websiteThemeFromKit()` / `kioskBrandFromKit()` / `boardFromKit()` / `printFromKit()` that Use this applies, so a designer that copies kit colours into its settings by hand shows one thing in the preview and saves another, and skips the readability fixes (white button words on the website, 7:1 words on boards, inks on the paper). A mapper must keep every setting a kit doesn't cover (`...current`): the website's background picture, spacing and hero, a kiosk's logo and layout. A new mapper is checked against its designer's own normaliser in `shared/test/themeKit.test.mjs`, or a font or value the designer drops slips through.
- **`hub/install/setup.cmd` is run by cmd.exe, which forgives little** — it is served with `\r\n` line endings (`installScript('cmd')`) and must stay plain ASCII; nothing it calls may pass a path or argument by pasting `%~f0` / `%~1` into PowerShell code (a downloaded file named "macaroonie-hub-setup (1).cmd", or an apostrophe in a user name, breaks it): hand them over in environment variables, as it does. Don't add `rem` lines or unquoted `(` `)` inside an `if (...)` block. Whether the install worked reaches it only through `$global:MacaroonieHubFailed`, set by `install.ps1`'s last line; a change there must keep setting it. `install.ps1` must stay plain ASCII too: the setup file runs it from a downloaded file, and Windows PowerShell 5.1 reads a file without a byte-order mark as ANSI.
- **Saving the group's brand or theme writes `tenant_site` only, never a location's `website_config`** — both save routes used to strip `colors` / `typography` from every location's theme and set its `template_key` to NULL, a clean-up left over from before migration 098. The column is NOT NULL, so every Brand identity save (it always sends the template) failed with a 500 once any location had website settings, and theme saves emptied locations' own Brand & theme overrides. A location's brand fields only apply while `use_brand_override` is on, as a whole, so nothing needs clearing. A test whose tenant has no `website_config` row can't catch this kind of bug: give the tenant a location with website settings (`api/test/website-brand.test.mjs`).
- **The default main menu exists twice: `DEFAULT_NAV_TREE` in `config/defaultNav.js` and the JSON in `migrations/196_main_menu.sql` (since 202: 196's tree with 202's Store tech change applied)** — the migration ran once for every tenant then; new tenants and Reset to defaults use the JS. `api/test/nav-default.test.mjs` fails when they differ or when a `ROUTE_CATALOG` route is missing or doubled, so a new page needs a catalogue entry and a place in the tree (change the migration's JSON only to keep the test's comparison true; it never runs again on the server).
- **Brand identity fields are saved per part (`IDENTITY_PARTS` in `Website.jsx`)** — the Brand page edits brand_name, tagline, logo and favicon; Website > Look & layout the site name and social picture; a location's own look every field. `BrandIdentitySection` sends only its part's fields, so two pages never overwrite each other's fields with stale values. A new identity field must be added to a part, or no page saves it.
- **What the venue wizard set always wins over Pepper's plan** — `normalisePlan()` reads each area setting through `pick()` (`overrides[key]` before the AI's raw plan), so a describe step after the person filled in hours or tables can't change them. A new wizard setting needs its key in `SETTING_KEYS` (else the PATCH refuses it), its cleaner in `normalisePlan()`, and its use in `storeSetup.js`. Wizard drafts carry `overrides.wizard = true` and may be created without a ready plan; other openings still need one.
- **Menus made from files go through `createMenuFromSections()` only** — the import route and Pepper's create_menu share the plan (tag matching, option groups, the whole-tree save), so a menu read from files can't hold another tenant's tags or groups and never creates tags. Don't save the reading's answer straight into the menu tables, and keep `POST /read` free of writes (the person reviews first).
- **The ordered price on a purchase order line never changes after ordering; the invoice's price is `invoice_price` beside it** (migration 197) — receiving, splitting and the price list update don't touch `price` or the PO totals. Code that reports what was actually charged must read `invoice_price` (falling back to `price`), and code that reports what was ordered reads `price`. A line `added_on_receipt` has qty 0 (the qty CHECK allows 0 only for these): sum ordered quantities over `qty`, received over `received_qty`, and leave added lines out of anything the supplier sees (`documentData()`).
- **Invoice prices are stored for everyone but shown only to people who see purchasing prices** — `invoiceView()` strips the reading's money and `poDetail()` strips `invoice_price` without `purchasing_setup` view; the receive route takes a typed `invoice_price` only from price viewers and otherwise uses the stored reading's price by `invoice_idx`. A new place that shows a reading or a line must go through these, and a new body field that carries a price from the browser must check `showPrices()` the same way.
- **A model's refs, not ids** — invoice reading sends the order's lines and the supplier's products to the model as `L1..` / `C1..` and `toInvoice()` turns them back into ids from those same lists; an unknown ref is ignored. Never ask a model to echo uuids back, and never trust one it returns: a misread id could point at another tenant's row.
- **Pepper's structured-output readers share `askForJson()` (`services/aiJson.js`)** — it checks the monthly allowance and the person's daily share first and counts the usage after; a new reader (a document, a photo) must use it rather than a fourth copy of the Anthropic client, or it skips those checks. Its route goes under `/api/ai/` for the proxy timeout and checks Pepper is on (`pepperOn()` in `routes/documentRead.js`).
- **The HTML `hidden` attribute loses to any `display` class** — `<button hidden className="inline-flex ...">` shows anyway (Tailwind's `inline-flex` beats the browser's `[hidden] { display: none }`), so a button meant to be hidden for View users was visible (the dish panel's Copy, caught by the browser check). Render it conditionally (`{!readOnly && <button ...>}`); `hidden={readOnly}` only works on elements with no display class.
- **An option group copy copies columns generically, but ids inside them aren't remapped** — `POST /api/menus/variant-groups/:id/copy` inserts every `menu_variant_groups` / `menu_variant_options` column but the id and timestamps, so a new plain column comes along without a code change; a new column holding the id of the group itself or of its options (like `child_group_id` asking the group itself) must be remapped in that route, and a new `menu_translations` key kind for groups or options added to its key list.
- **Test fixtures must not book or hold at a fixed date that will pass** — `fillTenant()` held a table at `2026-10-22T19:00Z`; on that day the booking cutoff refused it and every API test file failed in its `before` hook (the fixtures assert nothing failed). A time that has to be in the future is worked out from now (`holdTime()` in `api/test/support/fixtures.mjs`); fixed past dates (the rota week, Cash Recon days) are fine. Other fixed future dates in the tests (an override and exceptions in Dec 2026 / Jan 2027, an off period from 2026-11-02, store openings on 2026-12-01 / 05) still work only while the routes accept past dates for them.
- **What guests see as an option group's name is its display name, and only two places supply it** — a published menu holds it as the attached group's `name` (`shownGroup()` in `releaseContent()`), and the live group library is selected as `COALESCE(display_name, name) AS name` (`tillConfig()`, `kioskConfig()`, the translation sources). A new guest-facing reader must load menus through `loadMenuVersion(s)` (drafts for guests get `withShownGroupNames()`) or select the library the same way; reading `menu_variant_groups.name` or `loadMenuFull()` for guests shows staff names like Flavour x2 (copy). Staff pages (the Menus editor, Variant groups) keep the name with `display_name` beside it: `GET /api/menus/:id` reads `loadMenuFull()` straight for that reason.
- **A constant used inside `normaliseDesign()` must be defined above it in `shared/kiosk.js`** — the module calls `normaliseDesign()` while it loads (a default design), so a `const` list declared further down (`PICK_PHOTOS` was, at first) is still in its temporal dead zone and every importer of `shared/kiosk.js` (kiosk, admin, API, tests) fails with "Cannot access ... before initialization".
- **A choice has two pictures: `image_url` for its own tile, `photo_url` for the dish's photo (migration 199)** — anything that draws a choice (a display, the bundle's step tabs) uses `image_url`; anything that puts picks on the dish's photo goes through `splitImages()`, which takes `photo_url` first. Don't read either directly for the other job, or a cut-out tile picture lands on the dish's photo (or the dish shot on a small tile). A new picture field the kiosk must keep offline must be named `image_url`, `logo_url` or `photo_url`, the keys `configImages()` walks.
- **A Sleeper makes kitchen timing depend on the clock, not only on events (migration 200)** — a dish behind a Sleeper reaches the next station when `at` passes its wake time, with no event. Anything that decides where a dish is must call `flowTimes()` / `nodeState()` with the real time (the screen's `now`, the event's time), never without one (no `at` = the Sleeper never wakes), and never read `item.done` alone. Code that waits for an event to act on a dish arriving (printing, a status change) only sees a wake when something runs: kitchen printers get it through the minute sweep; order status doesn't change on a wake at all. "Balance box" in older entries here is the Splitter (kind `balance`).
- **A button that can be disabled for a reason the guest or staff can't see must say the reason** — the kiosk's Add to order was disabled whenever `priceItem()` failed, but only missing picks had a message, so a dish with no price was a silent dead end (reported from a live kiosk). Any new `disabled={...}` on a main action must show why next to it; and a guest surface that lists dishes must leave out ones that can't be priced (`isPriced()`), as online ordering and the kiosk do.
- **A base option's price is read only through `basePrice()` (`shared/orderPricing.js`)** — `o.price_pence ?? item.price_pence` treats £0 as a price, so a dish-price group whose options were left at £0 (flavours, sauces) made the dish free on the kiosk, till and online. A new place that prices or shows a base option must call `basePrice(o) ?? item.price_pence`.
- **A Store tech page's address is its `TABS` key (`/pos/<key>`), and the main menu links to it** — renaming or removing a key breaks every tenant's saved menu link (nav_items hold the route) and the `ROUTE_CATALOG` entry; add a new part to `TABS`, `ROUTE_CATALOG` and `DEFAULT_NAV_TREE` together, plus a migration that inserts the link into existing menus (as 202 does), or no existing tenant can reach it. Link to a part as `/pos/<key>`, never `/pos?tab=` (kept only as a redirect for old saved links). A page opened from a Store tech page (a designer) belongs under its address (`/pos/kiosks/designs/:id`) so the menu link stays lit.
- **A kiosk picture of a dish, choice, section or pick must be drawn with `Pic` (`pos/src/kiosk/parts.jsx`), never a bare `<img>`** — `Pic` is what notices a see-through picture and adds `.k-clear`, so a bare `<img>` (or a CSS rule that puts a background, ring or clip on a picture without a `:not(.k-clear)` way out) brings the box back behind cut-out PNGs. Banners, the logo and start screen media stay plain `<img>` on purpose.
- **A colour can be the word `transparent` (See-through, `clear` on `ColourPicker`)** — only on background fields: board backgrounds and boxes in `shared/screenLayout.js` (`colour()` passes it, `withAlpha()` keeps it) and the kiosk's `CLEAR_COLORS`. Code that reads one of those colours as a hex (contrast maths, `inkOn()`, appending alpha digits, `parseInt(hex.slice(1), 16)`) must handle it first (see `solidColours()` in `KioskDesigner.jsx`, `cleanHex()` in `shared/themeKit.js`); don't offer `clear` on a field whose colour is also used for text.
- **An order board tile is `.sl-onum` > (`img.sl-oicon`) + `.sl-otext` > caption / value / lines** — the look comes only from the custom properties `tileLook()` puts on `.sl-orders` (`shared/screenLayout.js`); a new tile option needs a property there and a rule in `SCREEN_LAYOUT_CSS`, never inline styles per tile, and any picture it adds must go through `tileImageUrls()` or screens show it late and lose it offline. Tests that match tile HTML must expect the `.sl-otext` wrapper.
- **An order's status is worked out, never stored: `orderStatus()` (`shared/pos/kitchen.js`) from where its dishes are in the kitchen flow** — anything that shows or reports statuses must call it (or read `boardNumbers()`'s `status`, which needs `cfg`: called without it, entries have no status). Don't write a status column or event for it, or it stops following the flow (a remake, a recall or a flow switch would leave it stale). A new kind of pass-through box must be added to `noBump()` and kept off kitchen screens the same way as the Setter.
- **Kiosk choice entrances are CSS keyed on the display's structure (`[data-display=...] > *`, `.k-hexgrid > .k-hex-cell`, `.k-slider > .k-slide > :first-child`)** — a new choice display needs `data-display` and one child per choice (or its own rule in `kiosk.css`), or its choices just appear. Animate a child of anything that already has a `transform` of its own (like `.k-slide`), and keep `animation-fill-mode: backwards`: `both` / `forwards` would hold the end frame over the element's own transform for good.
- **What suits a kiosk guest's allergens and diets is decided only by `guestFit()` / `countFits()` in `shared/kiosk.js`** — the allergens screen's count, the menu's marks, the dish page and meal deal choices all go through it (`fitOf()` / `countFor()` in `store.js`), so a new place that shows or filters dishes for the guest's picks must call it rather than read `item.dietary` itself, or it will disagree about may contain (doesn't suit), can be removed (suits, with a note) and meal deals (judged by their required steps' choices, not the union of every dish). The picks live only in the kiosk's state: never send them to the server or keep them past `restart()`.
- **A combo page's big picture is the dish page's `Hero` with a per-page `subject` (`ComboBuilder` in `pos/src/kiosk/Builder.jsx`), never a hand-built block** — the side column used to draw its own photo + name + price block, so a picked dish never showed big, the deal's description ignored `show_descriptions`, and an upright kiosk had no picture on a deal's pages at all; drawn through `Hero`, anything the dish page's top gains (a badge, a line, a layout) reaches the deal's pages for free. A new page kind in `comboPages()` must say what its subject is (the deal, unless the page is about one picked dish), and the running price may show in one place only: the TopBar, else the deal hero's `k-combo-price`, else `k-combo-head` above another subject's hero.
- **A Zod `.default()` on a field the code falls back for with `??` makes the fallback dead code** — the menu tree schemas had `sort_order: z.number().int().default(0)`, so `upsertMenuTree()`'s `item.sort_order ?? ii` never numbered anything by position and every dish sent without one tied at 0. Use `.optional()` where the handler has its own fallback. And never let a test (or a screen) read "the first" of rows whose order the query leaves open: `json_agg(DISTINCT jsonb_build_object(...))` orders by the jsonb, which compares the `id` key first, so ties come back in random-id order and a test that reads `items[0]` passes or fails by chance (the CI failures of 23-24 Oct 2026). Pick test data by id or name, and give every ORDER BY a unique last key.
- **A flow link's filter has two meanings: `exclude` false = only the dishes it names, true = every dish but those (migration 204)** — anything that reads, copies or rewrites links must carry `exclude` with `targets` (`loadFlowLinks()`, `loadFlow()`, the copies in `POST .../kds-flows`, `shareFlow()` / `resolveSharedFlow()`, a store opening's `copyTable()`), and anything that drops entries must not delete an Except link left with none (it took every other dish, so it becomes a link for every dish, as `dropFlowTarget()` does). Decide whether a link matches only through `linkRank()` / `isExcept()`: code that reads `targets` alone routes Except links backwards.
- **`pickImages().corner` holds `{ src, scale }` objects, not picture URLs (migration 205)** — draw corner pictures only through `CornerPicks` (widths from `cornerWidths()`), never by mapping `corner` to `<img src>`. And a flex row of pictures of different widths needs `align-items` other than `stretch` (`.k-corner-picks` uses `flex-end`): under the default stretch a smaller picture takes the row's height and its `aspect-ratio` is ignored, so it turns into a tall strip.
- **A Splitter picks by `(load + pieces added) x pace` in one place, `next()` inside `balanceTicket()`: a new way of sharing must change the pace or the allowed stations, never add a second picking path** — the turns (ties to the first after the last pick), big tickets and the busy order type rule all go through it, so a rule that picks a station by itself skips the tie turns and `cfg.balance.picks`. Anything that measures a station's pace must go through `stationPace()` (one bump counts from the later of when it reached the station and the bump before it), and the data must be orders the station bumped lately of any status: open orders alone miss tickets already collected, which is why the sequencer loads `recent` for a Smart box. A store without `loadRecentKitchenOrders()` (the hub's) falls back to the open orders and so to a pace from much less data.
- **A choice's picture style comes from the one `.k-opts-<style>` container above it, never the kiosk root (migration 206)** — option groups can look different on one page, and `.k-opts-X .k-opt` descendant rules from two containers would both apply (the later rule in kiosk.css wins, not the nearer container). So every set of choices carries its own class (`OptionGroup`'s `.k-choices` wrapper, a combo step's page, the step tab's thumbs) and sets never nest: a group an option opens renders after its parent's wrapper, not inside it. A new place that draws choices (`.k-opt`, `.k-opt-thumb`, `.k-opt-face`) must sit in such a container with the look from `groupLook()` / `choiceLook()`, and pictures outside one (corner pictures) carry `data-tiles` themselves. Entrances are the other way round: `data-enter` sets custom properties, which inherit, so the nearest one wins by itself.
- **A meal deal's picture is put together only by `bundleParts()` / `bundleLayout()` (`shared/kiosk.js`) and drawn by `BundlePhoto`** — a new place that shows a meal deal as picked (a receipt preview, the order number screen) must draw `BundlePhoto` from them, never place pictures by hand. The pop needs the `seen` ref kept by whatever owns the pages (`ComboBuilder`): a `BundlePhoto` holding its own would pop every part again whenever the picture unmounts (a picked dish's options page shows that dish instead). And a sticky block in a scrolling column must sit above everything in the column with a z-index of its own (the step tabs' pictures use 1-3), or those draw over it as they scroll under it.
- **A combo part's options come only from `dealItem()` / `partChoices()` (till, kiosk) or `partOptions()` (online), never from `part.pos_choices`, `posChoices(part)` or `choicesOf(part)` directly** — a dish or section choice in a combo step can change what its dish asks inside the deal (`option.groups`: left out, only some options, another group, how many), and the pricing applies those changes (`priceCombo()` hands the choice's groups to `partPrice()`). A new place that shows, picks or prices a combo part (a receipt preview, a Pepper tool, a new display) must find the step choice the pick came from (`sameChoice()` / matching `option_id` + `item_id`) and go through those helpers, or it offers options the pricing then refuses (BBQ in a deal that offers three flavours) or misses a swapped group entirely. Code that matches separate dishes to a deal must check `dealFits()`, and anything that turns a dish's picks into a deal's must trim them with `picksWithin()`.
- **A kiosk add that should fly needs a picture and a landing place: `addToBasket(entry, key, { flew: flyToBasket(picture) })`, and the button the order total is on must carry `data-fly-target="basket"`** — `flyToBasket()` (`pos/src/kiosk/fly.js`) quietly returns false without a visible target, and `addToBasket()` then bounces the order button at once as before, so a new add path or a new order button without these looks fine and simply never flies. Pass `flew` only from `flyToBasket()`'s result: true tells `addToBasket()` to leave the bounce to the landing, so a hard-coded true leaves the button still. Updates of an entry (a `key`) never fly.
- **A combo part's options must be asked with `askedChoices()` and priced or shown with the deal's own picks filled in (`withDealPicks()` / `fillDealPicks()`)** — `dealChoices()` keeps a group the deal leaves one option in, marked `fixed`, so code that renders `part.pos_choices` / `partOptions()` as they are asks the guest a question with one answer, and code that reads only the guest's picks (a part page's "complete" check, a picture, a summary) misses the deal's pick. Pricing fills it itself (the part callbacks of `priceItem()` / `priceLine()`), so never strip `fixed` groups before pricing, and never compute a part's options or picks outside these helpers. A new rule for when a group is fixed goes in `fixedPicks()` only.
- **A whole-tree save must send back data the page doesn't edit, never an empty list for it** — the Menus page "retired" a dish's own options by sending `variants: []` on every save, but other writers (the sample menu seeds, imports, the API, Pepper's `menuSaveBody()`) still made them, and the PATCH rewrites the whole tree, so every save deleted them and, through `cleanDealGroups()`, the meal deal rules that referred to them. Before declaring a field retired, grep every writer; while any remains, the page must pass it through (and show it, or nobody knows it is there).
- **A `truncate flex-1` label in a row of `shrink-0` badges and buttons goes to zero width when the row is narrow** — it shrinks first and never wraps, so a phone (or a tablet with a side panel open) showed rows of prices and buttons with no names. Give the label a `min-w-[…]`, put the rest in one group, and `flex-wrap` the row: the group then goes onto a second line before the label gets narrower than its minimum (`ItemRow` and the section heading in `Menus.jsx`). Test at 390 px with a long name, not only short ones (the mutation check passed with short names alone).
- **Don't lay an absolutely positioned notice over a device app's screen** — the kiosk preview's corner pill covered the top bar's running price and language button. A notice either takes its own room (an in-flow band that the screen starts below, as `.k-preview-band`) or is said by the page around the frame (the design editor's toolbar, the share page's footer).
- **Lines go onto a purchase order only through `PUT /pos/:id/lines` (`add`), which tops up a line already holding the same quote** — the supplier reads two lines of one product as two orders, so a new way of adding to a PO (an import, a route, a Pepper tool) must call it rather than insert `purchase_order_lines` itself. And `GET /purchasing/venues/:id/catalog?supplier_id=` narrows the items, not their prices: each item still carries every supplier's active quotes, so a caller offering one supplier's products must filter `quotes` by `supplier_id` too (`AddPoItemsModal` and Pepper's `poChangePlan()` do).
- **A kiosk bottom bar takes its colours from `--k-bar` / `--k-bar-text`, the side menu from `--k-rail` / `--k-rail-text`, never `--k-text` / `--k-bg` / `--k-surface`** — those are the design's own Bottom bar and Side menu colours, worked out by `partColours()` (`shared/kiosk.js`).
