A new bottom bar must be a `.k-bar`, and anything on it that inverts the bar (a solid button, a tint) must use the bar's pair, as `.k-bar .k-btn-back` does; a hard-coded Text / Page pair looks right on the default design and goes unreadable on a design with its own bar colour. A design colour that follows another until set must stay null in `normaliseDesign()` and be resolved in one function like `partColours()`: filling it with a default there stores that default on the next Save, and it stops following.
- **Kitchen screen colours come only from `ticketColours()` / `sectionColour()` (`shared/pos/kitchenColours.js`)** — the screen, the test bench and the admin preview all draw from them, so a new state the kitchen screen shows needs a key in `KITCHEN_COLOURS` with its standard look, and must be drawn with inline styles from it, never a fixed Tailwind colour class (the operator's colour wouldn't reach it). Words on a row take the row's `ink` (a set colour gets black or white from `inkOn()`): a fixed light text class on a row reads on the standard dark green and vanishes on a light colour someone picked.
- **At the pass a tick (`item.checked`) isn't done (`item.done`)** — `kitchen.item_checked` never moves a dish; only a bump (READY, or the last tick with `bump_when_checked`) does. Code that asks whether the pass has finished a dish must read `done`, and a new tap path at an expo or runner must send `item_checked` (prep stations `item_done`). A new way a dish goes back upstream (another undo) must clear the ticks after it with `untickAfter()`, or a stale tick counts towards the last-tick bump and finishes a dish a cook took back.
- **A design picture (banner, featured tile, start screen or reach media) must be drawn with `FitPicture` and its item's `fit`, never a bare `<img>` with object-cover** — the fit is the operator's choice of how the picture fills its space, and only `fitLayout()` turns it into styles (an inline object-fit that beats `img.k-clear`'s contain, so Fill can crop a see-through picture). Automatic depends on `useClear()` knowing the picture has a see-through background: a new picture field for designs needs a `fit: pictureFit(x?.fit)` in `normaliseDesign()` (or saving drops it) and must be drawn through `FitPicture` inside a positioned box that sets its size. A box sized by `aspect-ratio` with a `max-height` (the banner) only keeps its ratio while the cap doesn't bite, so don't promise a shape on wide screens.
- **The kiosk on a small screen is zoomed out only through the viewport tag (`useKioskViewport()`, `pos/src/kiosk/viewport.js`), never with a CSS transform or zoom** — kiosk.css sizes from vmin, banners cap at 36vh, the honeycomb measures `innerWidth`, and the root is `position: fixed`: under a transform those all keep the real phone's sizes while the drawing shrinks, so nothing lines up. The tag must set initial, minimum and maximum scale together (Chromium then clamps the page to them even when the tag changes after load; `width=800` alone stays at scale 1 when the phone is turned). Take the scale from `window.screen` (the same both ways round), not `innerWidth` / `innerHeight`, which the zoom itself changes (the effect would undo itself on the next resize). Don't put the device app's `initial-scale=1.0, maximum-scale=1` back for the kiosk, and don't apply the zoom outside the kiosk app or in a frame (the share page scales its kiosk itself). Check it in a mobile browser context (Playwright `isMobile`, a phone's `screen`): a desktop browser ignores the tag, so the cramped bottom bar never shows there.
- **The kiosk design editor stays mounted when it moves to another design (Save as navigates from `/pos/kiosks/designs/<old>` to `<new>`), so its per-design state must follow the row id, not the mount** — the load effect resets the draft, the saved copy and the dirty check when `loadedFor.current !== row.id`, and the preview's query key has the id. New state that belongs to one design (a confirm, an error, something fetched for it) must reset on the id change too (as `savedAs()` resets the save mutation and the leave confirm), or the new design opens showing the old one's. A design must be in the `['kiosk-designs']` data before navigating to it: the page renders "This kiosk design doesn't exist any more" while `row` is missing, so put the new row in with `setQueryData()` first rather than waiting for the refetch.
- **Kiosk state that changes what a guest sees must be in the store or in `useJourneyState()` (`pos/src/kiosk/journey.js`), never a plain `useState`** — a visit is recorded as the store's view plus each screen's `useJourneyState()` values, and played back from them; a sheet, page or pick kept in `useState` is missing from every recording and the playback shows the screen without it. The key must be unique per mounted thing (option groups key by item, choice and depth). A timer that moves the kiosk on by itself (a countdown, the idle warning) must stop when `s.replay` is set, or the playback runs off on its own. Every `restart()` call must say why (`done`, `guest`, `timeout`, `preview`): the visit's outcome comes from it.
- **Nothing a guest types may reach a journey** — the view keeps the name and tent number as lengths only, and a touch inside `[data-jprivate]` (the kiosk's keyboard and number pad) is recorded at its middle with el `keyboard`. A new text field on the kiosk must use those keyboards or carry `data-jprivate` itself, and a new view key must never hold typed words (a search box's text, a note). A playback shows typed values as dots (`replayView()`).

- **A kiosk size in CSS or JS comes from the kiosk's box, never the window** — the kiosk can sit in a box with bars around it (`useKioskBox()`, `kioskBox()` in `shared/kiosk.js`), so kiosk.css and the kiosk's Tailwind classes use `cqmin` / `cqh` / `cqw` (`.k-root` is the size container), and JS that sizes from the screen uses `boxShort()` (`pos/src/kiosk/viewport.js`). A new `vmin`, `vh`, `vw`, `innerWidth` or `innerHeight` in a kiosk size is wrong whenever the screen isn't the design's shape (too big beside bars). The root's own font size is the one exception: container units on the container read a container outside it, so it uses `--k-unit`. This corrects the earlier "zoom only through the viewport tag" entry's list of what must match the page: the zoom still only goes through the tag, and sizes follow the box.
- **`container-type: size` doesn't make an element the containing block for `position: fixed` descendants in Chrome** (layout containment was dropped from container-type) — `.k-root`'s fixed children (the flying picture, `fly.js`) are still placed in window coordinates, even when the root sits off the window's corner with bars; absolute children (sheets, `.k-overlay`) are placed in the root. Don't subtract the root's offset from a fixed element's position: the browser test caught a flight that started 180 px off when that was tried.

- **What the kiosk's order done screen shows comes only from `doneInfo()` (`shared/kiosk.js`), never from `result.order_no` read directly** — a design can make the tent, the name or the order number the big value per order type, and an order without the chosen value falls back to the next one. A new place that shows the finished order (a receipt preview, the staff panel, a replay label) must call it with the same order (`number: order_no ?? provisional`, the typed tent and name, the type's shown name) or it disagrees with the screen. A new kiosk prompt that the screen could show needs a key in `DONE_LINES` / `DONE_MAIN`, a word in every language and a case in `doneGaps()`.
- **A guest's email address on the kiosk is used to send and then forgotten** — `kioskReceipt()` stores only `emailHint()` and counts sends; the kiosk keeps the address in a plain `useState` inside `data-jprivate` (never in the store or `useJourneyState()`, which go into the visit's record), and records only the sheet's state and the address's length. Don't log it, put it in `email_log`, return it in a reply, or keep it for "send again": a second send asks for it again. (Corrected by migration 210: an order to pay at the counter keeps the address in `pos_receipts.email_to`, sealed, only until the till takes payment; it is cleared as soon as it is sent, the order is cancelled, a day passes or `MAX_TRIES` fail. Anything that reads `email_to` must `unseal()` it, never log or return it, and clear it the moment its job is done; a new reason to hold an address goes through the same column and its clean-up in `sendWaitingReceipts()`, never a new plain column.)
---

## Deployment — CI/CD via GitHub Actions

**Deployment is fully automated on push.** A GitHub Actions workflow (`.yml`) handles build and
deploy automatically when changes are pushed from the developer's laptop. **Never suggest running
`deploy.sh` manually** — it is not needed and should not be referenced at the end of responses.

- **To deploy:** `git push` from the local laptop. The Actions workflow does the rest.
- **Migrations run automatically** via `api/scripts/migrate.js`, invoked from both the GitHub
  Actions workflow and `deploy.sh`. The runner tracks applied migrations in a
  `schema_migrations` table and only applies new files in order. Each migration runs in a
  transaction — if it fails, the deploy aborts before the API restarts.
- **First-time baseline** — on a server that already had migrations applied manually via
  `psql`, SSH in once and run:
  ```bash
  cd /home/ubuntu/app/api && set -a; source .env; set +a
  node scripts/migrate.js --baseline-up-to 024
  ```
  This records 001–024 as applied without running them. Subsequent deploys then apply
  025, 026, and anything new.
- **Pull requests are checked by `.github/workflows/ci.yml`**: every migration on an empty Postgres 16 (no pg_cron), twice, then the API started against it, plus the shared unit tests. It doesn't deploy anything.
- **OS user is `ubuntu`** — app lives at `/home/ubuntu/app`.
- **deploy.sh** exists on the server as a fallback but is not part of the normal workflow.

---

## Running locally

```bash
# Prerequisites: Postgres 16, Redis 7, Node 22

# API
cd api
cp .env.example .env   # fill in values
psql $DATABASE_URL -f ../migrations/001_tenants_users.sql
# ... run 002-010 in order
psql $DATABASE_URL -f ../migrations/011_doors_close_time.sql
psql $DATABASE_URL -f ../migrations/012_reconfirmed_status.sql
psql $DATABASE_URL -f ../migrations/013_doors_close_per_sitting.sql
psql $DATABASE_URL -f ../migrations/014_schedule_exceptions.sql
psql $DATABASE_URL -f ../migrations/015_fix_get_available_slots.sql
psql $DATABASE_URL -f ../migrations/016_noslot_noshow_cancelled.sql
psql $DATABASE_URL -f ../migrations/017_seated_checked_out.sql
psql $DATABASE_URL -f ../migrations/018_customers.sql
psql $DATABASE_URL -f ../migrations/019_customer_visit_count.sql
psql $DATABASE_URL -f ../migrations/020_slot_start_filter.sql
psql $DATABASE_URL -f ../migrations/021_enable_arrived_status.sql
psql $DATABASE_URL -f ../migrations/022_slot_inclusive_last_order.sql
psql $DATABASE_URL -f ../migrations/023_sitting_names.sql
npm install
npm run dev            # starts on :3000 with --watch

# Admin portal (separate terminal)
cd admin
cp .env.example .env   # fill in VITE_ values
npm install
npm run dev            # starts on :5173, proxies /api to :3000
```

Visit `http://localhost:5173`. Auth0 login redirects to the admin portal.
Use `/widget-test` to test the full booking flow without deploying anything.

---

## Doc maintenance

- **Update CLAUDE.md as part of EOS, every session.** If you skip a session, the doc rots.
- **"Common mistakes to avoid" is append-only.** Never delete an entry — only correct one if it turns out to be wrong. This section beats every other section in long-term value.
- **Keep the TOC synced.** When adding or renaming a section, update the Table of Contents.
- **Don't let the doc grow past ~3,000 lines.** Once a module section exceeds ~200 lines, move it to `docs/<MODULE>.md` and link from CLAUDE.md.
- **Don't duplicate what's already in code** (table schemas exposed via API, route lists). Reference instead.
- **Don't include credentials, tokens, or secret values** — only their names and where they live.
- **Don't include time-sensitive status** ("in progress this sprint") — that's what Outstanding items is for.
- **Don't write planning / decision documents inline.** Decisions go in commit messages or PR descriptions.
- **Cross-link with docs/.** CLAUDE.md is the index; deep-dives live elsewhere.
- **Companion files worth setting up:**

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `MEMORY.md` (in `~/.claude/projects/<repo>/memory/`) | Persistent AI memory — short notes that survive across sessions |
| `docs/<MODULE>.md` | Deep dives that don't belong in CLAUDE.md |
| `CHANGE.md` | Living changelog — prepend on every deploy |
